[[sep-vplan-area-5]]
==== 5. Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

[[sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test]]
===== 5.1 `sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`target = "lsu_stub_all_live"`, `+skip_fuse_sense`)

*Source Module*: `sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Drives the watchdog reset input pin and proves it takes the SEP CPU-reset net low and releases it again, without disturbing the main SEP reset.

*Test Coverage*: `wdt_rst_ni_i` drops and releases the CPU-reset net only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the watchdog reset input; confirm the CPU reset is released and the main reset is high.
. Assert the watchdog reset input; after a few clocks confirm the CPU reset asserted.
. From the assert edge through the release settle, sample the main SEP reset on every clock and watch it for any value change; confirm it reads 1 throughout.
. Release the input again; confirm the CPU reset returns high.
. Assert the main reset; confirm the main SEP reset reads 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BASELINE
|Before any stimulus, both resets are released.
|CHK-ASSERT
|Asserting the watchdog reset input drives the CPU reset low.
|CHK-ISOLATION
|While the CPU reset is asserted, the main SEP reset stays high — and that observable is proven capable of reading 0, by asserting the main reset at the end of the test. Without that anchor a stuck-high read would have satisfied this row identically.
|CHK-RELEASE
|Releasing the SEP input `wdt_rst_ni_i` returns `sep_cpu_reset_n` high. This is the pin path only. Closed-loop bite stickiness is outside this DUT.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_nmi_sanity_test]]
===== 5.2 `sep_nmi_sanity_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_nmi_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Covers the non-maskable interrupt mechanism end to end: the vector register has the right default, accepts a new value, and refuses further writes once locked — and then a real watchdog bark delivers an actual non-maskable interrupt to that handler, which clears the condition and returns. The lock check is a negative test: the write after locking must be ignored and the vector must still hold its previous value.

*Test Coverage*: NMI vector read/write/lock plus a real watchdog bark to the handler.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Register the handler.
. Check the vector register default; write a new vector; read it back.
. Set the lock; attempt another write; confirm the vector is unchanged.
. Configure the watchdog with a small bark threshold and enable it; wait.
. On the interrupt, the handler clears the bark and disables the watchdog, then returns.
. Confirm the bark state is clear and the watchdog is disabled.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-VEC-DEFAULT
|The vector register holds its specified reset value.
|CHK-VEC-WRITE
|A new vector value is accepted and reads back.
|CHK-LOCK
|After locking, a further write is ignored and the vector keeps its value.
|CHK-WDT-NMI
|A real watchdog bark delivers the interrupt and the handler runs.
|CHK-WDT-CLEAR
|The bark status bit was observed *set* inside the handler — which is what attributes the NMI to the watchdog rather than to any other source vectoring to the same handler — and after the handler it reads clear with the watchdog disabled. The pre-clear sample matters: without it the bit is only ever seen as zero, and "cleared" cannot be distinguished from "never set".
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Sim scaling*: the watchdog bark threshold is reduced for simulation — the
watchdog clock runs roughly a thousand times slower than the core, so the
production threshold would dominate run time. The mechanism exercised is
identical: the counter still increments through the bark path and firmware still
reaches its wait before the interrupt fires.

'''''

[[sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test]]
===== 5.3 `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: Proves in one firmware boot the software reset register default and per-domain wiring, two bus-error interrupts, watchdog bark, pet and re-bark, and the bite reset request at the SEP output pin.

*Test Coverage*: Software reset register default and per-domain wiring, two bus-error interrupts, watchdog bark/pet/re-bark, and the bite request at the SEP output pin. Reset generation and return are owned by the integrating reset controller outside this DUT.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Check the software reset register default, which is `0x7E`: the Key Manager is held and OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, the TRNG and Adams Bridge come up released. JTAG-holds OTBN/AES/HMAC/KMAC across `rst_ni` release and fuse sense so they never raise crypto `edn_req` while the fabric is opening, then releases them. The TRNG is not parked, because it supplies entropy rather than requesting it. The firmware then probes each per-IP reset wire, including the shared TRNG bit: assert the bit, confirm the register goes to its reset value, release, confirm it is writable again.
. Perform a bad write and a bad read of the unmapped gap, with settling in between; expect two bus-error interrupts.
. Configure the watchdog with small bark and bite thresholds; enable.
. On the first bark the handler clears and disables. After one settle window of 40000 core cycles (`mcycle`, ten watchdog ticks at the bench clocks) for the disable to cross the register CDC, confirm the count reads the same non-zero value before and after one more window, pet returns it to zero, and it still reads zero after one more window.
. Re-enable; confirm a second bark, then let it run on to bite.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BADADDR
|Accesses to the unmapped gap raise exactly two bus-error interrupts.
|CHK-WDT-NMI
|The first bark delivers an interrupt and the handler runs.
|CHK-WDT-CLEAR
|The handler clears the bark state and disables the watchdog.
|CHK-WDT-PET
|Disabled, after one 40000-cycle `mcycle` settle window, the count reads the same non-zero value across one more window, petting returns it to zero, and it still reads zero after the same window. Each window spans ten watchdog ticks, more than one refresh of the bus-side count copy through the register CDC. The line logs each count and each measured window in core cycles.
|CHK-WDT-REBARK
|Re-enabling fires a second bark — the mechanism is re-armable, not one-shot.
|CHK-BITE
|The bite reset request reaches the real output pin.
|CHK-SWRST-DEFAULT
|The software reset register holds its specified default of `0x7E`.
|CHK-SWRST-WIRE
|Pulsing each per-IP bit, the shared TRNG bit and the Adams Bridge bit included, returns that domain's probe register to its reset value and leaves every probe outside that reset bit unchanged: seven probes for an OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC or Adams Bridge pulse and five for a TRNG pulse, since the entropy source, CSRNG and EDN share the TRNG bit. The firmware logs the count per pulse and the testbench requires those exact counts.
|CHK-SWRST-WRITABLE
|After each release the firmware writes the probe again and it must read back the written value, which differs from its reset value, so a domain left held in reset fails. A second pulse then returns the probe to its reset value. Probe addresses, write values and reset values come from the generated headers; the Adams Bridge interrupt-enable reset value is taken from `abr_reg.rdl` `global_intr_en_t`, which has no generated C field header.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Sim scaling and timing requirement*: watchdog thresholds are reduced for
simulation, as in the non-maskable-interrupt test. A settling delay is required
between the bad store and the bad load; without it the core's imprecise
store-error reporting coalesces the two bus-error interrupts into one, and the
entry requires exactly two.

'''''

[[sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test]]
===== 5.4 `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: SRAM strobe, pattern, sequential, and partial-write breadth on the 64-bit port.

*Test Coverage*: Every contiguous WSTRB mask, the 64-bit pattern set, sequential distinct words, the base plus last-valid SRAM word, and one word per SRAM address bit.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the seeded configuration and bring up in `no_cpu`.
. For each contiguous WSTRB mask, in the seeded order: write the initial word, write the masked bytes, and read the word back. Compare the readback against the Python strobe model (CHK-WSTRB).
. Write and read back each 64-bit pattern: the two alternating patterns, the walking one, the walking zero and the seeded extras (CHK-PATTERN).
. Write the base word and the last valid word, then read both back (CHK-BOUNDARY).
. Write a run of consecutive single-beat words with distinct values, then read each word back (CHK-SEQ).
. Write two addresses with complementary patterns, then read both back (CHK-NONVAC).
. Write the base word, one word at each power-of-two offset and the top word, each with a distinct value. Then read all of them back, and require at least one offset in the upper 192 KiB (CHK-ADDR-LINES).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WSTRB
|Every contiguous byte-strobe mask changes only its own byte lanes, with neighbours preserved — compared against a Python strobe model. A strobe-ignoring memory would zero the neighbours and fail, because unselected lanes carry zero data.
|CHK-PATTERN
|A set of 64-bit data patterns reads back exactly: the two alternating patterns, a walking one and a walking zero over all 64 bit positions, and seed-random extras.
|CHK-SEQ
|Consecutive single-beat 64-bit words hold distinct per-address values, which is the real address-decode proof in this entry.
|CHK-BOUNDARY
|The base address and the last valid SRAM word both read and write exactly. The extent is the generated map, not a doc literal: `SEP_TOP_SEP_SRAM_BASE_ADDR` = `0x10000000` and `SEP_TOP_SEP_SRAM_SIZE` = `0x40000` in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/sv/sep_addrmap_pkg.sv`, matching SramNEntries = 256 * 1024 / 8 in `hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv`, so the top valid 64-bit word is `0x1003fff8`. One-past-top decode-error is `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, not this row: that address is the ROM base.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Two addresses are written with complementary patterns and both are read back and value-checked, so a read path returning the same value for both fails. Requiring an *unwritten* neighbour to differ from a non-zero pattern is not claimed — that difference is already guaranteed by the pattern constructor; see <<audit>>.
|CHK-ADDR-LINES
|The base word, one word at every power-of-two offset from 8 bytes up to half of `SEP_SRAM_MEM_SIZE`, and the top word are all written with distinct values and then all read back exactly. A memory that decoded fewer address bits than the generated 256 KiB would alias two of them and fail. The offsets `+0x10000` and `+0x20000` sit in the upper 192 KiB.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Infrastructure gate*: non-contiguous byte strobes are not checked — the AXI
extension derives the strobe from address and length — and are not
claimed.

'''''

[[sep_sram_burst_type_test]]
===== 5.5 `sep_sram_burst_type_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_burst_type_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed FIXED and one directed WRAP burst, four 8-byte beats each.

*Objective*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc#sep-sram-target` specifies that the SRAM target serves INCR bursts of every length and single-beat bursts of every type, and refuses a multi-beat FIXED or WRAP burst before it reaches the memory: the write terminates with BRESP=SLVERR and leaves SRAM unchanged, and every beat of the read returns RRESP=SLVERR. Any other response code, and an OKAY-served burst at any beat addresses, fails.

*Test Coverage*: FIXED and WRAP, write and read, on the CPU-LSU master into SEP SRAM.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write a distinct background word to every word of the region with single beats and read it back.
. Issue the write burst, check BRESP, then read the region back with single beats and compare with the background. A failing log names the AXI4 or INCR image when the DUT wrote one.
. Restore the background, issue the read burst, and check RRESP on every beat.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BT-STIM
|The AW / AR handshake on the testbench port carries the case's start address, AxLEN, AxSIZE and AxBURST, so the verdict is about the named burst type.
|CHK-BT-CONTROL
|Single-beat background writes read back, so the region can show where a burst landed.
|CHK-BT-WRITE
|The write burst answers BRESP=SLVERR and the region still equals the background. Any other BRESP or image fails; the log names the AXI4 or INCR image when the DUT produced it.
|CHK-BT-READ
|The read burst answers RRESP=SLVERR on every beat. Any other code on any beat fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0. The SRAM target refuses both bursts (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc#sep-sram-target`): BRESP and every RRESP beat are SLVERR and the region keeps its background.

*Not claimed*: FIXED or WRAP bursts of other lengths, sizes or masters.

'''''

[[sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test]]
===== 5.6 `sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`) plus the external SMN-inbound master, `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU and external master), `SepSramInboundCfg` and `SepSramByteModel` (`seq_lib/sep_sram_inbound_burst_seq.py`)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the 16 KiB window, the burst lengths, starts and
data (walking one, walking zero or random), the overlay span and data, the
CPU-LSU words, and every inbound request attribute (AxID 1 to 63, AxPROT, a
non-reserved AxCACHE that is modifiable on bursts over 16 beats, AxQOS,
AxREGION, AxUSER, WUSER, ARLOCK on exclusive-legal reads) come from the run seed
(`SepSramInboundCfg`, `SepInboundAttrs`). Lengths 1 and 256 and two
exclusive-shaped short bursts, one read with ARLOCK set, run on every seed;
`reseed = 3`.

*Objective*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`: the SRAM target serves INCR bursts of every length, and the System Interface is the crossbar initiator that may issue bursts. The inbound filter decides on the address, `prot[1]`, `user[3:0]` and `AxLEN` only (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, "Match, Then Permit"), and the SEP documents give the other request fields no effect on an SRAM access, so none of them may change the data. AXI4 (IHI 0022, A3.4.3) gives the strobe rule: a byte whose strobe is low is not written.

*Test Coverage*: Inbound INCR bursts into SRAM, write and read, under seeded request attributes; partial strobes; cross-master readback.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped. Program two inbound-filter entries over the same 16 KiB SRAM window, `allow_ns = 1` and `allow_ns = 0`, both read+write, `src_id = 0`, `allow_burst = 1`.
. For each case: issue the burst write from the external master; issue a short unaligned overlay write inside its span; write two words of the span from the CPU-LSU master; burst-read the whole span from the external master; read the first, last and two seeded beats from the CPU-LSU master. Sample every AW and AR handshake, and RLAST on every R beat of each burst read, on the `m_axi` port. A data mismatch is recorded against its checker and the remaining cases still run.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SRAM-IN-STIM
|Each AW / AR handshake on `m_axi` carries the drawn ID, address, AxLEN, AxSIZE, AxBURST, AxLOCK, AxCACHE, AxPROT, AxQOS, AxREGION and AxUSER, so the verdicts are about the attributes the log names.
|CHK-SRAM-IN-WRITE
|Every inbound write answers OKAY with BID equal to AWID.
|CHK-SRAM-IN-READ
|Every beat of each burst read answers OKAY (EXOKAY allowed on an exclusive read), RLAST sampled on the `m_axi` R channel falls on the last beat and on no other, RID equals ARID, and every beat equals the byte model.
|CHK-SRAM-IN-STRB
|On the overlay's first and last beats, the strobed bytes hold the overlay and the unstrobed bytes keep the burst data.
|CHK-SRAM-IN-XPATH
|The CPU-LSU master reads the burst's beats back equal to the model, so the inbound writes landed at the addresses they named; and the inbound burst read returns the words the CPU-LSU master wrote, so the inbound read path returns SRAM content.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. After the last case the test fails once if any data compare missed, naming each of CHK-SRAM-IN-READ, CHK-SRAM-IN-STRB and CHK-SRAM-IN-XPATH that recorded a mismatch. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Not claimed*: per-beat RRESP. The VIP folds a burst's responses into one code, any non-OKAY beat winning, so a burst with one error beat fails but the beat is not named. Exclusive-write semantics: AWLOCK stays 0, because an exclusive write a slave refuses leaves memory unchanged and the SEP documents do not state whether SRAM supports exclusive access.

'''''

[[sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test]]
===== 5.7 `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, firmware `rom_lsu_read_test`

*Source Module*: `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW, `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv` (console + verdict mailbox; OKAY / zero read data for any address)

*Objective*: LSU reads of the Boot ROM match the loaded image; stores to ROM are ignored.

*Test Coverage*: LSU reads of the boot ROM match the image; stores to ROM are ignored.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. LSU-read ten half-words of the loaded Boot ROM image.
. Store to ROM; content must stay the image literal and the CPU must not trap.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROM-READ
|Load-store reads of ten half-words across five distinct 64-bit ROM words match the loaded image, against hard-coded literals. Nine of the ten expectations are non-zero, so a stuck-at-zero read path fails.
|CHK-ROM-BOUNDARY
|The last valid ROM word reads back exactly. One-past-top decode-error is `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ROM-WRITE-IGNORED
|A store to ROM leaves the content unchanged, compared against the *image literal* rather than against a pre-write read of the same address, and the CPU does not trap. The store's AXI response is not sampled on this firmware path.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test]]
===== 5.8 `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Warm vs cold scratch: warm bank clears, cold bank retains, both writable after release.

*Test Coverage*: Warm bank clears, cold bank retains, both writable after release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Write `SCRATCH_WARM[0]` and `SCRATCH_COLD[0]` and read both back. Require `scratch_cold_probe_o` to agree (CHK-NONVAC).
. Write a distinct pattern to all eight registers of each bank and read them back. Check the cold probe word by word (CHK-BANK-ALIAS).
. Drive `wdt_rst_ni_i` low, then high. Require `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` to read 1, then 0, then 1 (CHK-WARM-RST).
. Read `SCRATCH_WARM[0]` and require zero (CHK-WARM-CLEAR). Read `SCRATCH_COLD[0]` and the probe and require the pattern (CHK-WARM-RETAIN).
. Read both banks. Require every warm register at zero and every cold register at its pattern (CHK-WARM-BANK).
. Write new patterns to `SCRATCH_WARM[0]` and `SCRATCH_COLD[0]` and read them back (CHK-WARM-RECOVER).
. Write all eight registers of each bank with their patterns again and read them back (CHK-COLD-ARM).
. Pulse `rst_ni` and wait for fuse sense. Require `SCRATCH_WARM[0]`, `SCRATCH_COLD[0]` and the probe to read zero (CHK-COLD-REINIT).
. Read both banks and the probe. Require every register to read zero (CHK-COLD-BANK).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both scratch banks hold written values before any reset, so the retain and clear checks below are observations rather than reset-value reads.
|CHK-WARM-RST
|A warm reset source drops the CPU reset observable (assert then release). Isolation of the system-reset observable is not this row: `wdt_rst_ni_i` has no fan-out to it.
|CHK-WARM-RECOVER
|Both banks are writable again after the reset releases.
|CHK-COLD-REINIT
|A cold reset clears the cold bank too.
|CHK-WARM-CLEAR
|After the warm reset, `SCRATCH_WARM[0]` reads its reset default of zero. With CHK-WARM-RETAIN it is the pair that makes the domain split real.
|CHK-WARM-RETAIN
|After the same warm reset, `SCRATCH_COLD[0]` reads its written pattern on the CPU-LSU read, and `scratch_cold_probe_o` agrees.
|CHK-BANK-ALIAS
|Each of the sixteen implemented registers (eight per bank, `SCRATCH[8]` in `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_scratch/sep_scratch.rdl`) holds its own pattern, so an index-to-index or bank-to-bank alias fails.
|CHK-WARM-BANK
|A warm reset clears every warm register and leaves every cold register at its pattern.
|CHK-COLD-ARM
|Immediately before the cold reset, all eight registers of each bank are written with their non-reset patterns and read back, so no CHK-COLD-REINIT or CHK-COLD-BANK compare can pass on a register the earlier warm reset had already cleared.
|CHK-COLD-BANK
|A cold reset clears every register of both banks, each armed by CHK-COLD-ARM.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Independence limit*: the front-door and back-door values are not independent —
the probe is assigned from the same flops the register read returns — so their
agreement catches a read-mux fault, not an independent confirmation that the
reset landed.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test]]
===== 5.9 `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live` on Verilator; graded logs from `--target default` on VCS with the LSU force-splice), `+skip_fuse_sense`. The reason `dbg_rstb_i` isolation is not claimed (the pin has no netlist path to either reset observable) is shown for the stub build only. It is not checked for the full-CPU build that produces the graded logs.

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the CPU and system reset observables are released at rest, and the
CPU observable is live under a real reset source. `dbg_rstb_i` isolation is not
claimed: the pin has no netlist path to either observable in this build.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and system reset observables at rest; CPU reset observable liveness under `wdt_rst_ni_i` (`dbg_rstb_i` isolation not claimed).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Both reset observables released at rest with `dbg_rstb_i` high.
. Drive `wdt_rst_ni_i` low: `sep_cpu_reset_n` falls and `sep_reset_n` stays high. Release it: `sep_cpu_reset_n` is restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BASELINE
|Both reset observables are released at rest.
|CHK-LIVE
|A real reset source drops the CPU reset observable and then restores it, so the observable is live rather than stuck at one.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*No feature-matrix row.* The denominator rule is that the architecture
documents define the feature set. `dbg_rstb_i` is named in them — a port-table
line in `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` and a sentence in the integrator guide
describing a separate reset for the EL2 CPU debug module — but neither states a
behavioural contract, and nothing requires isolation from the system reset. A feature the specification does not define is not a
row, so this entry earns none. The two
checkers above are a reset-tree liveness guard, not a feature credit.

'''''

[[sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test]]
===== 5.10 `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense on the committed
default preload (`+sep_efuse_preload`)

*Source Module*: `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, eFuse image golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the armed, in-flight and post-reset data values come
from the run seed (`SepSeededRng`). The probes, both ops, every reset offset and
every sense offset are walked on every seed.

*Objective*: Proves that a cold reset which lands with a register access
outstanding, or with fuse sensing in progress, leaves every path usable.
`rst_ni` has asynchronous assertion (`doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, Clock
and Reset Requirements) and resets the whole subsystem
(`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture), so it is the one SEP reset
that can land on any cycle. The `SW_RESET_N` domains isolate and drain their
paths before their reset asserts (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`,
Isolation and Reset Sequencing), so no software reset is used and none can
reach these cases.

*Test Coverage*: one read-write probe register on each converted register port
the CPU-LSU master reaches: OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM`, AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, HMAC
`INTR_ENABLE`, KMAC `ENTROPY_PERIOD`, CSRNG and EDN `INTR_ENABLE`, watchdog
`WKUP_THOLD_LO`, SPI host `CONFIGOPTS`, DMA `SRC_ADDR_LO` and the Adams Bridge
`notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r` counter. Each probe is read and written. Fuse
sensing is reset on every early cycle and at points across the sense time.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up with real fuse sense; the base class compares the sensed shadow with
  the staged image after every sense-done. `clk_wdt_i` runs at eight core
  periods, as in `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`: a watchdog threshold access
  waits on the `clk_wdt_i` register synchronizer and outlasts the AXI timeout
  at the 200 kHz default.
. For each probe and op, run the probe access with no reset on the VIP master
  with its own AXI ID (3) and record the request and response handshakes on the
  `s_axi` pins. The response window is the cycle count from the last request
  handshake to the response handshake.
. For each offset from 0 to two less than the window (every cycle for a
  window up to 24 cycles, otherwise ten evenly spaced cycles with both ends):
  write a non-reset value to the probe and read it back, start the probe
  access, and assert `rst_ni` on the falling clock edge that many cycles after
  the clock edge that transfers the last request beat. Offset 0 lands half a
  cycle after that edge; the last offset lands half a cycle before the
  response handshake shows on the pins. Hold it 20 cycles, release it and wait
  for sense-done.
. Read the probe, write a new value and read it back, through the SEP
  sequencer.
. Measure the sense time. Then for every cycle 0 to 31 after the `rst_ni`
  release, and seven points across the sense time and two cycles before its
  end: assert `rst_ni` after a completed sense and read the shadow
  (`efuse_shadow_probe_o`) at the end of the hold, release it, assert `rst_ni`
  again at that offset with sense-done low and read the shadow again, then let
  the next sense complete.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PROBE-LIVE
|With no reset, each probe access completes OKAY: a read returns the value
written before it and a write reads back. Each probe is a real access, and its
window is measured on the DUT rather than assumed.
|CHK-ARM
|Before each landing the probe holds a written value that is not its RDL reset,
so CHK-RESET-VALUE fails on a path the reset did not reach.
|CHK-MIDFLIGHT
|At every landing the request handshake has completed: the reset asserts after
the clock edge that transfers the last request beat. For each probe and op at
least one landing comes before the response is accepted on `s_axi`, and for
every such landing the VIP master abandons the access (its completion carries
no response). The watchdog response window varies with the `clk_wdt_i` phase,
so a watchdog landing can come after its response; it is logged by offset and
grades recovery only.
|CHK-RESET-VALUE
|The first read after the reset completes OKAY with the probe's reset value from
the register export (`abr_reg.rdl` for the Adams Bridge counter), within half
the AXI timeout. A bridge left waiting for the response it lost wedges this
read and fails it by name, and a bridge that replays the lost response returns
a value this read can reject.
|CHK-RECOVER
|A write after the reset reads back exactly, OKAY.
|CHK-NO-STALE
|No R or B beat with the abandoned access's ID appears after the reset. The
sequencer issues ID 0, so such a beat can only be a response the DUT kept
across the reset.
|CHK-SHADOW-CLEAR
|With `rst_ni` low, every shadow word reads its reset value, both after a
completed sense and with sense-done low. The reset value is the RDL reset from
the register export, except the LC_STATE word: it reads the INVALID hold
(`{~4'b1111, 4'b1111}`) that `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (Fuse
Sensing and Boot Sequencing) gives before sensing completes. The staged image
differs from the reset value in at least one word, so a shadow that keeps its
value through the reset fails this check. At least one reset with sense-done
low must find shadow words the partial sense wrote, or the check fails as
vacuous.
|CHK-SENSE-RESTART
|A reset that lands with sense-done low is followed by a sense that completes,
and the sensed shadow matches the staged image word for word. The shadow read
its reset value before that sense (CHK-SHADOW-CLEAR), so the match comes from
words the restarted sense wrote.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the DMA master path (the DMA engine's own outbound accesses),
eFuse programming and the token digest engines with a reset in flight, and a
Key Manager reset while the Key Manager runs. No probe here starts those
operations. The JTAG reset overrides also skip the isolation sequence, but they
are a debug path (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`) and are not used.

'''''

[[sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test]]
===== 5.11 `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Wakeup and watchdog tick, threshold expiry, wakeup cause and its RW1C, the watchdog pet, and the REGWEN lock on the AON timer. Bark and bite are not claimed here: `sep_nmi_sanity_test` and `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` own those legs, and no checker in this entry observes either.

*Test Coverage*: AON timer tick, threshold, wakeup cause RW1C, pet, and REGWEN,
plus that the `clk_ref_i` reference counter advances and its crossing onto
`clk_i` hands the value over. The software load of that counter is not claimed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Set `clk_wdt` to eight core periods and bring up in `no_cpu`. Program a high wakeup threshold and enable the wakeup counter with prescaler 0. Require `WKUP_COUNT` to advance over about 60 ticks and `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` to stay 0. Then force `wkup_expired` with `INTR_TEST`, require it set, and clear it (CHK-WKUP-COUNT).
. Measure the wakeup count advance over one window with prescaler 0 and over the same window with the seeded prescaler. Require the divided advance to be at least 1, at most the divisor bound, and below the undivided advance (CHK-WKUP-PRESCALE).
. Program a small threshold and enable the counter. Poll until `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` sets, then disable the counter (CHK-WKUP-EXPIRE).
. Require `WKUP_CAUSE` set. Reset `WKUP_COUNT` below the threshold and require the cause to stay set. Write 0 to `WKUP_CAUSE` and require it to clear (CHK-WKUP-CAUSE).
. Clear `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` by write-one-to-clear and require 0 (CHK-WKUP-EXPIRE).
. With the counter off and zeroed and the status clear, write `INTR_TEST`. Require `wkup_expired` to set, then clear it by write-one-to-clear (CHK-INTR-TEST).
. Set high bark and bite thresholds, enable the watchdog and wait 1200 ticks. Require `WDOG_COUNT` at or above `0x400`. Write 0 and require the count at or below `0x100` and below one eighth of the pre-pet count (CHK-WDOG-PET).
. Read `REFERENCE_COUNTER` before and after a 400-cycle window, high half first, and require it to advance (CHK-REFCNT-RUNS).
. Write `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and read it back (CHK-NONVAC). Write 0 to `WDOG_REGWEN`. Write `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` and require both unchanged (CHK-REGWEN-LOCK).
. With the lock closed, write `WKUP_THOLD_LO` and read it back. Let `WDOG_COUNT` climb past the literal floor, write 0, and require the readback to fall below the value it held (CHK-REGWEN-SCOPE).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WKUP-COUNT
|The wakeup counter advances on its own tick, and `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` stays 0 under the high threshold for that window.
|CHK-WKUP-EXPIRE
|The wakeup threshold expiry sets its interrupt, which then clears on write-one-to-clear.
|CHK-WKUP-CAUSE
|The wakeup cause register is still set after COUNT is reset, then write-0 clears it.
|CHK-WDOG-PET
|Petting the watchdog resets its counter. The observation window must run long enough that the pre-pet count exceeds the clock-crossing tolerance by a wide margin, and the post-pet value must be clearly below it. A tolerance wider than the count the window reaches passes when the pet is ignored — see <<audit>>.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A pre-lock threshold write lands, so the lock check below is not reading a reset value.
|CHK-REGWEN-LOCK
|Once locked, both `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` reject writes.
|CHK-WKUP-PRESCALE
|`WKUP_CTRL.prescaler` divides the wakeup count rate. The measurement window is sized from the programmed divisor so the divided run must advance (a frozen counter fails the lower bound) and must stay at or below the divisor bound (an ignored prescaler fails the upper bound). Both bounds come from the divisor, not from the undivided measurement. The divisor is the OpenTitan AON Timer specification's cycles-per-tick rule (one more than `WKUP_CTRL.prescaler`), carried by `sep_spec_tables`; it is not taken from the timer RTL.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|`INTR_TEST` forces `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` with the wakeup counter off, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-REGWEN-SCOPE
|With the watchdog lock closed, `WKUP_THOLD_LO` and `WDOG_COUNT` still accept writes. The lock covers the watchdog configuration only. `WDOG_COUNT` is free-running and `WDOG_CTRL` is itself lock-covered, so the write is proven by LOWERING the counter, which free running cannot do: the counter first climbs past a literal floor, and the write to zero must then read back below the value it held. Raising the counter instead would be satisfied by free running alone on a blocked write, and a target near the seeded bark threshold would trip a bark.
|CHK-REFCNT-RUNS
|`SEP_CPU_CTRL.REFERENCE_COUNTER` advances across an observation window. This is the only SEP counter clocked by `clk_ref_i` rather than `clk_i`: it counts on the reference edge and resynchronises the value onto `clk_i` for the CSR read, so a reference clock that is not running and a crossing that never hands the value over both present as a perfectly stable CSR. The 64-bit read takes the high half, the low half and the high half again, and repeats the low half if a carry landed between them, so a carry is not reported as a count that went backwards.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the software LOAD of `REFERENCE_COUNTER`. The written value is
lost when `clk_i` runs far faster than `clk_ref_i`. This bench drives
`clk_i` at 1.25 ns and `clk_ref_i` at 10 ns. The update crosses on a depth-1
async FIFO whose source comment states that an update arriving before the
previous one has crossed is "dropped with no error indication", and that
primitive's guard assertion is compiled out of this build, so the loss is
silent.

'''''

[[sep_clock_gate_control_test]]
===== 5.12 `sep_clock_gate_control_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_clock_gate_control_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that the one implemented `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` bit (`pka_cg_enable[0:0]`) stores in both directions, that the unimplemented bits read zero, and that the bit gates nothing. The RTL sinks the bit into an unused net.

*Test Coverage*: `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` storage and reserved-bit RAZWI; no live per-IP gate.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write `pka_cg_enable` 1 then 0 and read it back.
. Read AES / HMAC / OTBN / SW_DEBUG CSRs with the bit 0 and 1.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STORAGE
|The implemented bit writes and reads back in both directions.
|CHK-STUB-CONST
|AES STATUS, HMAC INTR_STATE, OTBN STATUS and SW_DEBUG read the *same value* with `pka_cg_enable` set and clear, not merely OKAY on both: the first read of each witness is kept and the second, taken with the bit flipped, must match it. A witness whose value moved with the enable would answer OKAY either way, so completing the access is not the contract. Bound: these CSRs are constant in this build, so the compare catches an enable that perturbs them and cannot by itself distinguish "the bit gates nothing" from "these witnesses never change".
|CHK-RSVD-RAZWI
|An all-ones write to both 32-bit halves of `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` reads back only the implemented bit. An unimplemented bit that stores, or an alias onto the upper half, fails here.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: the implemented contract is the designer placeholder, not a live
per-IP clock gate.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-mem-boot-reset]]
==== 5. Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock, Phase 3

This section holds the Phase 3 tests of the memory, boot, reset, watchdog and
clock area. Each test is written from the specification and the register
description only. The entries carry every field a checker needs.

Each entry opens its *Test Coverage* field with an Extends statement. It names
the tests that grade a neighbouring contract and states the cells that the entry
adds. A cell that another test grades is a control in the entry and is never
graded a second time. Tests that share a stimulus and a run mode are one test
with one leg for each contract.

*Observation class*. Every probe, port and inject that a test uses is in one of
four classes.

* *Approved*: the item is in the closed set of `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
  or it exists in `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` of the open tree.
* *Observation tap*: a read-only tap that is outside the closed set of
  `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and is not in `tb_top.sv`. It drives nothing. The test
  adds it to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list, and a check that needs it
  is graded. The observation taps are in the list <<sep-vplan-phase3-obs-taps>>.
* *Added hook*: an error inject, or a top-port drive (a drive of a port of the
  `sep` module), that is outside the closed set of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and is not
  in `tb_top.sv`. The test adds an error inject to the inject exceptions of
  `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and a top-port drive to the port table of its HDL Top
  section, and a check that needs it is graded. The added hooks are in the list
  <<sep-vplan-phase3-added-hooks>>.
* *PROPOSED*: a drive, force or deposit of a net inside the `sep` module for a
  purpose other than error injection, that is outside the closed set of
  `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and is not in `tb_top.sv`. A check that needs a PROPOSED
  item is a *blocked* check: the entry marks it `Blocked: needs <item>`
  (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). A blocked check does not run and is not counted
  as reached. An FCOV bin that needs a blocked check is not collected. The
  PROPOSED items are in the list <<sep-vplan-phase3-proposed-drives>>.

The *BFM / Model* field of each entry gives the class of every probe, port and
inject that the entry uses.

*Bench taps*. A tap is a read-only cross-module reference to a net that the
specification names. A tap never drives a signal. The bench index of an
isolate or reset tap is a bench table that resolves a path name. It is never an
expected value.

[cols="3,5,3",options="header"]
|===
|Tap |Reads |Class
|`dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (`sep_reset_n`), `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` (`sep_cpu_reset_n`)
|The SEP reset and the CPU reset (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller).
|Approved: SEP reset probes of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`ip_rst_n[6:1]`
|The sequenced engine resets `*_gated_rst_n_probe_o` of OTBN (1), AES (2), HMAC (3), KMAC (4), TRNG (5) and Adams Bridge (6). The bench index is the `SW_RESET_N` bit number (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`).
|Approved: crypto reset-sequencer probes of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`ip_rst_n[0]`
|The Key Manager warm reset, bit 0 of `sep_crypto_gated_rst_no`.
|Observation tap. `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` lists no Key Manager reset probe
|`iso_ack` and `iso_req` of the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge host paths, `iso_ack` of their Key Manager key paths and of the three TRNG host paths
|The isolate-complete bits `*_host_isolated_probe_o`, `*_km_isolated_probe_o` and `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o`, and the host isolate request `*_host_isolate_req_probe_o` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing).
|Approved: crypto reset-sequencer probes of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`iso_req` and `iso_ack` of all other isolate stages
|The OTBN and AES host and key paths, the three TRNG requests, the five Key Manager key paths and the Key Manager to eFuse path. The 14 isolate stages are the rows of the table of paths isolated before each software reset. The bench index x of a stage is a bench table: 0 to 4 the host paths of OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge; 5 to 7 the ESRC, CSRNG and EDN host paths of the TRNG; 8 to 12 the five Key Manager key paths to OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge; 13 the Key Manager to eFuse path.
|Observation tap. One 14-bit request vector and one 14-bit complete vector at the crypto AXI interconnect
|`scratch_cold_probe_o`
|The cold scratch bank words.
|Approved: scratch probe of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`secure_tm_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `wdt_timer_rst_req_o`, `ext_debug_bus_o`
|DUT outputs that `tb_top.sv` taps.
|Approved: existing in `tb_top.sv`
|`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`
|The vector that feeds the PIC. Bit i is source i+1 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map).
|Approved: IRQ aggregate probe of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`dbg_iccm_active_o`, `dbg_dccm_active_o`
|Any clock enable of the ICCM or the DCCM macro request.
|Approved: existing in `tb_top.sv`
|`cpu_trace_valid_o`, `cpu_trace_addr_o`, `cpu_trace_insn_o`, `cpu_trace_ecause_o`, `cpu_trace_interrupt_o`, `dbg_cpu_trace_exc_o`
|The CPU trace port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `sep_cpu_trace_o`).
|Approved: CPU trace of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|Firmware console and outbound mailbox marker
|Words that firmware stores to the outbound mailbox (`sep_outbound_mbx`).
|Approved: FW console of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`aon_rst_n`
|The watchdog timer-domain reset `rst_aon_ni` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`, Clock, Reset, and Debug Behavior).
|Observation tap
|`mpc_run_sync`
|The output of the run-request synchronizer (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller).
|Observation tap
|`km_cond_rst[1:0]`
|The two Key Manager reset conditioner outputs.
|Observation tap
|`ifu_req` and `ifu_rsp`, `lsu_rom_req`, `lsu_xbar_req` and `lsu_xbar_rsp`, `dbg_sb_req` and `dbg_sb_rsp`
|The CPU master ports after the demuxes (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU).
|Observation tap
|`xbar_tcm_req` and `cpu_tcm_axi_resp`
|The request and the response port of the local crossbar `cpu_tcm` target, the DMA path into the TCM. It is not the macro port `sep_cpu_tcm_req_o`.
|Observation tap
|`dmi_uncore_en_o`, `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o`, `jtag_tdo_o`
|DUT outputs that are open in the DUT instance.
|Observation tap
|===

The entries name the extension port, the outbound port, the ROM, SRAM and TCM
macro ports and the TCM response as `ext_req`, `out_req`, `rom_req`, `rom_rsp`,
`sram_req`, `sram_rsp`, `tcm_req` and `tcm_rsp`. Each is a net of the observation
taps PR-EXT, PR-OUT, PR-ROM, PR-SRAM and PR-TCM in
<<sep-vplan-phase3-obs-taps>>.
Only the TCM clock enables and the bank
address are Approved, through `dbg_iccm_active_o` and `dbg_dccm_active_o`.

*Bench drives and injects*.

[cols="3,5,3",options="header"]
|===
|Drive or inject |Use |Class
|`rst_ni`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i`, `mpc_reset_run_req`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `test_en_strap_i` (`secure_tm_req_i`), `rst_vec_i`
|Reset, boot and strap stimulus.
|Approved: TB inputs of `tb_top.sv`
|`clk_i`, `clk_wdt_i`, `clk_ref_i` with a chosen period and start phase
|Clock-domain legs.
|Approved: cocotb clock drive of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i`, `jtag_<ip>_rst_hold_i`, the IC_RESET TDR inputs `jtag_ic_reset_*` with the `SepIcResetTdr` driver
|Reset overrides.
|Approved: TB inputs of `tb_top.sv`
|`+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`
|Boot variants.
|Approved: plusargs of `tb_top.sv`
|`tcm_load_i`, ROM image initialisation, the SEP SRAM word probes
|Image load into public macro arrays and a read of the SEP SRAM words.
|Approved: `tb_backdoor_mem` image initialisation of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|`j_axi_*`
|The JTAG AXI-Lite port for the `SEC_DIS` token.
|Approved: TB inputs of `tb_top.sv`
|`test_en_i` and `scan_rst_ni` as bench inputs
|DFT stimulus. `tb_top.sv` ties them to 0 and 1.
|Added hook: top-port drive
|`dmi_core_enable_i` and `dmi_uncore_enable_i` as bench inputs
|Debug-module stimulus. `tb_top.sv` ties both to 0.
|Added hook: top-port drive
|Bench-owned `jtag_tck_i`, `jtag_tms_i`, `jtag_tdi_i`, `jtag_trst_ni` as TB inputs and a DTM shifter
|The reset-vector TDR and the debug module. `tb_top.sv` keeps the pins in internal registers.
|Added hook: top-port drive
|TCM bit-flip hook (word address, bit mask) on the ICCM and DCCM macro arrays, with a time-0 flip list for firmware legs
|Fault injection of stored-word bit errors.
|Added hook: error inject
|ROM macro bit-flip hook
|Fault injection of stored-word bit errors in the boot ROM.
|Added hook: error inject
|===

*Conventions*.

* Logging. Every checker prints `CHK-<NAME> PASS: ...` with the measured
  values. A FAIL line prints the expected and the actual value. A test passes
  only when `results.xml` holds one PASS line for each checker the entry lists
  that is not blocked. A clean simulator exit is not evidence.
* Randomness. One `SepSeededRng(seed)` per leaf
  (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_seeded_rng.py`). Each draw is logged as
  `DRAW <name>=<value>`. The stream is not a CSPRNG. No draw makes a key, a
  token or a fuse word. `--stage sim --seed N` replays a leaf. A per-seed value
  is not VPLAN evidence. RAND-REP means that many seeds replay a legal matrix
  in one cheap leaf. RANDCFG means one random configuration per leaf (the leaf
  is a boot or runs firmware).
* Expected values. Every expected value comes from a cited sentence of the
  specification or the register description. A value that only the DUT shows
  is a logged observation (`OBS-`) and is never graded. A calibration run that
  places a stimulus (a measured response window) is not an expected value.
* Tolerances. A tolerance in a checker (synchronizer latency, count slack, a
  liveness timeout) is an environment choice. The entry says so. The
  specification gives no tolerance.
* Control windows. A "never" check names a control window in the same leaf. In
  the control window a healthy design drives the same signal. A check without a
  live control is not part of the test.
* Blocked checks. A check that needs a PROPOSED item is listed under
  *Blocked*. It keeps its contract text and its control. It has no CHK log
  line.

[[sep_boot_release_terms_test]]
===== 5.13 `sep_boot_release_terms_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense (no skip plusarg), Verilator and VCS. The X checks run on VCS only. Two testlist entries, `reseed = 3` each, because the bench configuration differs: `sep_boot_release_terms_test` (normal leaf, no extra argument) and `sep_boot_release_terms_test_smc_hold` (hold leaf, `+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`). The plusarg sets the bench SMC fuse-sense model at time 0 (`tb_top.sv`), so the seed cannot select it and no drive changes it during a leaf. Each leaf runs three boots (the calibration boot, the H = yes epoch and one drawn epoch), each started by a cold reset (`rst_ni`) with a real sense.

*Source Module*: `sep_boot_release_terms_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` (the LSU read path); eFuse image golden (`env/sep_efuse_image.py`: the staged image is the golden of the shadow words); the bench SMC fuse-sense input model. Approved probes and drives: the `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` taps (SEP reset probes of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`), `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` (existing in `tb_top.sv`), `rst_ni`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i`, `+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`. No PROPOSED item.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, one draw per line in the log (`DRAW <name>=<value>`). Every leaf runs the error-injection epoch of `ext_boot_seq_done_i`, BOOT_AFTER_SENSE with H = yes (the term held low after sensing), in its own boot after a cold reset. The seed draws one more epoch E, in its own boot, from BOOT_FIRST (before the `rst_ni` release), BOOT_WITH_RST (at the `rst_ni` release), BOOT_MID (after the `rst_ni` release and before `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises) and BOOT_AFTER_SENSE with H = no (a short delay after `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises), with the weights 2, 2, 2 and 1. T is the sense time that the calibration boot measures in step 2. A value whose range depends on T is drawn as a fraction in [0,1) and is scaled to clocks in the step that places the stimulus, so the log shows the draw and the scaled clock count. The draws, in this order: the epoch E; the order of the two epochs; per epoch the reset width W (2 to 40 `clk_i` cycles); fuse image I (`LC_STATE` from {TEST_DEV, RMA_SIP, RMA_CHIPLET}, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` from the inbound-filter-safe vector set, never `SEC_DIS`); the arrival delay of each epoch (BOOT_FIRST: 1 to W clocks before the release; BOOT_WITH_RST: 0; BOOT_MID: a fraction `u_m`, scaled in step 4 to 1 + floor(`u_m` times (T-1)) clocks after the release; BOOT_AFTER_SENSE with H = no: 1 to 400 clocks after `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises; BOOT_AFTER_SENSE with H = yes: a hold extra of 0 to 400 clocks, added in step 4 to a hold of 2 T clocks); per epoch the probe count N (3 to 8), for each probe the shadow word index (0 to 255), and for each probe after the first a fraction `u_i` (scaled in step 5 to 1 + floor(`u_i` times (T-1)) clocks after the release); last, the poll count n of step 8 (8 to 64). Probe 1 is not drawn in time: it issues 1 clock after the release. The SMC input level comes from the testlist entry (held: `+sep_smc_fuse_sense_hold`; done: no argument), not from a draw. `reseed = 3` per entry samples different epochs E, orders, delays, widths, images, probe words and poll counts. The drawn epoch fills no `cp_release_order` bin: in each drawn arrival the sensing or a short delay is the last term. No draw makes a key, a token or a fuse word.

*Objective*: `sep_reset_n` and `sep_cpu_reset_n` release only after `rst_ni` is high, fuse sensing is done and `ext_boot_seq_done_i` is high, in any arrival order (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture, boot-qualified SEP reset). `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` follows the SMC fuse-sense input (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `smc_fuse_sense_done_i`; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`). From the `rst_ni` release until `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises, a shadow-map read answers SLVERR, whatever the staged image holds (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing: "Until then, APB accesses to the MAP window return SLVERR"). After sensing, the same read returns the staged word.

*Test Coverage*: Extends the open tests `sep_sec_dis_override_test` and `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` (both resets stay low until sensing completes with `ext_boot_seq_done_i` held at 1, so sensing is the only open term), `sep_efuse_sense_test` (the rise of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`) and `sep_efuse_image_test` (`SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` = 1 after sensing). Those open tests hold `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1, and this test drives it low. Only the new cells are graded here, and the open owners keep the rest. The new cells are: `ext_boot_seq_done_i` as a release term in a held case in every leaf and in four arrival orders that the seed draws, the follow of `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` in the hold entry and the normal entry, and the SLVERR answer of the shadow map before sensing completes with the same word read back after it. The sense-only release, the rise of the port and the first sense status read are controls. The BOOT_FIRST, BOOT_WITH_RST and BOOT_MID cells are controls too, because the sensing is the last term in them. The boot term is graded in the BOOT_AFTER_SENSE cell with H = yes. The `FEAT_CTRL` profile and the value of the first `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` poll belong to the open owners. The polls are logged as `OBS-SENSE-POLL` and are not graded. Functional coverage: `SEP_FCOV` Phase 2 coverpoints `cp_release_order` (the H = yes epoch), `cp_smc_status_follow` and `cp_presense_window`, and the Phase 1 group `sep_efuse_sense_cg` (`cp_done`). The calibration boot, the port rise and the sense status polls are controls and fill no Phase 3 bin. The drawn epoch fills `cp_presense_window` only. Its (OKAY, 1) bin samples only the read-back of step 7 of a word whose window probe answered SLVERR in the same boot.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw all values in the order above and log them (the values that depend on T are fractions at this point). Stage image I with the eFuse image staging helper. Hold `wdt_rst_ni_i` and `dbg_rstb_i` high and the JTAG `sep_reset_n` override off for the whole leaf. Steps 3 to 7 run once for each of the two epochs, in the drawn order, and each epoch uses its own W, delay and probes. In the hold leaf the SMC input stays low and the leaf sends no request to the SMC (a request to the SMC hangs while its fuse sense runs, `sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`).
. Calibration boot. Drive `ext_boot_seq_done_i` to 1, pulse `rst_ni` for W cycles and release it. Count the `clk_i` cycles from the release to the rise of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`: this is T. Wait for both reset taps to read 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). T only places the stimulus of the next steps. It is never an expected value.
. Assert `rst_ni`. While `rst_ni` is low, drive `ext_boot_seq_done_i` to 0. Start the sampler: on every `clk_i` cycle from this point until both reset taps read 1 it records the `sep_reset_n` tap, the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i` and `rst_ni`.
. Place the arrival of `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at the drawn point: BOOT_FIRST raises it while `rst_ni` is low, at the drawn clock before the release. BOOT_WITH_RST raises it in the `clk_i` cycle of the release. BOOT_MID raises it at 1 + floor(`u_m` times (T-1)) clocks after the release (log `u_m` and the clock count). BOOT_AFTER_SENSE with H = no raises it at the drawn delay after `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises. Error injection, H = yes: the term stays low for 2 T plus the drawn extra clocks after `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises and is then raised. Expected response: both reset taps read 0 on every cycle of the hold and rise after the term rises.
. Release `rst_ni` after W cycles. From the release, issue the N window probes in order. Each probe is a 4-byte read of its shadow-map word. Probe 1 issues 1 clock after the release, so at least one probe falls in the window for every draw (the sensing takes at least 257 clocks: 256 response strobes and one cycle, `otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing). Probe i, for i from 2 to N, issues at 1 + floor(`u_i` times (T-1)) clocks after the release (the fractions are sorted in rising order; log each `u_i` and its clock count) (a probe whose clock has passed issues when the previous response completes). Record for each probe the response code, the read data and the value of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` at the cycle of the response handshake. A probe whose response lands with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` high is logged and not scored.
. Wait for both reset taps to read 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-REL-GATE). Under H = yes the taps read 0 during the whole hold of step 4 and the wait ends after the term rises.
. Read the same N shadow-map words in the same order. Each answers OKAY with the staged word of image I (CHK-PRESENSE-CTL, which is also the control of CHK-PRESENSE; the graded window stays open across this step).
. Sense status. In the normal leaf, wait for the bench SMC input to read 1 (a bounded wait). Poll `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` n times (n from the draw of step 1), each as a 64-bit read. Poll `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` n times and log the polls as `OBS-SENSE-POLL`.
. Evaluate the sampler records and the readbacks.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-REL-GATE
|The `sep_reset_n` tap and the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap stay 0 from the `rst_ni` release until the last of the three terms (`rst_ni` high, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` high, `ext_boot_seq_done_i` high), and rise after it (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). A high on either tap before the last term is a FAIL. The rise follows the last term within a bounded wait; a timeout is a FAIL. A design that ignores `ext_boot_seq_done_i` shows a premature high in the BOOT_AFTER_SENSE cell with H = yes, where the term is last and stays low after the sensing ends. A design that never releases shows the timeout. Anchor: the `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` taps against the timestamps of the three terms at their ports. Control: in the same leaf both taps rise after the last term. In BOOT_FIRST, BOOT_WITH_RST and BOOT_MID the last term is the sensing, which the open owners grade: a design that ignores `ext_boot_seq_done_i` releases at the same time as a healthy one, so these cells are controls. The BOOT_AFTER_SENSE cells with H = no draw a delay that can fall inside the two-flop synchronizer latency (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller), so they are extra cells and do not separate a boot-blind design either. On VCS, `sep_reset_n` is never X while `rst_ni` is low. Evidence: `CHK-REL-GATE PASS: order=<O> t_rst_rel=<c0> t_sense=<c1> t_boot=<c2> t_sep_rst_rise=<c3> last_term=<name> premature_high_cycles=0 timeout_ok=1`.
|CHK-REL-HOLD
|Applies to the H = yes epoch, which every leaf runs. Under H = yes both taps stay 0 for the whole hold and rise after `ext_boot_seq_done_i` rises (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). A design that releases on sensing alone shows a high tap during the hold, because its release follows the sensing within the synchronizer latency and the hold lasts at least 2 T clocks. This is the cell that separates such a design. Anchor: the two reset taps and the port. Control: the same leaf releases once the term rises. Evidence: `CHK-REL-HOLD PASS: held=<n> cycles sep_reset_n=0 sep_cpu_reset_n=0, rose <m> cycles after ext_boot_seq_done_i`.
|CHK-SMC-FOLLOW
|`SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` equals the level of `smc_fuse_sense_done_i` on every poll: 0 on every poll of the hold leaf, 1 on every poll of the normal leaf after the bench model raises the input (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`). A register stuck at 0 fails the normal leaf, and one stuck at 1 fails the hold leaf. Anchor: the LSU readback against the bench input level. Control: the expected value of the two leaves differs, so one stuck value cannot pass both. Evidence: `CHK-SMC-FOLLOW PASS: leaf=<hold\|normal> polls=<n> smc_status=<v> control_other_leaf=<v>`; `control_other_leaf` is the value that the other leaf expects. The `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` polls are logged as `OBS-SENSE-POLL: polls=<n> first_poll=<v> ones=<n>` and are not graded.
|CHK-PRESENSE
|Each window probe whose response handshake falls while `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is low answers SLVERR, whatever the staged image holds (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing). At least one probe falls in the window, or the leaf fails. A design that answers OKAY in the window, with data of any value, fails. Anchor: the real master response code, qualified by the `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` level at the response handshake. The read data of the SLVERR answer is not claimed. Control: CHK-PRESENSE-CTL. Evidence: `CHK-PRESENSE PASS: hit=<i> cyc=<c> word=<w> resp=SLVERR done_at_resp=0`.
|CHK-PRESENSE-CTL
|After the sensing the same word returns OKAY and the staged word of image I (`hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, Fuse Sensing and Boot Sequencing). The expected value is the word that the leaf staged, not a DUT readback. A design that answers OKAY in the window fails CHK-PRESENSE against this control. Anchor: the real master readback. Evidence: `CHK-PRESENSE-CTL PASS: word=<w> resp=OKAY exp=0x<v> got=0x<v>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. CHK-REL-GATE, CHK-PRESENSE and CHK-PRESENSE-CTL PASS in both epochs of the leaf, and CHK-REL-HOLD PASSes in the H = yes epoch; a leaf that misses the H = yes epoch fails. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The sense-only release, the rise of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and the first `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` read are graded by the open owners named in Test Coverage. The `FEAT_CTRL` profile is not graded. The bench has no drive that releases the SMC input in the middle of a leaf, so the leaf grades the follow of a stable input only. The leaf sends no outbound SMC request: the register description states that a request sent while `smc_fuse_sense_done` is 0 hangs and gets no response, and the wait bound that would end the hang is a choice of the environment (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The read data of a shadow-map read that answers SLVERR is not claimed. The polarity and layout of `lc_state_o` are not claimed (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).

'''''

[[sep_cold_reset_phase_test]]
===== 5.14 `sep_cold_reset_phase_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense` with `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1, so `rst_ni` is the only open term. VCS only (sub-cycle phase and X on reset need VCS). `reseed = 4`. Testlist `system.toml`, groups `all` and `cpu_stub`. Each leaf runs two `rst_ni` passes: the control pass and one graded pass.

*Source Module*: `sep_cold_reset_phase_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` (the LSU register path); the cocotb `clk_i` and `clk_wdt_i` drives with a chosen period and start phase. Approved probes and drives: the `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` tap, the `rst_ni` drive at a chosen phase, `clk_i` (SEP reset probes and TB inputs of `tb_top.sv`), the `clk_wdt_i` start phase (cocotb clock drive of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`). `aon_rst_n` is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, one draw per line in the log. The graded pass takes one third j of the `clk_i` period (10 to 33, 33 to 67 or 67 to 90 percent), which the seed draws with equal weights. Every third is off the `clk_i` edge, so each leaf can fail CHK-RST-SYNC. The draws, in this order: the third j; the reset width Wc of each pass (8 to 64 `clk_i` cycles; the floor of 8 is an environment choice); the assert phase pa_0 of the control pass (10 to 90 percent of the `clk_i` period); the assert phase pa_j and the release phase pr_j of the graded pass, both inside third j; and the `clk_wdt_i` start phase (0 to one `clk_wdt_i` period). `clk_wdt_i` runs at eight `clk_i` periods in every leaf. The leaf places the release of the graded pass in the `clk_i` cycle that puts it inside third j of the `clk_wdt_i` period. `reseed = 4` samples different thirds, widths, phases and `clk_wdt_i` start phases. The control pass releases at a fixed phase. The third bins of `cp_rst_release_sync` and `cp_aon_release_sync` are seed-dependent and fill on a merge of regressions: the three cells are equally likely in a leaf.

*Objective*: The SEP reset (`sep_reset_n`) releases synchronously to `clk_i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller, `sep_reset_no`: the 2-flop `u_sep_reset_sync` releases synchronously to `clk_i`). The input of `u_sep_reset_sync` is `sep_intermediate_reset_n`, which the eFuse wrapper makes by synchronizing the AND of `rst_ni` and the other boot terms. The specification gives no assertion timing for that synchronizer, so the delay from the `rst_ni` fall to the `sep_reset_n` fall is logged and not graded (<<sep-vplan-phase3-unstated>>). The watchdog timer-domain reset releases synchronized to `clk_wdt_i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture; `hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`, Clock, Reset, and Debug Behavior). After a cold reset the watchdog and wakeup control registers hold their reset values (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture, cold reset row; `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aon_timer/regs/gen/adoc/aon_timer.adoc`, Reset column of `WDOG_CTRL`, `WKUP_CTRL`, `INTR_STATE`). The cold default of `WDOG_REGWEN`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` and `WKUP_THOLD_HI` has one owner, `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (CHK-REGWEN-COLD).

*Test Coverage*: Extends the open tests `sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test` (it asserts `rst_ni` only as the liveness anchor of its isolation leg), `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test` (the recovery after a cold reset that lands with an access outstanding) and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` (it excludes the watchdog control registers from its reset leg). Only the new cells are graded here, and the open owners keep the rest. The new cells are: the phase of the `rst_ni` release against `clk_i`, the release of the watchdog timer-domain reset against `clk_wdt_i`, and the reset values of three armed watchdog and wakeup control registers (`WDOG_CTRL`, `WKUP_CTRL`, `INTR_STATE`). The other four armed registers (`WDOG_REGWEN`, the two watchdog thresholds and `WKUP_THOLD_HI`) keep the timer from firing and are read after the reset as the logged control `CTL-COLD-WDT`; `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` grades their cold default. Two passes: the control pass releases `rst_ni` just after a `clk_i` rising edge, and the graded pass asserts and releases it inside the drawn third of the `clk_i` period, with the release inside the same third of the `clk_wdt_i` period. Functional coverage: `SEP_FCOV` Phase 2 coverpoints `cp_source_edge` (the `rst_ni` cells), `cp_rst_release_sync`, `cp_aon_release_sync` and `cp_domain_crossing` (the watchdog-window cell). The control pass runs with the graded window closed, so the `rst_ni` cells of `cp_source_edge` and the thirds of `cp_rst_release_sync` come from the graded pass only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw all values in the order above and log them. Start the `clk_wdt_i` clock at the drawn start phase.
. Boot with the skip-sense plusarg and `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1. Wait for the `sep_reset_n` tap and the `aon_rst_n` tap to read 1 (bounded waits; a timeout fails the leaf). Run steps 3 to 7 two times: the control pass, then the graded pass.
. Arm non-default values in this order: `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` = 0x0FFF_0000 and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` = 0x0FFF_1000 (both far above the count, which restarts at 0 with each reset), `WKUP_THOLD_HI` = 0x0FFF_0000, `WKUP_CTRL` with `enable` = 1 and prescaler 5, `WDOG_CTRL.enable` = 1, `INTR_STATE.wdog_timer_bark` = 1 through `INTR_TEST`, then `WDOG_REGWEN.regwen` = 0. No bark, bite or wake request occurs before the reset. Read each register back.
. Wait for a `clk_i` rising edge, then wait the assert phase of the pass (pa_0 or pa_j) of one `clk_i` period and drive `rst_ni` low. Record the time of the `rst_ni` fall and the time of the fall of the `sep_reset_n` tap, and log them as `OBS-RST-ASSERT`. Before the fall the tap reads 1.
. Hold `rst_ni` low for Wc `clk_i` cycles.
. Release `rst_ni`: in the control pass at 10 percent of a `clk_i` period after a `clk_i` rising edge, in the graded pass at the phase pr_j, in the `clk_i` cycle that puts the release inside third j of the `clk_wdt_i` period (error injection at the `rst_ni` input: the release is not aligned to `clk_i`. Expected response: the `sep_reset_n` tap rises at a `clk_i` rising edge after the release). Record the time of the `rst_ni` rise, the time of the rise of the `sep_reset_n` tap, the time of the rise of the `aon_rst_n` tap and the `clk_wdt_i` phase.
. Wait for the `sep_reset_n` tap and the `aon_rst_n` tap to read 1 (bounded waits; a timeout of the `sep_reset_n` wait fails the leaf, and a timeout of the `aon_rst_n` wait fails CHK-AON-REL). The specification gives no depth for the synchronizer of the timer-domain reset, so the timer-domain release can come after the `sep_reset_n` release. Read every armed register. Log the four registers that CHK-COLD-DEFAULT does not grade as `CTL-COLD-WDT: pass=<ctl|graded> regwen=<v> bark=<v> bite=<v> wkup_hi=<v>`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ARM
|Each armed register reads back its non-default value before the reset, so the default compare of CHK-COLD-DEFAULT is a real change. A register that does not take its armed value is a FAIL. Anchor: the real LSU readback of the seven registers in each pass. Evidence: `CHK-ARM PASS: <reg>=<val> default=<val0>` for each register.
|CHK-RST-SYNC
|In every pass the `sep_reset_n` tap rises on a `clk_i` rising edge after the `rst_ni` release (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: releases synchronously to `clk_i`). The offset to the edge is at most 1 ps, an environment choice that covers the simulator resolution. In the graded pass the release lands inside the drawn third of the period, away from the edge, so a release that is not synchronized rises off the edge and FAILs. Anchor: the `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` tap against `rst_ni` and `clk_i`. Control: the control pass releases at a fixed small offset after an edge. Evidence: `CHK-RST-SYNC PASS: pass=<ctl\|graded> rst_ni rose t=<ps> phase=<pct> sep_reset_n rose t=<ps> offset_to_clk_edge=<ps>`.
|CHK-AON-REL
|The `aon_rst_n` tap rises on a `clk_wdt_i` rising edge, within the offset of 1 ps (`hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`, Clock, Reset, and Debug Behavior; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture). A release that is not aligned to `clk_wdt_i` is a FAIL. Anchor: the `aon_rst_n` probe against `clk_wdt_i`. Control: the tap is low in each `rst_ni` low window and rises at every release of the leaf. Evidence: `CHK-AON-REL PASS: aon_rst_n rose t=<ps> offset_to_clk_wdt_edge=<ps> wdt_phase=<ps>`.
|CHK-COLD-DEFAULT
|After the cold reset `WDOG_CTRL` = 0, `WKUP_CTRL` = 0 and `INTR_STATE` = 0 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aon_timer/regs/gen/adoc/aon_timer.adoc`, Reset column). A register that keeps its armed value is a FAIL. The cold default of `WDOG_REGWEN.regwen`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` and `WKUP_THOLD_HI` is not graded here: `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` owns it, and this leaf logs the values as `CTL-COLD-WDT`. Anchor: the real LSU readback in each pass. Control: CHK-ARM shows that each register held a non-default value before the reset. Evidence: `CHK-COLD-DEFAULT PASS: <reg>=<val> armed=<val0>` for each of the three registers.
|===

`OBS-RST-ASSERT` is an observation and not a checker. It records, for each pass, the delay from the `rst_ni` fall to the `sep_reset_n` fall and the count of `clk_i` rising edges between them. The specification gives no assertion timing for `sep_intermediate_reset_n`, so no value of the line is a FAIL. The line is `OBS-RST-ASSERT: pass=<ctl\|graded> third=<1\|2\|3> rst_ni fell t=<ps> sep_reset_n fell t=<ps> delta=<ps> clk_edges_between=<n>`.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: A reset shorter than one `clk_i` period and a stopped clock are not driven. The assertion timing of `sep_reset_n` after the `rst_ni` fall is not claimed. The recovery after a reset that lands with an access outstanding belongs to `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test`. The cold default of the other watchdog and wakeup registers is not graded here, and the cold default of `WDOG_REGWEN`, the two watchdog thresholds and `WKUP_THOLD_HI` is graded in `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_reset_retention_test]]
===== 5.15 `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1, so `rst_ni` is the only open term. One testlist entry on `lsu_stub_all_live`, 4 seeds, which gives two leaves. The Verilator leaf (`lsu_stub_all_live`) runs the `wdt_rst_ni` sequence only. The VCS leaf (`--target default`: the full VeeR core held off, the LSU force-splice, no firmware) adds the `dbg_rstb_i` sequence and CHK-DBG-SYNC; the leaf selects these legs from the simulator. The Verilator stub has no `dbg_rstb_i` path to the observed taps and no run-request synchronizer.

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` (the LSU register path); register model from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta`) for the RDL reset values; the Secure DMA copy model (a source snapshot compared with the destination). Approved probes and drives: `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `rst_ni`, `mpc_reset_run_req` (SEP reset probes and TB inputs of `tb_top.sv`); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (IRQ aggregate probe); `scratch_cold_probe_o` (scratch probe); `ip_rst_n[1]`, `ip_rst_n[2]`, `ip_rst_n[3]`, `ip_rst_n[4]` and `ip_rst_n[6]` (crypto reset-sequencer probes); `iso_ack` of the KMAC, Adams Bridge and HMAC host and key paths (crypto reset-sequencer probes). `iso_ack` of the OTBN and AES paths (bits of the isolate-complete vector) and `mpc_run_sync` (the output of the run-request synchronizer) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, one draw per line in the log. Dimensions: event count (2 to 6) for each sequence; the WDT sequence (WDT_PULSE events only) runs on both leaves, and the DBG sequence (DBG_PULSE events only) runs on the VCS leaf; pulse width (2 to 32 `clk_i` cycles) and spacing (0 to 64 cycles) per event; overlap domain D in {OTBN, AES, KMAC, Adams Bridge} (D is not a coverage axis); the order of the overlap runs and the offset inside each band as a fraction in [0,1) (the overlap leg walks every pulse class, WDT_PULSE and, on the VCS leaf, DBG_PULSE, against every position band, before the write, between the write and the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall, and after the fall); the order of the two DMA modes (the DMA leg runs IDLE and RUNNING in every leaf); copy delay as a fraction `f_c` in [0,1); the latched peripheral block L in {KMAC, OTBN} with L different from D (the specification does not state which reset clears the bridge error bit of a block); the armed register values (distinct and non-default). The isolation latency Tiso (step 2a) and the clean copy time C (step 2b) are measured after the draw, so the offset and the copy delay are drawn as fractions and scaled in the step that places the stimulus (step 7 and step 8); the log shows each draw and its scaled clock count. With `f_o` the fraction of the offset: band before the write, 1 + floor(`f_o` times Tiso) clocks before the write handshake; band between, floor(`f_o` times Tiso) clocks after the write handshake; band after the fall, floor(`f_o` times Tiso) clocks after the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall. The copy delay is floor(C/4) + floor(`f_c` times (C/2)) clocks after GO, inside the middle half of C, so that the copy has started to write and has not ended. No `rst_ni` draw except the cold-reset leg, which always lands in the middle of a copy. The seed never selects which pulse class, band or DMA mode runs. `reseed = 4` samples different event sequences, widths, offsets, delays, domains D, orders and data.

*Objective*: A `wdt_rst_ni` or `dbg_rstb_i` pulse leaves the software reset requests, the engine state, the DMA and peripheral fault status, the SPI and DMA registers and a running DMA transfer intact. The two fault latches keep their aggregator sources pending after the pulse with no new fault. A pulse before, inside or after a pending `SW_RESET_N` sequence leaves the sequence intact: the domain isolates, resets and returns released after the write of 1. A cold reset clears all of them, also in the middle of a DMA transfer. The CPU reset follows the `wdt_rst_ni` pulse. The check that a `dbg_rstb_i` pulse leaves both reset taps high is CHK-DBG-RST of `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`, whose `dmactive` readback shows that the pulse reached the debug module. This leaf logs both taps across its `dbg_rstb_i` pulses as `OBS-CPU-RST-DBG`, because a tap that stays high is also the result of a pulse that never arrives. The retention reads after a `dbg_rstb_i` pulse are graded on the VCS leaf: CHK-DBG-SYNC, through the `mpc_run_sync` tap, shows that the `dbg_rstb_i` pin reaches the debug domain. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc` (Reset Architecture); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`, `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`, `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller, Isolation and Reset Sequencing); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map, sources 41 and 43); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/gen/adoc/secure_dma.adoc` (reset values).
