*Test Coverage*: Extends the open tests `sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test` (a `wdt_rst_ni` pulse drops `sep_cpu_reset_n` and leaves `sep_reset_n` high, with the `rst_ni` leg as the isolation anchor), `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test` (the same stimulus with weaker checks, no `dbg_rstb_i` claim), `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test` (the clear and retain of both scratch banks) and `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test` (the cold reset class only). Only the new cells are graded here, and the open owners keep the rest. `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` grades the watchdog registers across every reset event, and `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test` grades the reset taps across a `dbg_rstb_i` pulse. The new cells are: the `dbg_rstb_i` sequence on the VCS leaf, the retention of the listed registers, the two fault latches and a running DMA copy across a CPU-only reset, the overlap of a pulse with a pending `SW_RESET_N` sequence, and the cold reset in the middle of a DMA copy. The `wdt_rst_ni` cells of the CPU reset and both scratch banks are controls. Cells: pulse class (WDT_PULSE, DBG_PULSE) against the armed state; pulse position against a pending sequence of D in {OTBN, AES, KMAC, Adams Bridge}; DMA mode IDLE and RUNNING for a CPU reset, both in every leaf; the cold reset class in the middle of a copy with both latches set; the recovery copy after the cold reset. Functional coverage: `SEP_FCOV` Phase 2 coverpoints `cp_source_edge` (the `wdt_rst_ni` and `dbg_rstb_i` cells), `cp_mpc_sync_reset`, `cp_power_up_value` (the `hmac` field and the aggregate), `cp_cpu_only_reset_scope`, `cp_reset_class`, `cp_pulse_vs_sequence`, `cp_dma_reset_value`, `cp_dma_nonzero_reset` and `cp_dma_failsecure` (the cells of the registers that the cold-reset leg moves away from their reset values; CHK-DMA-COLDRST grades that leg, so the graded window is open across it), `cp_dma_lock_recovery`, `cp_latch_x_reset_kind` and `cp_boot_consequence`, and the Phase 1 group `sep_scratch_reset_cg`. The scratch bank reads (`CTL-BANK`) are a liveness control and fill no Phase 3 bin. The fall of the aggregator bits after the clear writes is the control of CHK-AGG-SURVIVE and fills no Phase 3 bin. The graded window opens at the arming step (step 3). The `wdt_rst_ni` cells of `cp_source_edge` and the watchdog reset class of `cp_reset_class` come from the WDT_PULSE events of steps 5, 7 and 8, and the `dbg_rstb_i` cells of `cp_source_edge` from the DBG_PULSE events of the VCS leaf. The per-IP software reset class comes only from the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall of an overlap run, so the HMAC hold of step 3 fills no class cell. The power-on class comes from the cold-reset leg.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw all values in the order above and log them. Boot with the skip-sense plusarg and `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1. Wait for both reset taps to read 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Hold `mpc_reset_run_req` at 0 (a stimulus choice that keeps the core from running boot code; no check depends on its effect). Start the tap sampler: on every `clk_i` cycle it records the `sep_reset_n` tap, the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap and, on the VCS leaf, the `mpc_run_sync` tap.
. Calibration, before any armed state. Placement values only, never expected values. (a) Write `SW_RESET_N` with the bit of D at 0 and the other bits at the reset value 0x7E. Count the `clk_i` cycles from the address handshake of the write to the fall of `ip_rst_n[D]` (Tiso), and note the cycle at which the host-path isolate-complete bit of D rises. Write the bit to 1 and wait for `ip_rst_n[D]` to read 1 (a bounded wait). (b) Before any write to `RANGE_REGWEN`, program `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE` and `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT` for a window of the SEP SRAM and write `RANGE_VALID` = 1 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/secure_dma.rdl`: a transfer with no configured range ends in `ERROR_CODE.RANGE_VALID_ERROR`). Program a Secure DMA memory-to-memory copy in that window, with a size chosen so that C exceeds 8 times the widest drawn pulse. Write GO and poll `STATUS`. C is the count of `clk_i` cycles from the GO write to the first read with `DONE` = 1; `ERROR` reads 0 at that read, or the leaf fails. Write 1 to `STATUS.DONE` to clear it.
. Arm the state. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x76 (HMAC held) and wait for `ip_rst_n[3]` to read 0 and the HMAC isolate-complete bits to read 1 (a bounded wait). Write one probe register each in OTBN, AES, KMAC, CSRNG and EDN (the registers that `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test` uses; a shadowed register takes two equal writes), and for D = Adams Bridge the Adams Bridge probe `notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r`. Write SPI `CONFIGOPTS` and DMA `SRC_ADDR_LO`. Program `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE`, `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT` and `RANGE_VALID` = 1 for the copy window, then write `RANGE_REGWEN` = 0. Read `RANGE_REGWEN` back as the cleared value Lk. The field is RW0C with reset value 0x6, so the write of 0 clears every set bit and Lk reads 0x0, or the leaf fails. The specification does not state that the value 0x0 applies the lock (only the False encoding 0x9 does, and no write reaches it from 0x6; <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs>>, DMA RANGE_REGWEN false value), so no step relies on the lock and no check grades a lock effect. The leaf grades the stored value Lk only. Write both scratch banks with non-zero values. Read back every armed register: it holds its armed value and differs from its RDL reset value.
. Error injection: latch both fault latches. Access a misaligned or an unmapped Secure DMA register offset: expected response SLVERR, and `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.reg_path_err` reads 1. Access a hole offset inside the extent of block L: expected response SLVERR, and the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` bit of L reads 1. Read the aggregator probe `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`: bits 40 and 42 (sources 41 and 43) read 1.
. Apply the events of the WDT sequence in the drawn order. A WDT_PULSE drives `wdt_rst_ni_i` low for the drawn width. A DBG_PULSE (the DBG sequence, VCS leaf) drives `dbg_rstb_i` low for the drawn width and releases it. No step of this leg changes `mpc_reset_run_req`. After each pulse and the drawn spacing, read the aggregator probe bits 40 and 42, then read both scratch banks (log `CTL-BANK`), then write the warm bank again with a non-zero value.
. After the last event read every armed register, `RANGE_REGWEN`, `SW_RESET_N` and the HMAC isolated window (a 4-byte read). Read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` and `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS`. Write 1 to `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR.clr` and to the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` bit of L. Read the aggregator probe bits 40 and 42 and both status registers again. On the VCS leaf, repeat steps 4 to 6 for the DBG sequence. CHK-RETAIN and CHK-AGG-SURVIVE grade its reads.
. Overlap leg, for each pulse class and each position band, in the drawn order (six runs per leaf on the VCS leaf, three on the Verilator leaf). Arm a non-default probe in D and in the sibling (AES, or OTBN for D = AES), and write both scratch banks. Write `SW_RESET_N` with the bit of D at 0 and the other bits at 0x76. Apply the pulse (a DBG_PULSE only on the VCS leaf) with its assertion edge at the drawn offset from the address handshake of that write (the band is placed with Tiso from step 2, and the drawn offset fraction is scaled as the randomization states). Wait for `ip_rst_n[D]` to read 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-OVL) and note the cycle at which the isolate-complete bits of D read 1. When the pulse has ended, write the bit of D to 1 and wait for `ip_rst_n[D]` to read 1 (a bounded wait). Read the probe of D, the probe of the sibling and `SW_RESET_N`. Read both scratch banks (log `CTL-BANK`) and write the warm bank again.
. DMA leg, a CPU reset against a Secure DMA copy. Run this step twice, once in mode RUNNING and once in mode IDLE, in the drawn order. Latch both fault latches again (error injection: the two accesses of step 4 at the same points; expected response SLVERR and the matching latch set). Write 1 to `STATUS.DONE` and `STATUS.CHUNK_DONE` and read both bits as 0. Fill the copy source with seeded non-zero data and the destination with a pattern A that differs from the source on every word. Program the copy in the armed range and read back the programmed registers. Write GO. Mode RUNNING: wait the copy delay of the randomization (log `f_c` and the clock count), then drive `wdt_rst_ni_i` low for the drawn width. The first `STATUS` read after the pulse shows `BUSY` = 1, or the leaf fails (the pulse did not land on a running copy). Mode IDLE: poll `STATUS` until `DONE` = 1, then drive the pulse. Poll `STATUS` until `DONE` = 1 (a bounded wait of a multiple of C; a timeout fails). Read `ERROR`, the programmed registers, `RANGE_REGWEN`, both status registers, and every destination word through the LSU path.
. Cold-reset leg (graded by CHK-DMA-COLDRST, and the control of CHK-RETAIN). Write every armed register again with a non-default value (so each reads non-default before the reset) and write both scratch banks. Write 1 to `STATUS.DONE` and `STATUS.CHUNK_DONE` and read both bits as 0, so a poll for `DONE` cannot return a bit that the copy of step 8 left. Fill the destination with a non-zero pattern B that differs from the source on every word and from pattern A. Write `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` = 0 (reset value 0x7FF; the copy does not use the hardware handshake). Program the copy, read back the programmed registers and `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` (each reads a value other than its RDL reset value), and write GO. Wait the same copy delay as in the RUNNING run of step 8 (the scaled clock count), so the reset lands in the middle of the copy. Drive `rst_ni` low for the drawn width (at least the floor of 8 `clk_i` cycles) and release it. Wait for both reset taps to read 1 (a bounded wait).
. After the cold reset: read every destination word (R1). Read every readable Secure DMA register, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`, `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS`, `SW_RESET_N`, every armed register and both scratch banks (log `CTL-BANK`). Wait the measured copy time C. Read every destination word again (R2) and compare it with R1. Count the words of R1 that equal their source word and the words of R1 that equal pattern B.
. Recovery copy. Refill the source with seeded non-zero data and the destination with pattern A, so that the copy does not rely on memory content across the reset. Program the range registers (`RANGE_REGWEN` reads 0x6 after the reset) and program a copy. Write 1 to `STATUS.DONE` and `STATUS.CHUNK_DONE` and read both bits as 0. Write GO and poll `STATUS` until `DONE` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read `ERROR` and compare the destination with the source.
. Run-request synchronizer leg (VCS leaf). It runs after the recovery copy, so no retention read follows it. Control: with `dbg_rstb_i` high toggle `mpc_reset_run_req` 0, 1, 0 with a bounded wait after each toggle, and sample the `mpc_run_sync` tap. Error injection at `mpc_reset_run_req_i`: drive `dbg_rstb_i` low for the drawn width, toggle `mpc_reset_run_req` at least once while it is low, release `dbg_rstb_i`, and toggle the input once more after the release. Expected response: while `dbg_rstb_i` is low the tap does not follow, and after the release it follows within the synchronizer latency plus the allowance of CHK-DBG-SYNC. End with `mpc_reset_run_req` at 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RETAIN
|After the events every armed register reads its armed value, `RANGE_REGWEN` reads Lk, `SW_RESET_N` reads 0x76, and the HMAC isolated window answers a 4-byte read with SLVERR and data `0x1501A7ED` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture: a watchdog restart does not return the engines or their software reset requests to cold defaults; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing). A register that returns to its RDL default is a FAIL. This check grades the WDT sequence and, on the VCS leaf, the DBG sequence. A retained value after a DBG_PULSE can also come from an unconnected pin, so the DBG half takes credit only when CHK-DBG-SYNC passes in the same leaf. Anchor: the real LSU readbacks. Control: the cold-reset leg returns every armed register to its RDL default, so a dead readback cannot pass. Evidence, one line for each sequence: `CHK-RETAIN PASS: event=<WDT\|DBG> regs_retained=<m>/<m> RANGE_REGWEN=0x<Lk> SW_RESET_N=0x76 hmac_iso_rd4=0x1501a7ed cold_leg_defaults=<m>/<m>`.
|CHK-AGG-SURVIVE
|Aggregator probe bits 40 and 42 (sources 41 and 43) stay 1 after each pulse with no new fault, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.reg_path_err` and the `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` bit of L read 1 before the clear, and the probe bits read 0 after the writes to both clear registers (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`, `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, sources 41 and 43). A pulse that clears a latch is a FAIL. This check grades the WDT sequence and, on the VCS leaf, the DBG sequence. A latch that survives a DBG_PULSE can also come from an unconnected pin, so the DBG half takes credit only when CHK-DBG-SYNC passes in the same leaf. Anchor: the `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` probe and the status readbacks. Control: the probe falls after the clear writes, so a stuck-high probe fails. Evidence: `CHK-AGG-SURVIVE PASS: event=<WDT\|DBG> latch=<kmac\|otbn> src41=1 src43=1 new_faults=0 after_clear=0`.
|CHK-DBG-SYNC
|On VCS only: while `dbg_rstb_i` is low the `mpc_run_sync` tap is constant although `mpc_reset_run_req` toggles, and after the release it follows the input within the two-flop synchronizer latency plus an environment allowance of 2 `clk_i` cycles (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU: `mpc_reset_run_req_i` passes a two-flop synchronizer; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). The held value is not graded, because the specification gives no reset value. A tap that follows the input while `dbg_rstb_i` is low, or that never follows after the release, is a FAIL. Anchor: the `mpc_run_sync` probe. Control: outside the low window the tap follows a toggle of the input (step 12). The toggles are error injection: the specification states no result for a change of the request after reset release beyond the synchronizer. Evidence: `CHK-DBG-SYNC PASS: constant_cycles=<n> toggles_in_window=<t> follow_cycles=<k> control_follow=1`.
|CHK-OVL
|For each overlap pulse of D the domain isolates (its host-path isolate-complete bit is low before the write, as the calibration of step 2 shows, and high before the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall; the key-path bits are high already because the Key Manager is held), resets, returns released after the write of 1, and reads its probe at the RDL default (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, steps 1 to 5; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture, CPU reset row). The sibling probe keeps its armed value and `SW_RESET_N` reads 0x76. A pulse that loses the sequence (no `ip_rst_n[D]` fall within the bounded wait), or that resets the sibling, is a FAIL. Anchor: `ip_rst_n[D]`, the isolate-complete bits and the real probe readbacks. Control: the pulse-free run of step 2 shows the same order. A sequence that survives a DBG_PULSE can also come from an unconnected pin, so a run with `pulse=DBG` takes credit only when CHK-DBG-SYNC passes in the same leaf. A pulse on the exact reset cycle is graded for order and outcome, not latency. Evidence: `CHK-OVL PASS: dom=<D> pulse=<WDT\|DBG> band=<b> iso_ack=<c1> rst_assert=<c2> engine_default_after_release=1 sibling_retained=1 pulse_free_ack=<c3> pulse_free_fall=<c4>`.
|CHK-DMA-CPURST
|A CPU reset in the DMA leg leaves the DMA untouched. In mode RUNNING the copy completes after the pulse. In mode IDLE the copy is complete before the pulse and stays complete. At the end `DONE` = 1 and `ERROR` = 0, the destination equal to the source, the programmed registers (address, size, width, config, range base, range limit and `RANGE_VALID`) unchanged, `RANGE_REGWEN` = Lk and both fault latches set (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`: a CPU-only reset does not reset the DMA). A DMA that is reset or aborted by the pulse is a FAIL. Anchor: the real LSU readbacks of the registers and of the destination words, and the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap. In mode RUNNING the first `STATUS` read after the pulse shows `BUSY` = 1. Control: the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap shows the pulse. Evidence, one line for each mode: `CHK-DMA-CPURST PASS: mode=<RUNNING\|IDLE> cpu_rst_pulse=1 busy_after_pulse=<b> done=1 error=0 regs_kept=1 bus_err_status=0x<..>`.
|CHK-DMA-COLDRST
|After a cold reset in the middle of a copy every Secure DMA register that read a value other than its RDL reset value before the reset (the armed registers of step 9 and the copy configuration) equals its RDL reset value, including `RANGE_REGWEN` 0x6 from the cleared value Lk (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/gen/adoc/secure_dma.adoc`, Reset column). `HANDSHAKE_INTR_ENABLE` returns to 0x7FF from the written 0. A readable register that the leaf never moves from its reset value (for example `ADDR_SPACE_ID` 0x77, `CFG_REGWEN` 0x6, or `TRANSFER_WIDTH` 0x2 when the copy uses that width) cannot show a missing reset: it is compared and logged as `OBS-DMA-COLD-UNMOVED` and is not graded. `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` and `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` read 0 and `SW_RESET_N` reads 0x7E (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, Reset column; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, `SW_RESET_N`). Before the reset `SW_RESET_N` reads 0x76, so only the `hmac_sw_rst_n` field moves; the other six fields hold their reset value through the reset and are logged as `OBS-SWRST-COLD-UNMOVED: fields=km,otbn,aes,kmac,trng,abr` and not graded per field. The destination words of the interrupted copy do not change between R1 and R2, which are one measured copy time apart, so a DMA that continues after the reset FAILs. This destination half is graded only when its control holds, because the specification does not state that the SRAM keeps its content across a cold reset. Anchor: the real LSU readbacks of the registers and of the destination words. Control for the destination half: R1 holds at least one word that equals its source word (the copy wrote it before the reset) and at least one word that equals pattern B (the copy had not reached it). The source words and pattern B are non-zero, so a cleared array cannot meet the control. When the control is not met, the leaf logs `OBS-DMA-COLD-MEM: src_match=<n> patB_match=<n>` and grades only the register half. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-COLDRST PASS: delay=<d> regs=<n> unmoved_logged=<m> mismatch=0 bus_err_status=0x00 periph_latch=<kmac\|otbn> periph_status=0x00 SW_RESET_N=0x7e dest_graded=<1\|0> dest_unchanged_after_reset=<1\|na> control_src_words=<a> control_patB_words=<b>`.
|CHK-DMA-COLDRST-RECOVER
|A recovery copy after the cold reset completes with `DONE` = 1 and `ERROR` = 0, and the destination equals the source word for word. A DMA left wedged by the reset fails the bounded wait. Anchor: the real `STATUS` readback and the destination words. Control: the range registers read their reset values before the program step, so the copy is a new one. Evidence: `CHK-DMA-COLDRST-RECOVER PASS: done=1 error=0 mem_mismatch=0`.
|===

`CTL-CPU-RST` is a control and not a checker. The leaf fails as a failed precondition unless at least one `wdt_rst_ni` pulse drops the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap and leaves the `sep_reset_n` tap high on every cycle (`sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test` grades this contract), and the `rst_ni` pulse of the cold-reset leg drops the `sep_reset_n` tap. The two taps are sampled on every `clk_i` cycle. These are the live controls of the retention checks. The line is `CTL-CPU-RST: wdt_events=<n> sep_cpu_reset_n_low_on_wdt=<n> sep_reset_n_high_on_wdt=1 control_cold_sep_reset_n_low=1`. The DBG sequence logs `OBS-CPU-RST-DBG: pulses=<k> sep_cpu_reset_n_low=<n> sep_reset_n_low=<n>` and does not grade it. `CTL-BANK: event=<WDT\|DBG\|COLD> warm=<cleared\|retained> cold=<cleared\|retained>` records the scratch banks after each event. It is a liveness control and not a checker.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The reset value of a `SW_RESET_N` field other than `hmac_sw_rst_n` after the cold reset is not graded here, because the leaf holds those fields at their reset value before the reset. Which reset boundary clears the `sep_cpu_ctrl` registers beyond the items that the specification states (scratch banks, DMA and peripheral status, watchdog, `SECURE_TM`, software reset requests) is not claimed. The scratch clear and retain belong to `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`. The content of the SEP SRAM across a cold reset is not claimed. The read data of a 1-, 2- or 8-byte isolated read is not claimed (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The held value of `mpc_run_sync` is not claimed. The reset taps across a `dbg_rstb_i` pulse are graded in `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`, and the watchdog registers across every reset event in `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`.

'''''

[[sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test]]
===== 5.16 `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, with the Key Manager image that the open Key Manager tests stage, 4 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Leg A (subset and reserved bits) and leg B (isolation matrix) share the `SW_RESET_N` write stimulus and run in one leaf.

*Source Module*: `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` (the LSU register path and the host-path master); register model from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta`) for the RDL reset values. Approved probes: `ip_rst_n[6:1]` (crypto reset-sequencer probes); `iso_ack` of the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge host and key paths and of the three TRNG host paths (crypto reset-sequencer probes). The isolate request and isolate-complete vectors (`iso_req` and `iso_ack` of the OTBN and AES paths, of the five Key Manager key paths and of the Key Manager to eFuse path: one 14-bit request vector and one 14-bit complete vector at the crypto AXI interconnect) and `ip_rst_n[0]` (the Key Manager warm reset) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, one draw per line in the log. Leg A dimensions: the partition of the six engine domains into step subsets S (at least one step with |S| of 2 or more, every domain in exactly one subset), the step order, the write style of a step with |S| of 2 or more (one write, or one write for each domain; at least one such step uses one write), the spacing between writes of different domains (0 to 8 `clk_i` cycles, shorter than the isolation latency so sequences overlap), the reserved data bits 63:7, and the write shape (64-bit lane, 32-bit high half, 32-bit low half with random bits 31:7). Leg B dimensions for each domain: the count of host transactions issued before the `SW_RESET_N` write (0 to 4, with at least one read for OTBN and AES), the delay to that write (0 to 6 cycles), the isolated-window access sizes (1, 2, 4 and 8 bytes), addresses and operations, the hold time (1 to 200 cycles from the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall), and the never-isolated port. Leg B pair dimensions: the victims (D, D2) of each of the two pair passes, two different domains of OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge, and the gap of D2 (0 to 8 cycles). One pair pass releases D first and the other releases D2 first, so both release orders run in every leaf. The TRNG bit is drawn only while no engine runs. A single-victim pass has no second victim. Every domain runs a single-victim pass in every leaf. `reseed = 4` samples different partitions, orders, spacings, reserved bits, access sets and victim pairs.

*Objective*: Leg A. A `SW_RESET_N` field at 0 resets exactly its domain. A field at 1 releases it. Engine state in the other domains stays intact for any subset, order and spacing of writes. Bits 63:7 have no field in the register description, so a write to them changes no field and resets no domain. The register description states no read value for them, so the leaf logs them and does not grade them. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (Software Reset Register, Isolation and Reset Sequencing); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc` (`SW_RESET_N`, bits 63:7 with Access "-"); `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc` (Reset Architecture). Leg B. For each domain a `SW_RESET_N` assert request first isolates the domain paths listed in the specification table. Accepted host transactions drain. Host transactions that arrive after the request complete with SLVERR and read data `0x1501A7ED` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, step 2). The reset asserts only after all paths report isolated. A write of 1 releases reset and isolation together. Sibling domains keep their state. The Key Manager mailbox, the eFuse wrapper host port, the lifecycle controller and the external TRNG ports have no isolate stage (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing): they answer as in the memory map while a domain is isolated. The Key Manager to eFuse path is isolated by the Key Manager domain (table of paths isolated before each software reset). The host keeps the Key Manager idle before a TRNG reset; holding it in reset is sufficient (Resetting the TRNG). Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (Software Reset Register, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, the table of paths isolated before each software reset, Resetting the TRNG); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc` (`SW_RESET_N`); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (external TRNG window).

*Test Coverage*: Extends the open tests `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` (`SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E and the wire of a single-domain pulse), `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` (the order and the drain of the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains, the SLVERR answer of an HMAC read and write in the window, and that a neighbour result survives), `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test` (the SLVERR answer of an `MLKEM_STATUS` read while the Adams Bridge domain is held), `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` (the three TRNG paths: order, drain, and the SLVERR answer of each port), `sep_km_isolate_termination_test` (a Key Manager store during isolation) and `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` (the Key Manager restart). Only the new cells are graded here, and the open owners keep the rest. The new cells are: a multi-domain write, the reserved bits of `SW_RESET_N`, the read data `0x1501A7ED` of a 4-byte read in the isolated window for all six domains, the SLVERR answer of the size sweep for OTBN, AES, KMAC and Adams Bridge, and the order and drain of the OTBN, AES and Key Manager domains. The single-domain steps (|S| = 1), the single-domain wire, the SLVERR answer of HMAC and TRNG accesses and of an Adams Bridge `MLKEM_STATUS` read (logged as `CTL-ISO-RESP`), and the order and drain of HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and TRNG are controls. Cells: leg A subsets and the reserved-only leg; leg B for seven domains (OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge, TRNG, Key Manager; the Key Manager has no host path, so its pass covers order and paths only), in-flight traffic, isolated-window reads of 1, 2, 4 and 8 bytes and writes, two victim pairs with both release orders in every leaf, the probes of every other engine domain after each single-victim pass, the never-isolated ports and the exact set of isolation requests. Functional coverage: `SEP_FCOV` Phase 2 coverpoints `cp_field_transition`, `cp_multi_field_write`, `cp_reserved_field_write`, `cp_no_sw_reset_units`, `cp_independence_pair` (the leg A steps with |S| of 2 or more; seed-dependent), `cp_bank_class_outcome` (the per-IP software reset cells; the scratch bank registers are unrelated registers of CHK-SUBSET), `cp_km_reset_condition`, `cp_trng_iso_order` (the release cell), `cp_iso_order_per_domain` (the KMAC and TRNG release cells, and the OTBN, AES and Key Manager cells), `cp_never_isolated_port` and `cp_trng_nonmember`. The reset-fall cells of KMAC and TRNG are `CTL-ISO-ORDER` controls and fill no Phase 3 bin.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw all values in the order above and log them. Boot with the skip-sense plusarg. Read `SW_RESET_N` as the first access (a control: the value 0x7E is graded by the open owner, and the read fills no Phase 3 bin). The Key Manager stays held (`km_sw_rst_n` = 0) through leg A, so the TRNG precondition holds for every TRNG step.
. Leg A, arm. Write one non-default probe register in each engine domain: OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM`, AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, HMAC `INTR_ENABLE`, KMAC `ENTROPY_PERIOD`, TRNG (CSRNG `INTR_ENABLE`) and Adams Bridge `notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r` (a shadowed register takes two equal writes). Write the unrelated registers: SPI `CONFIGOPTS`, DMA `SRC_ADDR_LO`, a word of the mailbox inbound window and one register in each scratch bank. The watchdog registers across a software reset are graded in `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`. Read each back: it holds its armed value and differs from its RDL reset value.
. Leg A, reserved-only leg. Read `SW_RESET_N` as a 64-bit access. Error injection: write bits 63:7 with random non-zero data and bits 6:0 equal to the value just read, in each of the three shapes (64-bit lane, 32-bit high half, 32-bit low half with random bits 31:7), all with full-lane strobes. Expected response: a response arrives (its code is not stated), no effect, and a 64-bit read returns bits 6:0 at the value just read with OKAY. Bits 63:7 of the read are logged (`OBS-SWRST-RSVD`). Sample the `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps on every cycle of the leg.
. Leg A, subset steps, in the drawn order. For a step of subset S: write the field of each domain of S to 0 (one write or one write for each domain at the drawn spacing), with random reserved bits and the other fields at their current value. After every write read `SW_RESET_N` as a 64-bit access (CHK-SWRST-RSVD). Wait for the `ip_rst_n` taps of S to read 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails). Write the fields of S to 1 in the same style and wait for the taps to read 1 (a bounded wait). The release is written only after the taps of S are low, because an early release has no stated result. Read the probe of every engine domain and every unrelated register. Re-arm the probes of S.
. Leg B, Key Manager state. The Key Manager boot helper of the open Key Manager tests releases the Key Manager (`km_sw_rst_n` = 1) before the passes of OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and Key Manager. Wait until the Key Manager key-path isolate-complete bits of HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge read 0 and `ip_rst_n[0]` reads 1 (a bounded wait). This state is the baseline: every isolate request reads 0. Before the TRNG pass write `km_sw_rst_n` = 0 and wait until those key-path bits read 1 and `ip_rst_n[0]` reads 0. This is the TRNG baseline. After the TRNG pass write `km_sw_rst_n` = 1 again. The passes run in the order OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge, the two pair passes, Key Manager, TRNG. A single-victim pass has no D2; a pair pass uses its drawn pair (D, D2).
. Leg B, pass for domain D other than the Key Manager. Arm the probe of D and, with a second victim, the probe of D2 (for TRNG one register each in ESRC, CSRNG and EDN) and read them: each returns its armed value with OKAY (control: the path is live).
. Issue the drawn count of host transactions on the host path of D, single-beat and accepted by the IP, without waiting for their responses. For OTBN and AES the host master holds RREADY low from the issue of the first transaction, so at least one read response is outstanding when the request rises for every drawn count and delay. BREADY stays high, so the B beat of the `SW_RESET_N` write, which the same master issues, and of every host write completes. The master sets RREADY high when the isolate request of D reads 1. The leaf logs `inflight_at_iso_req`. Write `SW_RESET_N` with the field of D at 0 after the drawn delay. With a second victim, write the field of D2 at 0 at the drawn gap after the field of D.
. Wait for every accepted transaction to complete with a response (a bounded wait; a timeout fails). The response code of a drained transaction is not stated and is not scored. Wait for `ip_rst_n[D]` and, with a second victim, `ip_rst_n[D2]` to read 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails), because a release written before the fall has no stated result. Note the cycle at which every isolate-complete bit of D reads 1.
. In the window with `ip_rst_n[D]` low, issue the drawn reads (sizes 1, 2, 4 and 8 bytes) and writes at drawn decoded addresses of D (for TRNG one ESRC, one CSRNG and one EDN register). Error injection: each access targets an isolated host path. Expected response: SLVERR, and read data `0x1501A7ED` for a 4-byte read. Write a drawn value that differs from the RDL reset value. In the same window read the probe of a sibling domain outside {D, D2} whose probe is armed (it returns OKAY and its armed value) and the drawn never-isolated port: the external TRNG window, the Key Manager mailbox (not for D = TRNG, where the Key Manager is held), the eFuse wrapper host port (a mapped register from the generated memory map), the lifecycle controller, and for D = TRNG the entropy pool `STATUS`.
. Hold for the drawn hold time counted from the `ip_rst_n[D]` fall. Write `SW_RESET_N` with the field of D at 1 (a pair releases in the order of its pair pass, at the drawn gap). The write of 1 never comes before the fall. Wait for `ip_rst_n[D]` (and `ip_rst_n[D2]`) to read 1 (a bounded wait). Note the cycles of the `ip_rst_n[D]` rise and of the fall of the isolate-complete bits of D. Read the probe of D: it returns its RDL default with OKAY, and the written value of step 9 is absent. In a single-victim pass, read the probe of every other engine domain: each returns its armed value with OKAY, or the leaf fails (`CTL-INDEP: dom=<D> others_retained=<k>/<k>`, a control that fills no Phase 3 bin). Write the armed value again to the probe of D and of D2 and read both back, so that every probe is armed for the next pass.
. Leg B, pass for the Key Manager. With the Key Manager released and idle, write `km_sw_rst_n` = 0. Sample the isolate request vector, then `ip_rst_n[0]`, then the isolate-complete vector. Hold, then write `km_sw_rst_n` = 1 and wait for `ip_rst_n[0]` to read 1 (a bounded wait). Note the cycles of the `ip_rst_n[0]` rise and of the fall of the isolate-complete bits of the Key Manager set (the five Key Manager key paths and the Key Manager to eFuse path; every engine domain is released in this pass, so each key path has no other endpoint in reset) for CHK-ISO-REL.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SUBSET
|For each step with \|S\| of 2 or more the domains of S read their RDL default after release, and the other domains and the unrelated registers keep their armed value (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Software Reset Register; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture). Each domain of S holds a non-default value before the reset, so the reset is visible. A domain outside S that resets, or a domain of S that keeps its value, is a FAIL. Anchor: the real LSU readbacks and the `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps. Control: the steps with \|S\| = 1 log `CTL-SUBSET`. Evidence: `CHK-SUBSET PASS: step=<i> S=0b<..> probes_reset=<m>/<m> others_retained=<k>/<k>`; `CTL-SUBSET: step=<i> S=0b<..> probes_reset=<m>/<m> others_retained=<k>/<k>`.
|CHK-SUBSET-COVER
|The partition asserts every one of the six engine domains at least once, so a domain that no step writes cannot hide. Anchor: the `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps, one fall for each domain. Evidence: `CHK-SUBSET-COVER PASS: all six domains asserted at least once steps=<K>`.
|CHK-SWRST-RSVD
|After every write with bits 63:7 set, in the reserved-only leg and in each subset step, a response arrives, and the 64-bit readback answers OKAY with bits 6:0 equal to the written field values (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, `SW_RESET_N`, bits 63:7 with Access "-"). The expected field values come from the writes of the leaf. The register description states no response code for a write to the reserved bits, so only the arrival of the response is scored for the write, and OKAY is scored for the readback only. It states no read value for bits 63:7, so the leaf logs them (`OBS-SWRST-RSVD`) and does not grade them. A readback whose bits 6:0 differ from the written field values, or a write with no response, is a FAIL: a DUT that lets reserved write data reach a field, or that drops the field part of a write with reserved bits set, fails. Anchor: the real 64-bit readback. Control: the subset writes set the fields of S to 0, so the written field bits differ from those of the reserved-only leg, and a readback that does not follow the written fields (for example one that returns the reset value 0x7E) fails. Evidence: `CHK-SWRST-RSVD PASS: leg=<rsvd\|subset> n=<n> writes responses=<n> hi=0x<..> written_fields=0b<..> readback_fields=0b<..>` and `OBS-SWRST-RSVD: leg=<rsvd\|subset> bits63:7=0x<..>`.
|CHK-SWRST-SCOPE
|The `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps stay stable on every cycle of the reserved-only leg and the unrelated registers stay unchanged, so a write to bits 63:7 resets nothing. Anchor: the `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps and the readbacks of the unrelated registers. Control: a real reset in the same leaf (the first subset step) moves a tap; a tap that never moves fails the leaf. Evidence: `CHK-SWRST-SCOPE PASS: taps stable 0b<..> unrelated_regs_unchanged=<k>/<k> control tap moved fall=<c> rise=<c>`.
|CHK-ISO-RESP
|In the isolated window of every domain with a host path (OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge, TRNG) every 4-byte read returns data `0x1501A7ED`. For OTBN, AES, KMAC and Adams Bridge every read and write of the size sweep also completes SLVERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, step 2). The SLVERR answer of every HMAC and TRNG access and of an Adams Bridge `MLKEM_STATUS` read is graded by the open owners named in Test Coverage. This leaf logs it as `CTL-ISO-RESP` and does not score it. The read data of 1-, 2- and 8-byte reads is logged and not scored (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A written value is absent after the release. A graded access that answers OKAY, or a 4-byte read with other data, is a FAIL. Anchor: the real master responses and readbacks. Control: the same path answers OKAY before the request and after the release, and the `CTL-ISO-RESP` lines show the answers that the open owners grade in the same window. Evidence: `CHK-ISO-RESP PASS: dom=<D> rd4=<k> rd4_data=0x1501a7ed graded_slverr=<k> other_size_data=<list> control_okay=1`, with `graded_slverr=0` for HMAC and TRNG; `CTL-ISO-RESP: dom=<D> reads=<k> writes=<k> slverr=<k>`.
|CHK-ISO-REL
|For D in OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge, TRNG and Key Manager the isolate-complete bits of D fall within the environment allowance of the `ip_rst_n[D]` rise: reset and isolation release together (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, step 5). The isolate-complete bits of the Key Manager are its five key paths and the Key Manager to eFuse path, and its tap is `ip_rst_n[0]`. The allowance is a test parameter set at build and printed in the log. For D other than the Key Manager, after the release the probe reads its RDL default with OKAY. An isolation that outlasts the reset is a FAIL. Anchor: the isolate-complete probes and the isolate-complete vector against the `ip_rst_n[D]` tap, and the real probe readback. Control: the path answers again after the release; for the Key Manager, which has no host path, the same bits rise before the `ip_rst_n[0]` fall of the same pass (CHK-ISO-ORDER), so the bits are seen to move in both directions. Evidence: `CHK-ISO-REL PASS: dom=<D> rst_rise=<c> ack_fall=<c> abs_delta=<c> allow=<c>`.
|CHK-ISO-NEVER
|Each never-isolated port answers as in the memory map, not with the isolated answer (SLVERR with data `0x1501A7ED`), in the same isolated window as the SLVERR reads of CHK-ISO-RESP (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`, external TRNG window). The Key Manager mailbox, the eFuse wrapper port, the lifecycle controller and the entropy pool `STATUS` return OKAY. The external TRNG window completes with any response except the isolated one. A port that returns the isolated answer is a FAIL. Anchor: the real master response and read data. Control: CHK-ISO-RESP in the same window shows an isolated port answering SLVERR. Evidence: `CHK-ISO-NEVER PASS: dom=<D> port=<ext_trng\|km_mbox\|efuse\|lifecycle\|pool> resp=<r> rdata=0x<d> not_isolated_answer=1`.
|CHK-ISO-PAIR
|In each pair pass, with both victims from OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge, each victim follows its own sequence: its host-path isolate-complete bits are high before its `ip_rst_n` tap falls, its tap rises only after its own write of 1, and its isolate-complete bits fall within the allowance of CHK-ISO-REL after that rise (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, steps 1 to 5). The specification states these steps for one domain and gives no order or timing between two domains, so the order of the two tap edges is logged as `OBS-ISO-PAIR-ORDER` and is not graded. Sibling probes stay readable with their armed value. A second victim that does not isolate, or a victim that does not release after its own write of 1, is a FAIL. Anchor: the isolate-complete bits and the `ip_rst_n` taps of both victims against the time of each write, and the sibling readback. Control: the single-victim passes of the same leaf. Evidence, one line for each pair pass: `CHK-ISO-PAIR PASS: victims=<D,D2> both_isolated=1 both_released=1 write_order=<fwd\|rev>`.
|CHK-ISO-ORDER
|For D in OTBN, AES and Key Manager the `ip_rst_n[D]` tap falls at or after the cycle all isolate-complete bits of D are high (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, step 4). A reset that asserts before an isolate-complete bit is a FAIL. Anchor: the isolate-complete vector and `ip_rst_n[0]` (Key Manager), `ip_rst_n[1]` and `ip_rst_n[2]` (OTBN, AES). Control: the path answers OKAY before the request. HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and TRNG log the same measurement as `CTL-ISO-ORDER: dom=<D> ack_cycle=<a> rst_assert_cycle=<r>` with the approved probes. Evidence: `CHK-ISO-ORDER PASS: dom=<D> ack_cycle=<a> rst_assert_cycle=<r> r-a=<d>`.
|CHK-ISO-DRAIN
|For D in OTBN and AES at least one accepted transaction has no response at the cycle the isolate request rises, and every accepted transaction completes with a response before the reset asserts (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing, steps 2 to 4). The response code of a drained transaction is not stated and is not scored. A reset that asserts with a transaction still open is a FAIL. The host master holds RREADY low until the request rises (step 7), so a leg with no read in flight at the request is a failed precondition and fails the leaf. Anchor: the isolate request vector and the master VIP response log. Control: HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and TRNG log `CTL-ISO-DRAIN` below. Evidence: `CHK-ISO-DRAIN PASS: dom=<OTBN\|AES> accepted=<n> completed=<n> inflight_at_iso_req=<k>`. The control line is `CTL-ISO-DRAIN: dom=<D> accepted=<n> completed=<n>`.
|CHK-ISO-PATHS
|From the baseline (all isolate requests 0 with the Key Manager released; for D = TRNG the Key Manager taps high with the Key Manager held), the requests that rise equal the table set for the victims written, and no other request rises (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, the table of paths isolated before each software reset). A key path isolates when either endpoint is in reset. For D = TRNG the Key Manager set (bench indices 8 to 13: the five key paths and the Key Manager to eFuse path) is already high in the baseline and does not count as rising. An extra or a missing request is a FAIL. Anchor: the isolate request vector. Control: before the write the request vector equals the baseline: all 0, or for D = TRNG bits 8 to 13 at 1 and all other bits at 0. Evidence: `CHK-ISO-PATHS PASS: dom=<D> isolated=<set> others_low=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: Byte-lane partial strobes on `SW_RESET_N` have no stated result and are not driven. The `SOFT_RST_CODE` isolation bypass and a Key Manager reset in the middle of a command or a key transfer belong to the Key Manager area. The lane and size placement of the isolated-read pattern for 1-, 2- and 8-byte reads is not claimed (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).

'''''

[[sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test]]
===== 5.17 `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense (no skip plusarg, so `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` can fall and rise), `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive` (<<sep-vplan-phase3-edn-waiver>>), 4 seeds, Verilator and VCS.

*Source Module*: `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` (the LSU register path); `SepIcResetTdr` (`env/sep_ic_reset_tdr.py`, the IC_RESET TDR driver on the `jtag_ic_reset_*` inputs); register model from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta`) for the RDL reset values. Approved probes and drives: the `jtag_ic_reset_*` inputs, `ip_rst_n[6:1]`, `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `rst_ni` (TB inputs of `tb_top.sv` and the crypto reset-sequencer probes); the host-path `iso_ack` of the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge paths and of the three TRNG paths (crypto reset-sequencer probes). `iso_ack` of the OTBN and AES paths (bits of the isolate-complete vector) and `aon_rst_n` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, one draw per line in the log. Dimensions: the override enable vector E and the value vector V over the six engine domains (walking-one and walking-zero over E, each with V all 0 and with V all 1, then 8 random (E, V) pairs); the hold time of each pair (at least the settle time of 4 `clk_i` cycles); the order of the priority runs (every leaf runs each of OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC in both release orders, software bit first and override first); the `sep_reset_n` override width (at least 8 `clk_i` cycles); the event count (1 to 3); the armed values (distinct and non-default). `reseed = 4` samples different random pairs, hold times, orders, widths and armed values.

*Objective*: For OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, TRNG and Adams Bridge the per-IP JTAG override pair forces the domain reset (value 0) or forces it released (value 1), whatever `SW_RESET_N` holds. It does not run the isolation sequence. The domain returns when the override releases. With `rst_ni` high, a JTAG override that forces `sep_reset_n` low resets every `sep_reset_n`-domain block and every sequenced engine domain (each is ANDed with `sep_reset_n`). It leaves the `rst_ni`-domain blocks intact: this leaf shows it through `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, and `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` grades the watchdog registers across the override. The pairs are driven through the IC_RESET TDR. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller, Isolation and Reset Sequencing); `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc` (Reset Architecture); `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` (Token Activation); `doc/integrator/src/smu-composition.adoc` (SEP slice, TDI to TDO order); `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc` (IC_RESET Support: port n at TDR bits 2n+1 `reset_enable`, active low, and 2n+2 `reset_control`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends the open tests `sep_sec_dis_override_test` (the `sep_reset_n` override on the bench pins), `sep_sec_dis_reset_reach_test` (the `sep_reset_n` pair through the IC_RESET TDR), `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test` (the Key Manager pair through the TDR), `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` (`CHK-ABR-JTAG-OVERRIDE`) and `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test` (`CHK-TRNG-JTAG`). Only the new cells are graded here, and the open owners keep the rest. The new cells are: the OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC override, the value 1 (force released) of any engine pair, and the effect of a `sep_reset_n` override on the engine domains and the `sep_reset_n` domain. The watchdog registers (`WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`) across the override are graded in `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` and are not armed here. The cells of the Adams Bridge and TRNG pairs with value 0 are controls. Cells: walking and random (E, V) pairs in two passes (pass 1 with every software bit at 1, pass 2 with every software bit at 0); a pulse leg across a `wdt_rst_ni` pulse; a priority leg on OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC in both release orders; a `sep_reset_n` override with `rst_ni` high against armed registers in the `sep_reset_n` domain and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Functional coverage: `SEP_FCOV` Phase 2 coverpoints `cp_ip_ovrd_x_sw`, `cp_ovrd_no_iso`, `cp_ovrd_priority` and `cp_reset_class` (the watchdog reset, per-IP software reset and `sep_reset_n` override classes). The control of step 4, the set-up write that clears the field of d1 in step 5 and the `rst_ni` control of step 9 run with the graded window closed and fill no Phase 3 bin. The per-IP software reset class of `cp_reset_class` comes from the priority leg and from the `SW_RESET_N` = 0x00 write of pass 2.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw all values in the order above and log them. Boot with real fuse sense and wait for both reset taps to read 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). `SW_RESET_N` reads 0x7E, so the Key Manager stays held in every step and the TRNG precondition holds. Start the tap sampler on `ip_rst_n[6:1]`, `sep_reset_n`, `sep_cpu_reset_n`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and the host-path isolate-complete bits.
. Arm one non-default probe register in each engine domain, each with an RDL reset value: OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM`, AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, HMAC `INTR_ENABLE`, KMAC `ENTROPY_PERIOD`, TRNG (CSRNG `INTR_ENABLE`) and Adams Bridge `notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r` (a shadowed register takes two equal writes). Read each back.
. Pass 1, every software bit at 1. For each (E, V) pair, shift the TDR word with `reset_enable` cleared for the ports in E and `reset_control` set to V for them (all other ports idle), through `SepIcResetTdr`, and wait the drawn hold time. After a settle of 4 `clk_i` cycles (an environment choice; the override passes a two-flop synchronizer) sample the six `ip_rst_n` taps. Sample the host-path isolate-complete bits of the domains over the whole hold. Shift the idle TDR word to remove the overrides and wait for the taps to settle. For a domain held with V = 0, wait for its tap to read 1 (a bounded wait) and read its probe. Read the other probes. Re-arm the probes that read their default.
. Control, for each of OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and TRNG in turn: write the `SW_RESET_N` field of the domain to 0 and note the cycle at which its host-path isolate-complete bits rise. Wait for its tap to read 0. Write the field to 1 and wait for the tap to read 1 (a bounded wait). Re-arm the probe. The cycle is logged for each domain.
. Pulse leg, in the pass 1 state. Pick a domain d0 for V = 0 and a domain d1 for V = 1. Write the field of d1 to 0 and wait for its tap to read 0 (a bounded wait). Shift the TDR word that forces d0 with V = 0 and d1 with V = 1. After the settle, drive `wdt_rst_ni_i` low for the drawn width and release it. Sample both taps and the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap on every `clk_i` cycle of the pulse. Shift the idle TDR word. Wait for the d0 tap to read 1 and sample the d1 tap. Write the field of d1 to 1 and wait for its tap to read 1. Re-arm the probes.
. Priority leg, for each domain D in OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC and for both release orders. Write the field of D to 0 and wait for its tap to read 0 and every host-path isolate-complete bit of D to read 1 (a bounded wait). Shift the TDR word that forces D with V = 0. Order software first: write the field of D to 1 and sample the tap over the settle time and the drawn hold, then shift the idle word. Order override first: shift the idle word, sample the tap over the settle time and the drawn hold, then write the field of D to 1. After both releases wait for the tap to read 1 (a bounded wait) and read the probe of D. Re-arm the probe. The software bit is written to 1 only after its tap is low.
. Pass 2, every software bit at 0. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x00 and wait for the six taps to read 0 (a bounded wait). Apply the same (E, V) pairs as in step 3 with the same settle and hold. Sample the six taps. Do not read probes in this pass, because an isolated host path answers SLVERR. Remove the overrides. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E, wait for the six taps to read 1 (a bounded wait) and arm the probes again.
. `sep_reset_n` override leg, for each of the drawn events. Arm the `sep_reset_n`-domain registers SPI `CONFIGOPTS` and DMA `SRC_ADDR_LO` and the six engine probes of step 2 with non-default values (the previous event resets them). Read `SW_RESET_N` (0x7E) and the engine probes. Shift the TDR word that forces `sep_reset_n` with value 0 (`rst_ni` stays high) and hold it for the drawn width. Sample `ip_rst_n[6:1]`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and `aon_rst_n` on every cycle. Shift the idle word. Wait for the `sep_reset_n` tap to read 1 (a bounded wait). Read all armed registers, the engine probes and `SW_RESET_N`.
. Control for step 8: pulse `rst_ni` for at least 8 `clk_i` cycles, wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and both reset taps to read 1 (a bounded wait). Sample `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and `aon_rst_n` on every cycle from the `rst_ni` fall to that point: each reads 0 on one or more cycles (the controls of CHK-OVR-SENSE and CHK-OVR-AND).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OVR-IP
|For every pass, pair and domain the `ip_rst_n` tap equals the expected value after the settle: low for E = 1 and V = 0, high for E = 1 and V = 1 (also with the software bit at 0), and equal to the `SW_RESET_N` bit for E = 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing: the per-IP override muxes are applied last; `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc`, IC_RESET Support). A tap that ignores V, or that ignores E, is a FAIL. Anchor: the `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps against the TDR word that the driver shifted. Control: the E = 1, V = 0 cells of Adams Bridge and TRNG are graded by the open owners and log `CTL-OVR-IP`. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-IP PASS: dom=<D> E=<b> V=<b> sw_bit=<b> ip_rst_n=<b> expected=<b>`.
|CHK-OVR-SKIP-ISO
|For every (domain, pair) whose software bit reads 1 the host-path isolate-complete bits of the domain stay 0 over the whole override, because an override does not run the isolation sequence (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing: the override muxes allow debug to force reset without waiting for isolation). A bit that rises under an override alone is a FAIL. The Key Manager key-path bits are high while the Key Manager is held and are not scored. Anchor: the isolate-complete probes. Control: step 4 runs a software assert of each of OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and TRNG in the same leaf, and the assert of a domain raises its own host-path bits, so the check for a domain is void without its own control. A domain whose control does not raise its bits logs `OBS-OVR-SKIP-ISO: dom=<D> control not met` and is not counted as reached. Domains OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, Adams Bridge and TRNG run. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-SKIP-ISO PASS: dom=<D> sw_bit=1 iso_ack_max=0 over <n> cycles control_sw_assert_iso_ack_cycle=<c>`.
|CHK-OVR-RETURN
|After the override is removed, a domain held with V = 0 reads its probe at the RDL default and the other probes keep their armed value (pass 1 only). A domain that keeps its armed value, or a neighbour that resets, is a FAIL. Anchor: the real LSU readbacks. Control: each probe holds a non-default value before the pair. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-RETURN PASS: dom=<D> probe=<default\|retained>`.
|CHK-OVR-PULSE
|Each forced tap of the pulse leg holds its forced value on every `clk_i` cycle of the `wdt_rst_ni` pulse: the V = 0 tap low and the V = 1 tap high. A pulse that moves a forced tap is a FAIL. Anchor: the `ip_rst_n` taps on every cycle. Control: the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap is low during the `wdt_rst_ni` pulse, and after the overrides are removed the V = 0 tap rises and the V = 1 tap falls to its software bit (0), so both taps can move. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-PULSE PASS: pulse=WDT v0_dom=<D> v0_tap_low_cycles=<w> v1_dom=<D2> v1_sw_bit=0 v1_tap_high_cycles=<w> cpu_rst_low_on_wdt=1 moved_after_removal=1`.
|CHK-OVR-PRIO
|With the software bit and the override both active, the tap stays low until both are released, in either order (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, Isolation and Reset Sequencing). A tap that rises after the first release is a FAIL. The software bit is written to 1 only after every host-path isolate-complete bit of the domain is high. Anchor: the `ip_rst_n` tap and the isolate-complete probes. Control: after both releases the tap rises and the probe reads its default. D runs on OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-PRIO PASS: dom=<D> order=<sw_first\|ovr_first> iso_ack_high_before_sw_release=1 tap_after_first_release=0 tap_after_second=1`.
|CHK-OVR-AND
|During the `sep_reset_n` override `ip_rst_n[6:1]` are low on every cycle after the settle, while the `SW_RESET_N` bits for those domains read 1 before the override (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: each engine reset is ANDed with `sep_reset_n`). An engine tap that stays high is a FAIL. The `aon_rst_n` tap stays high. Anchor: the `ip_rst_n[6:1]` taps, the `aon_rst_n` tap and the `SW_RESET_N` readback before the override. Control for `aon_rst_n`: the `rst_ni` pulse of step 9 drops `aon_rst_n` in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-AND PASS: width=<w> ip_rst_n[6:1]=0b000000 aon_rst_n=1 SW_RESET_N_before=0x7e`.
|CHK-OVR-DOMAIN
|After the `sep_reset_n` override the `sep_reset_n`-domain registers (SPI `CONFIGOPTS`, DMA `SRC_ADDR_LO`) and the engine probes read their RDL default (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). A `sep_reset_n`-domain register that keeps its value is a FAIL. `SW_RESET_N` reads 0x7E before the override, so its read after the override cannot show a reset: the leaf logs it as `CTL-OVR-SWRST: SW_RESET_N=0x<v>`, fails on a value other than 0x7E, and does not count it as reset evidence. The watchdog registers of the `rst_ni` domain across the override are graded in `sep_timer_clock_contract_test` (CHK-REGWEN-HOLD). Anchor: the real LSU readbacks. Control: each register holds a non-default value before the override, so a reset is visible. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-DOMAIN PASS: sep_reset_n_domain_reset=<m>/<m>`.
|CHK-OVR-SENSE
|`sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is high on every cycle of the `sep_reset_n` override, because the OTP controller is in the cold reset domain (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture, cold reset row). A drop of the port is a FAIL. Anchor: the `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` output on every cycle. Control: the `rst_ni` pulse of step 9 drops `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` in the same leaf, so the output can fall. Evidence: `CHK-OVR-SENSE PASS: sep_fuse_sense_done_o high every cycle control_rst_ni_sense_done_low=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The Key Manager override and the `sep_reset_n` override on the bench pins belong to the open owners named in Test Coverage. Isolation of a key path is not graded here, because the Key Manager stays held. A `dbg_rstb_i` pulse is not driven: `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller) names the EL2 debug module and the run-request synchronizer as the blocks that it resets, and neither is an observable of this stub build. `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test` owns the pulse in the full-CPU build.

'''''
[[sep_scan_reset_override_test]]
===== 5.18 `sep_scan_reset_override_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense (no skip plusarg), 4 seeds. Tool: VCS only (the release phase is sub-cycle and the nets can hold X). Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu_stub` with `tools = ["vcs"]`; testlist `system.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_scan_reset_override_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master on `s_axi` (`SepAxiAgent` over `ocah_axi_vip`, approved), used for the `SW_RESET_N` write only; cocotb clock drive of `clk_i` and of `clk_wdt_i` (approved), with a value-change time stamp on every net under test; `clk_wdt_i` runs at a fixed period of 8 `clk_i` periods and a fixed phase of 5 percent of a `clk_i` period after a `clk_i` rising edge, so a `clk_wdt_i` edge never falls on a `clk_i` edge; reset taps `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o`, `ip_rst_n[6:1]`, `secure_tm_o` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, and the strap input `test_en_strap_i` (`secure_tm_req_i`) as a bench drive (all approved). Added hook (top-port drive): `test_en_i` and `scan_rst_ni` as bench inputs, which the test adds to the HDL Top port table of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`; `tb_top.sv` ties them to 0 and 1. `aon_rst_n`, `km_cond_rst[1:0]` and a read-only tap of every clock-gater test pin and AXI cell test input that the hierarchy walk finds are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The expected value of every net comes from the cited sentences. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RAND-REP. Draw order: `w`, `phase`. `w` is the low width of `scan_rst_ni` in `clk_i` cycles, 4 to 64 (the floor of 4 is an environment choice). `phase` is the release point of `scan_rst_ni`, 10 to 90 percent of the `clk_i` period after a `clk_i` rising edge, so the release is never at or near a `clk_i` edge or a `clk_wdt_i` edge. `reseed = 4` samples four `(w, phase)` pairs. The cells, their order and the strap values are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: While `test_en_i` is high, `scan_rst_ni` replaces the output of the SEP reset synchronizer, each scan bypass mux, the output of the watchdog reset synchronizer and both Key Manager reset conditioner outputs. With `test_en_i` low it has no effect. `test_en_i` drives the clock-gater test ports and the AXI cell test inputs. The strap input `secure_tm_req_i` and `test_en_i` are separate ports. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`test_en_i`, `scan_rst_ni`, `secure_tm_req_i`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller); `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc` (Reset Architecture); `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc` (Entry and Exit, Protection While Active).

*Test Coverage*: `tb_top.sv` ties `test_en_i` and `scan_rst_ni` to 0 and 1, so this test grades every cell of the two inputs. `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` grades that `secure_tm_o` follows the strap at the sampling event. That cell is a control of CHK-STRAP-INDEP here. Cells: `scan_rst_ni` x `test_en_i` (four cells); the 11 replaced nets `sep_reset_n`, `sep_cpu_reset_n`, `ip_rst_n[6:1]`, `aon_rst_n` and `km_cond_rst[1:0]`, with the Key Manager released so that the `km_cond_rst` taps start high; every clock-gater test pin and AXI cell test input that a hierarchy walk finds; `test_en_i` toggles against both strap values; strap toggles against `test_en_i` = 0. Functional coverage: `sep_reset_source_cg` (`cp_source_edge` for the `scan_rst_ni` cells, `cp_scan_bypass`, `cp_scan_net_effect`), `sep_static_tieoff_cg` (`cp_test_en_consumer`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>`. Draw `w` and `phase`, and print `DRAW w=<w> phase=<ph>`.
. Boot with real fuse sense. Drive `test_en_i` = 0 and `scan_rst_ni` = 1 from before the `rst_ni` release. Start the `clk_wdt_i` drive before the `rst_ni` release. Drive the strap `test_en_strap_i` = 1, stable from before the `rst_ni` release. Wait until the `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` taps are high and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` is high (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Wait for `secure_tm_o` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-STRAP-INDEP). This is the control of CHK-STRAP-INDEP.
. Run the hierarchy walk. Collect every clock-gater test pin and every AXI cell test input. Print the count of each class. A class with no pin fails CHK-TEST-EN-PINS.
. Independence with the strap at 1. Hold `scan_rst_ni` = 1. Error injection at `test_en_i`: toggle it 0, 1, 0 outside DFT use and hold each value for a hold interval of H `clk_i` cycles (a test parameter, logged; H is at least two `clk_wdt_i` periods). Expected response: every pin equals `test_en_i`, and `scan_rst_ni` = 1 forces no replaced net low. At each value wait until every pin equals `test_en_i` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-TEST-EN-PINS). Sample `secure_tm_o` on every `clk_i` rising edge. The nets are not sampled here, because the Key Manager is still held in reset.
. Error injection at `secure_tm_req_i`: with `test_en_i` = 0, toggle the strap 1, 0, 1 after the capture and hold each value for H cycles. The specification requires a stable strap, so this is illegal stimulus. Expected response: every pin keeps its value 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`: two separate ports).
. Independence with the strap at 0. Set the strap to 0. Pulse `rst_ni` low and release it, with the strap stable from before the assert. Wait until the reset taps and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` are high (a bounded wait). `secure_tm_o` = 0. Error injection at `test_en_i`: toggle it 0, 1, 0 outside DFT use as in step 4. Expected response: every pin follows `test_en_i`, and `secure_tm_o` stays 0.
. Release the Key Manager. Through the LSU master, write 1 to `SW_RESET_N.km_sw_rst_n`. Wait until both `km_cond_rst` taps are high (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Read the 11 nets: all are high before the next step.
. Control A. With `test_en_i` = 0, drive `scan_rst_ni` low for `w` `clk_i` cycles. Release it at the drawn `phase`. Sample the 11 nets on every `clk_i` rising edge from the `scan_rst_ni` fall to the end of the hold interval after the rise. All stay high.
. Leg B, error injection at `test_en_i`: raise `test_en_i` outside DFT use. Expected response: the listed nets follow `scan_rst_ni`. With `scan_rst_ni` = 1 hold the nets under sample for H cycles: all are high. Drive `scan_rst_ni` low for `w` cycles. Every net is low on the last `clk_i` rising edge before the release. Release `scan_rst_ni` at the drawn `phase`. Record the time of the `scan_rst_ni` rise, the time of each net rise, the time of the first `clk_i` rising edge and of the first `clk_wdt_i` rising edge after the `scan_rst_ni` rise. Sample the nets until the end of the hold interval.
. Print the summary lines.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SCAN-IDLE
|With `test_en_i` = 0, a low `scan_rst_ni` leaves every one of the 11 nets high on every `clk_i` rising edge from the `scan_rst_ni` fall to the end of the hold interval after its rise (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `scan_rst_ni`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller, last paragraph: the override acts while `test_en_i` is high). A DUT that ignores `test_en_i` shows a low net. Anchor: the reset taps (approved) and the `aon_rst_n` and `km_cond_rst` taps. Control: CHK-SCAN-REPLACE moves the same 11 nets with the same pulse in the same leaf, and the baseline of step 7 shows them high before the pulse. Evidence: `CHK-SCAN-IDLE PASS: scan_rst_ni low <w> cycles test_en_i=0 nets_high=<n>/<n>`.
|CHK-SCAN-REPLACE
|With `test_en_i` = 1, every one of the 11 nets is low on the last `clk_i` rising edge before the `scan_rst_ni` rise, with `rst_ni` high, fuse sensing done and `SW_RESET_N` released. With `scan_rst_ni` = 1 and `test_en_i` = 1 every net is high. `sep_reset_n` rises before the first `clk_i` rising edge after the `scan_rst_ni` rise, and `aon_rst_n` rises before the first `clk_wdt_i` rising edge after it, because the scan reset replaces the outputs of `u_sep_reset_sync`, which releases to `clk_i`, and of `u_rst_wdt_sync`, which synchronizes into `clk_wdt_i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: the `u_sep_reset_sync` item, the watchdog synchronizer sentence and the `scan_rst_ni` sentence; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture). The specification names no clock for the other nets, so their rise offsets are logged as `OBS-SCAN-OFFSET` and not graded. A DUT that keeps `u_sep_reset_sync` or `u_rst_wdt_sync` in the path releases the net at an edge of its own clock and fails. A DUT that does not replace the nets keeps them high and fails. Anchor: the reset taps (approved), the `aon_rst_n` and `km_cond_rst` taps, the `clk_i` and `clk_wdt_i` edge times (approved) and the value-change time stamps. Control: CHK-SCAN-IDLE shows the same nets high for the same pulse with `test_en_i` = 0, so a fall is attributable to `test_en_i`. Evidence: `CHK-SCAN-REPLACE PASS: nets_low=<n>/<n>; scan_rst_ni rose t=<ps> sep_reset_n rose t=<ps> next_clk_i_edge t=<ps> aon_rst_n rose t=<ps> next_clk_wdt_i_edge t=<ps>`.
|CHK-TEST-EN-PINS
|The hierarchy walk finds at least one pin in each class (clock-gater test ports, AXI cell test inputs). Every pin equals `test_en_i` at both values of `test_en_i`, for the strap at 1 and at 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `test_en_i`). A pin that stays at one value fails. A walk that finds no pin in a class fails the leaf. Anchor: the read-only pin taps. Control: every pin is read at both values of `test_en_i`, so a constant pin cannot pass. Evidence: `CHK-TEST-EN-PINS PASS: pins=<N> clock_gater=<a> axi_cell=<b> follow test_en_i`.
|CHK-STRAP-INDEP
|`secure_tm_o` stays unchanged across the `test_en_i` toggles, and every pin stays unchanged across the strap toggles (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`: `test_en_i` and `secure_tm_req_i` are separate ports; `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit). A DUT that derives one input from the other shows a moved output or a moved pin. Anchor: `secure_tm_o` (approved), the pin taps and `test_en_strap_i` (approved). Controls: `secure_tm_o` reads 1 after sensing with the strap at 1 and 0 after sensing with the strap at 0 in the same leaf, so the output can take both values; every pin follows `test_en_i` in the same leaf (CHK-TEST-EN-PINS), so a pin can move. Evidence: `CHK-STRAP-INDEP PASS: secure_tm_o=<v> unchanged across <k> test_en_i toggles; pins unchanged across <k> strap toggles`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test grades the 11 listed nets and the pins that the walk finds. It does not grade the reset behaviour of any other net, the protections of `SECURE_TM` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, Protection While Active) or the state that the scan reset leaves in the SEP. The sampling of the strap at the sampling events stays with `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` and `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test`.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_reset_entry_test]]
===== 5.19 `sep_cpu_reset_entry_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu` (real core; a stub CPU makes the test vacuous), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 4 seeds. Tool: Verilator and VCS. The TAP reset scope belongs to this CPU-mode leaf; its reset-vector and run-request legs share the cold reset and first-fetch stimulus. Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu`; testlist `cpu.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_reset_entry_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU trace port `cpu_trace_valid_o` and `cpu_trace_addr_o` (approved); outbound mailbox marker words that the staged code stores (approved firmware console); `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o`, `scratch_cold_probe_o`; bench drives `mpc_reset_run_req`, `rst_ni`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `ext_boot_seq_done_i` and `rst_vec_i` (approved); ROM and SRAM image initialisation by backdoor (approved). Added hook (top-port drive): bench-owned `jtag_tck_i`, `jtag_tms_i`, `jtag_tdi_i` and `jtag_trst_ni` with a DTM shifter, which the test adds to the HDL Top port table of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`; `tb_top.sv` keeps the pins in internal registers. `rom_req` and `ifu_req` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. The model holds the vector and the marker of each code image. It reads no RTL. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RANDCFG. Draw order: `A1`, `A2`, TCK period, TRST width, `d`. `A1` and `A2` are halfword aligned vectors in SRAM, 0x1000_1000 to 0x1003_F000, outside the regions that hold the other staged code images, and at least the size of one code image apart, so the two images do not overlap. The TCK period is 4 to 16 `clk_i` periods (slower than `clk_i`; an environment choice). The TRST width is 2 to 50 TCK cycles. `d` is the `ext_boot_seq_done_i` delay after the `rst_ni` release, 0 to 200 `clk_i` cycles. `reseed = 4` samples four draws of each. The leg order is fixed.

*Objective*: Reset-vector leg. With the default TDR the first CPU fetch after cold reset is at the boot ROM base 0x1004_0000. An address shifted into the TDR (JTAG opcode 0x18) while the CPU is in reset redirects the first fetch. The value persists through CPU resets. Only `jtag_trst_ni` or the Test-Logic-Reset state restores the default. `jtag_trst_ni` and the Test-Logic-Reset state reset only the TAP and its reset-vector TDR. `sep_reset_n`, `sep_cpu_reset_n` and the registers are not reset. Run-request leg. `mpc_reset_run_req` = 1 at reset release lets the CPU fetch from the boot ROM. No instruction retires before both resets are high. The port table gives only "run/halt selection after reset" with the default 1, so the effect of the value 0 is not claimed and the value 0 is not driven. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc` (Boot ROM); `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (Reset and Boot, Reset Vector, Debug); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`jtag_trst_ni`, `mpc_reset_run_req_i`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` and `sep_rom_sanity_test`, which grade that the core fetches and retires code from the Boot ROM with the bench driving `rst_vec_i`; they do not grade the default vector. `sep_hello_world_test` grades the boot from the ICCM. This test grades only the new cells: the default-vector first fetch, the redirect and its persistence, and the TAP reset scope. The open owners keep the rest. The ROM execution, the boot from the ICCM and the run cell (run request 1 lets the core run, `CTL-RUNREQ`) are controls here. Cells: default vector, with and without a TAP reset before the CPU reset; redirect to `A1`, persistence across a `wdt_rst_ni` CPU reset, rewrite to `A2`, each vector with its own marker code; TAP reset by `jtag_trst_ni` and by five `jtag_tms_i` = 1 cycles, each with the TDR at its default and holding a programmed value, against armed registers and both scratch banks (the warm bank is a liveness control); two cold resets with run request 1, one with a drawn and one with a zero boot-sequence delay. Functional coverage: `sep_reset_source_cg` (`cp_source_edge` for the `jtag_trst_ni` and Test-Logic-Reset cells, `cp_jtag_mech`), `sep_clk_domain_cg` (`cp_domain_crossing` for the `jtag_tck_i` cell), `sep_boot_milestone_cg` (`cp_first_fetch`), `sep_reset_scope_retention_cg` (`cp_reset_class` for the TAP class, `cp_bank_class_outcome` for the TAP reset cells), `sep_debug_trace_cg` (`cp_tap_instruction`, `cp_rstvec_tdr`, `cp_trace_activity` (the reset intervals of the cold resets and CPU resets)), and the Phase 1 `sep_boot_rom_cg` (`cp_ifu`) and `sep_cpu_boot_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>`. Draw `A1`, `A2`, the TCK period, the TRST width and `d`. Print one `DRAW` line for each.
. Stage three code images by backdoor. The ROM image holds code at 0x1004_0000 that stores marker `M_ROM` to the outbound mailbox. The SRAM holds code at `A1` that stores `M1` and code at `A2` that stores `M2`. The three code sequences differ, so a marker names the code that ran. Each sequence ends in one common retention routine. The routine does four things in order. It reads the first register of each scratch bank, `WKUP_THOLD_LO` and `SEP_SW_DEBUG`, and stores the four words to the outbound mailbox. It writes a unique non-zero value to each of the four. It reads the four back in a loop, compares them with the written values, and stores a pass count to the outbound mailbox on each pass. It never stops.
. Drive `rst_vec_i` at its idle value. Start a sampler that records `cpu_trace_valid_o`, `cpu_trace_addr_o`, the `rom_req` and `ifu_req` taps and the `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` tap on every `clk_i` rising edge, and keep it running through every cold reset and every `wdt_rst_ni` pulse of the steps that follow, to the end of step 11 (CHK-NO-EARLY-RETIRE, CHK-NO-EARLY-FETCH).
. Run-request leg, first cold reset. Drive `mpc_reset_run_req` = 1 (the port default) from before the `rst_ni` fall to the end of the observation window. Pulse `rst_ni` low and release it. Hold `ext_boot_seq_done_i` low for `d` `clk_i` cycles after the release and then raise it. The observation window is W_obs `clk_i` cycles from the `sep_cpu_reset_n` rise (an environment choice, fixed for the leaf and logged). Sample the trace port on every `clk_i` rising edge from the `rst_ni` fall to the end of the window. The first retired PC is 0x1004_0000 and the marker is `M_ROM` within the window (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf as a failed precondition, `CTL-RUNREQ`; the PC and the marker are graded by CHK-RSTVEC-DEFAULT).
. Second cold reset with `mpc_reset_run_req` = 1, a delay of 0 clocks (the term is high at the release) and the same W_obs. Apply the checks of step 4.
. Third cold reset with `mpc_reset_run_req` = 1. The default TDR holds, and no TAP reset has occurred. The first retired PC is 0x1004_0000 and the marker is `M_ROM`.
. TAP reset with the default TDR. Pulse `jtag_trst_ni` low for the drawn TRST width in TCK cycles and release it. Pulse `wdt_rst_ni` low and release it as the CPU reset. The first retired PC is 0x1004_0000 and the marker is `M_ROM`. Repeat with five `jtag_tms_i` = 1 cycles on `jtag_tck_i` with `jtag_trst_ni` high in place of the TRST pulse.
. Redirect. Hold the CPU in reset with `wdt_rst_ni` low. Stage the SRAM content of step 2 again by backdoor, because the SRAM content across the cold resets of steps 4 to 6 is not stated. Through the DTM shifter, shift the instruction 0x18, then shift `A1[31:1]` into the TDR. Release `wdt_rst_ni`. The first retired PC is `A1` and the marker is `M1`. No fetch request appears on `rom_req` or `ifu_req` before the `sep_cpu_reset_n` rise.
. Persistence. Pulse `wdt_rst_ni` low and release it. The first retired PC is `A1` and the marker is `M1`.
. Rewrite. Hold the CPU in reset. Shift `A2[31:1]` into the TDR. Release. The first retired PC is `A2` and the marker is `M2`.
. TAP reset scope. Run four sub-legs in this order: `jtag_trst_ni` with the TDR at `A2`; Test-Logic-Reset with the TDR at default; `jtag_trst_ni` with the TDR at default; Test-Logic-Reset with the TDR at `A2`. The first sub-leg starts from step 10 (the TDR holds `A2` and the CPU runs the code at `A2`). Before the second and third sub-legs the CPU runs the ROM code.
