Before the fourth sub-leg, hold the CPU in reset, shift the instruction 0x18 again (the `jtag_trst_ni` reset of the third sub-leg resets the TAP), then shift `A2[31:1]`, and release. In each sub-leg do the following.
.. Wait until the retention routine reports a pass count after the marker of the current run (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Log `scratch_cold_probe_o` word 0 and the pass count.
.. Apply the TAP reset: `jtag_trst_ni` low for the drawn TRST width, or five `jtag_tms_i` = 1 cycles with `jtag_trst_ni` high. Sample the `sep_reset_n` and `sep_cpu_reset_n` taps on every `clk_i` rising edge from the start of the TAP reset to the next pass count of the routine after it.
.. The routine reports a pass count above the one logged at the start of the TAP reset, with all four compares true (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-TRST-SCOPE). `scratch_cold_probe_o` word 0 is unchanged.
.. Pulse `wdt_rst_ni` low and release it as the CPU reset. The first retired PC is 0x1004_0000 and the marker is `M_ROM`, also when the TDR held `A2`. The entry words of the routine of that reset show the warm scratch word at 0 and the cold scratch word at its armed value. These two readbacks are the controls of CHK-TRST-SCOPE.
. Evaluate every checker and print the summary lines.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RSTVEC-DEFAULT
|With the default TDR and `rst_vec_i` at its idle value, the first retired PC on the trace port after each cold reset with run request 1 is 0x1004_0000 and the marker on the mailbox is `M_ROM` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, Boot ROM; `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector). A DUT that starts at another address, or that never fetches, fails. Anchor: the trace port and the mailbox marker (approved). Control: the code images at `A1` and `A2` differ from the ROM code, so a marker names the code that ran. Evidence: `CHK-RSTVEC-DEFAULT PASS: first_pc=0x10040000 marker=M_ROM`.
|CHK-RSTVEC
|After the TDR is programmed with `A`, the first retired PC equals `A` and the marker is the one planted at `A` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector: an address shifted in while the CPU is in reset redirects the first fetch). A DUT with an inert TDR starts at 0x1004_0000 with `M_ROM` and fails. This is a specification-versus-build finding, not an expected-fail entry. Anchor: the trace port and the marker. Control: the default leg of the same leaf starts at the ROM base. Evidence: `CHK-RSTVEC PASS: leg=<redirect\|rewrite> vector=0x<A> first_pc=0x<A> marker=<M>`.
|CHK-RSTVEC-PERSIST
|After a `wdt_rst_ni` pulse the first retired PC equals the programmed vector again (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector: CPU reset alone does not restore the default). A DUT that restores the default at a CPU reset starts at 0x1004_0000 and fails. Anchor: the trace port and the marker. Control: CHK-RSTVEC-TAPRST shows that a TAP reset does restore the default in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-RSTVEC-PERSIST PASS: after wdt reset first_pc=0x<A1> marker=M1`.
|CHK-RSTVEC-TAPRST
|After a TAP reset (`jtag_trst_ni` or Test-Logic-Reset) and a CPU reset, the first retired PC is 0x1004_0000 and the marker is `M_ROM`, also when the TDR held a programmed vector before the TAP reset (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). A DUT whose TAP reset leaves the TDR programmed starts at the old vector and fails. Anchor: the trace port and the marker. Control: CHK-RSTVEC-PERSIST shows the programmed vector surviving a CPU reset in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-RSTVEC-TAPRST PASS: via=<TRST\|TLR> tdr=<default\|programmed> first_pc=0x10040000 marker=M_ROM`.
|CHK-TRST-SCOPE
|During `jtag_trst_ni` and Test-Logic-Reset the `sep_reset_n` and `sep_cpu_reset_n` taps stay high, and the four armed values (both scratch banks, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `SEP_SW_DEBUG`) stay retained (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `jtag_trst_ni` resets the EL2 JTAG TAP). A DUT that resets the SEP, the CPU or a register with the TAP shows a low tap or a failed compare. Anchor: the two reset taps, `scratch_cold_probe_o` and the routine pass count (approved). Controls: the CPU reset that follows clears the warm scratch word and moves the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap, and the `rst_ni` reset at the start of the leaf holds the `sep_reset_n` tap low until the release, so both taps can move. Evidence: `CHK-TRST-SCOPE PASS: via=<TRST\|TLR> tdr=<default\|programmed> sep_reset_n_low=0 sep_cpu_reset_n_low=0 regs_retained=<k>/<k> control_warm_cleared=1`.
|CHK-NO-EARLY-RETIRE
|No instruction retires on the trace port before the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap is high, in every cold reset of the leaf and in each CPU reset, with the trace port and the tap sampled together on every `clk_i` rising edge from each reset assertion to the first retirement after it (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: the CPU reset is `sep_reset_n` ANDed with `wdt_rst_ni`). A DUT that runs the core in reset shows a valid trace beat while the tap is low. Anchor: the trace port and the tap (approved). Control: the run leg produces a retirement in the same leaf, and the tap is low at the start of every cold reset and every CPU reset. Evidence: `CHK-NO-EARLY-RETIRE PASS: resets=<n> retire_before_release=0 control_retire_cycle=<c>`.
|CHK-NO-EARLY-FETCH
|No fetch request appears on `rom_req` or `ifu_req` before the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap is high, in every cold reset of the leaf and in each CPU reset, with the taps sampled together on every `clk_i` rising edge from each reset assertion to the tap rise (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: the CPU reset is `sep_reset_n` ANDed with `wdt_rst_ni`). A DUT that fetches in reset shows a request while the tap is low. Anchor: `rom_req` and `ifu_req`. Control: the run leg produces a fetch on `rom_req` after the tap rise in the same leaf, so the taps are live. Evidence: `CHK-NO-EARLY-FETCH PASS: resets=<n> fetch_before_release=0 control_fetch_cycle=<c>`.
|===

`CTL-RUNREQ` is a control and not a checker. With `mpc_reset_run_req` = 1 the first retired PC is 0x1004_0000 in the observation window after the `sep_cpu_reset_n` rise, for the drawn delay `d` and for the zero delay; the open boot tests grade that the core runs from the boot ROM with the request at 1, CHK-RSTVEC-DEFAULT grades the first retired PC, and `sep_boot_release_terms_test` grades the boot term. The leaf fails as a failed precondition when no instruction retires in the window. The window length is an environment choice. The line is `CTL-RUNREQ: run_req=1 d=<d> first_pc=0x<..> retired_in_window=<n> window=<w>`.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: A vector outside ROM and SRAM belongs to `sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test`. The test does not claim the IDCODE value and its source, the value that Capture-DR returns from the TDR, the priority, combination and pulse-width rule for the halt and run requests, the fetch behaviour of a halted core, or a change of the request after the reset release (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Combinations of halt and run requests are not driven, and the value 0 of `mpc_reset_run_req` is not driven. The ROM execution and the boot from the ICCM stay with `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`, `sep_rom_sanity_test` and `sep_hello_world_test`. If the TDR shift is inert in the build, the result is a specification-versus-build finding and not an expected-fail entry.

'''''

[[sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test]]
===== 5.20 `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense (no skip plusarg), because the early capture must fall before sense completion, `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive` for the `sep_reset_n` override pulse of leg B (<<sep-vplan-phase3-edn-waiver>>), 4 seeds. Tool: Verilator and VCS. The `dbg_rstb_i` event of leg B runs on VCS only. Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu_stub`; testlist `efuse_lcc.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_secure_tm_strap_sampling_test`

*BFM / Model*: strap drive `test_en_strap_i` (`secure_tm_req_i`), `secure_tm_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `rst_ni`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i`, `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i`, the `ip_rst_n[3]` tap, the `SW_RESET_N` register through the CPU-LSU master (`SepAxiAgent` over `ocah_axi_vip`), and `j_axi_*` for the `SEC_DIS` token helper (all approved). No PROPOSED item. The model holds the sampling table of `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc` and reads no RTL. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RAND-REP. Draw order: `c`, event order. `c` is the number of `clk_i` cycles from the last sampling event to a strap change after it, 8 to 200. The event order is a permutation of the four events of leg B. The strap value of each leg is fixed by the leg, and both values run in legs C and D. The `SEC_DIS` setup is fixed and not drawn. `reseed = 4` samples four `(c, order)` pairs.

*Objective*: `SECURE_TM` clears when cold reset asserts. With `SEC_DIS` asserted at the early capture point the strap is sampled once after cold-reset release. It is also sampled at each fuse-sense completion. No other event samples it. The sampled value is held until the next sampling event. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc` (Entry and Exit); `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc` (Reset Persistence); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`secure_tm_req_i`, `secure_tm_o`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test`, which raises the strap, re-senses the image and grades that `secure_tm_o` follows the strap at the fuse-sense completion (`CHK-SECURE-TM`, `CHK-SECURE-TM-ON`, `CHK-SECURE-TM-OFF`) and that `secure_tm_o` does not qualify `FEAT_CTRL`; and `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`, which grades the shadow write block under the strap. This test grades only the new cells: the early capture with `SEC_DIS`, the clear at cold reset, the retention across other events, and a strap change between sampling events. The open owners keep the rest. The sampling at fuse-sense completion is a control here. Cells: leg A cold clear; leg B four non-sampling events at `secure_tm_o` = 1; leg C strap 1 to 0 and 0 to 1 with no sampling event; leg D0 early-capture control with no match; leg E late `SEC_DIS`; leg D early capture with the match present: strap 1 (the graded capture) and strap 0 (a retention leg, because 0 is also the value of the cold-reset clear), each with a flip after the release. Functional coverage: `sep_documented_absence_cg` (`cp_secure_tm_state` (the cold-reset clear), `cp_stm_event_x_strap`, `cp_stm_strap_stability` (the strap changes after a capture)). The fuse-sense completion samples and leg D0 are controls and fill no Phase 3 bin. The cold boots that start legs C, D0 and E are set-up reset sequences and run with the graded window closed, so only leg A fills the cold-reset clear of `cp_secure_tm_state`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>`. Draw `c` and the event order. Print one `DRAW` line for each. A cold boot in this procedure means: set the strap, pulse `rst_ni` low and release it, and wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). The strap is stable from before the `rst_ni` fall to the end of the boot. Sample `secure_tm_o` on every `clk_i` rising edge from the start of each boot to the end of its hold. A hold is H `clk_i` cycles (a test parameter, logged).
. Leg A, cold clear. Cold boot with the strap at 1. Wait for `secure_tm_o` = 1 after sense completion (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-STM-RETAIN and CHK-STM-CLEAR). This is the control of both checks.
. Apply a cold reset with the strap still 1: pulse `rst_ni` low and hold it low for a test-chosen number of `clk_i` cycles. Sample `secure_tm_o` on every `clk_i` rising edge while `rst_ni` is low. It reads 0 at the last rising edge before the release (CHK-STM-CLEAR).
. Release `rst_ni` and wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. `secure_tm_o` reads 1 again. Wait for `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` to read 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), because `sep_reset_n` resets the Reset Controller register block that the `SW_RESET_N` event of leg B writes (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller).
. Leg B, non-sampling events with `secure_tm_o` = 1. In the drawn order apply `wdt_rst_ni` low then high; `dbg_rstb_i` low then high (VCS only); a `SW_RESET_N` pulse (write 0 to `hmac_sw_rst_n`, wait for the `ip_rst_n[3]` tap to fall, write 1, wait for the tap to rise); and a `sep_reset_n` override pulse (`jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` = 1 with `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` = 0 for a test-chosen number of cycles, then the override 0, then wait for `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` high). Each wait is bounded. After each event hold for H cycles. `secure_tm_o` stays 1 (CHK-STM-RETAIN).
. Leg C, strap change with no sampling event, strap 1 to 0. Cold boot with the strap at 1. After `secure_tm_o` = 1, wait `c` cycles after the rise of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Error injection at `secure_tm_req_i`: change the strap 1 to 0. The specification requires a stable strap. Expected response: no sample, and `secure_tm_o` keeps 1 for a hold (CHK-STM-RETAIN).
. Leg C, strap 0 to 1. Cold boot with the strap at 0 (the strap is 0 after step 6 and stable from before the `rst_ni` fall). `secure_tm_o` reads 0 after sense completion. Wait `c` cycles after the rise of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Error injection at `secure_tm_req_i`: change the strap 0 to 1. Expected response: no sample, and `secure_tm_o` keeps 0 for a hold (CHK-STM-RETAIN).
. Leg D0, early-capture control, before any `SEC_DIS` match. The strap is 1 after step 7. Cold boot. `secure_tm_o` reads 0 from the release until `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises and reads 1 after it. Log the release-to-sense-completion interval as `T_sense`. The interval is a calibration for step 11 and is not an expected value.
. Leg E, late `SEC_DIS`. Set the strap to 0. Cold boot. `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises, so no match is present (log `match_present=0`), and `secure_tm_o` reads 0.
. Error injection at `secure_tm_req_i`: raise the strap to 1 (a change after the capture). Present the `SEC_DIS` token through the `j_axi_*` helper. Hold for H cycles. Expected response: the strap is not sampled, because no cold reset follows the match (`hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`, Reset Persistence), and `secure_tm_o` stays 0 (CHK-STM-LATE).
. Leg D, early capture, strap 1. The match survives reset and is present. The strap is 1 and stable. Pulse `rst_ni` low and release it. Wait until `secure_tm_o` equals the strap with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` low, for at most `T_sense` cycles after the release (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-STM-CAPTURE). `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` stays low for the whole step. If it rises, the token did not match and the leaf fails as a failed precondition (`match_present=0` at this boot). Log `match_present=1`.
. Error injection at `secure_tm_req_i`: change the strap 1 to 0 `c` cycles after `secure_tm_o` reaches 1. Expected response: no sample, and `secure_tm_o` keeps 1 for a hold (CHK-STM-CAPTURE, CHK-STM-RETAIN).
. Leg D, strap 0 (retention leg). The strap is 0 and stable. Pulse `rst_ni` low and release it. Log `secure_tm_o` within the same bounded wait, with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` low, as `OBS-STM-CAPTURE0` (not graded as a capture: the cold-reset clear gives the same 0). Error injection at `secure_tm_req_i`: change the strap 0 to 1 `c` cycles after the release. Expected response: no sample, and `secure_tm_o` keeps 0 for a hold (CHK-STM-RETAIN).
. Evaluate every checker. CHK-STM-LATE and CHK-STM-RETAIN read their controls at the end of the leaf.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STM-CAPTURE
|In leg D with the match present and the strap at 1, `secure_tm_o` reads 1 with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` low, at a time no later than `T_sense` after the release, and keeps that value after the later strap flip (`hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit table; `hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`, Reset Persistence). The two documents give the second clock edge and one cycle. Neither is graded. A DUT with no early capture never raises `secure_tm_o` with the strap at 1 while sensing is held, and fails. A DUT that samples again after the capture shows the flip. Anchor: `secure_tm_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and the strap (approved). Control: leg D0, the same boot without a match, shows the strap value only at sense completion. The strap-0 leg is not part of this check: `secure_tm_o` = 0 is also the value that the cold-reset clear gives, so a DUT with no early capture passes it. It logs `OBS-STM-CAPTURE0: match_present=1 strap=0 secure_tm_o=<v> sense_done=0` and its flip is graded by CHK-STM-RETAIN. Evidence: `CHK-STM-CAPTURE PASS: leg=D match_present=1 strap=1 secure_tm_o=1 at release+<k> sense_done=0 after_flip=1 control_D0=0`.
|CHK-STM-RETAIN
|In legs B, C and D `secure_tm_o` keeps its value through every listed event and after every strap change (`hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit: neither sampling event retains the sampled value). A DUT that clears `secure_tm_o` at an event, or samples the strap at one, shows a changed value. Anchor: `secure_tm_o` sampled on every `clk_i` rising edge (approved). Control: leg A shows `secure_tm_o` = 1 after sensing in the same leaf, and the check fails if `secure_tm_o` never reads 1 in the leaf. The strap changes of leg C move the strap away from the held value, so a resample shows. Evidence: `CHK-STM-RETAIN PASS: leg=<B\|C\|D> events=<list> value=<v> cycles=<n> control_legA=1`.
|CHK-STM-CLEAR
|With `rst_ni` low `secure_tm_o` is 0, with the strap still 1 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, Entry and Exit: cold reset asserted clears `SECURE_TM`). A DUT that keeps the sampled value through cold reset reads 1. Anchor: `secure_tm_o` and `rst_ni` (approved). Control: it reads 1 after sensing in leg A, before the reset. Evidence: `CHK-STM-CLEAR PASS: rst_ni low -> secure_tm_o=0 at cycle <c>`.
|CHK-STM-LATE
|In leg E, with no match present at the boot, `secure_tm_o` stays 0 after the strap rises and the `SEC_DIS` token arrives, because the strap is sampled only at a sampling event (`hw/sys/sep/doc/security_disable.adoc`, Reset Persistence: a match without a following cold reset never samples the request). A DUT that samples at the token shows 1. Anchor: `secure_tm_o` and the strap (approved). Control: leg D with strap 1 shows `secure_tm_o` = 1 after an early capture, and the check fails if it never does. Evidence: `CHK-STM-LATE PASS: match_present=0 strap=1 SEC_DIS late secure_tm_o=0 cycles=<n> control_legD=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the edge of the early capture (second edge or one cycle) or the clear by a later capture, because the specification says "can". Leg B runs with a stable strap, so it shows that an event does not clear `secure_tm_o` and does not show a resample of an unchanged strap. The protection outputs of `SECURE_TM` belong to `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test`. The sampling at fuse-sense completion, the follow of the strap and the shadow write block stay with `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` and `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test]]
===== 5.21 `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 4 seeds. The CPU control leg and the scratch bank leg share the register write stimulus and run in one leaf. Tool: Verilator and VCS. Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu_stub`; testlist `system.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_shape_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master on `s_axi` (`SepAxiAgent` over `ocah_axi_vip`, approved) with full strobes on the 64-bit lane or on one 32-bit half; `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `rst_ni`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `scratch_cold_probe_o` (approved); register model from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta`) and the access types of the register description. No PROPOSED item. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RAND-REP. CPU control leg draws: values (including all-ones, values with bit 0 set and values with bits 63:32 non-zero; a value written to `SEP_NMI_VEC` after the lock differs from the stored vector in bits 31:1, and at least four of the locked writes use the 64-bit lane or the low 32-bit half), write order and lane (64-bit or one 32-bit half, full strobes only). Scratch leg draws: the event sequence of 4 to 10 events from {`WRITE_BANK(b)`, `WDT_PULSE`, `COLD`}, with at least one `WDT_PULSE` and at least one `COLD`, each after a write to both banks, at least two `WRITE_BANK` events for each bank, and no `sep_reset_n`-only event (cold-bank retention across it is not stated); the index permutation of each bank write; the values (unique and non-zero across the sixteen registers); random non-zero bits 63:32; the write shape of each bank write (one 64-bit lane write, or two 32-bit halves in a drawn order), with each bank written in both shapes in every leaf. `reseed = 4` samples four sets of each draw; the event kinds and the two shapes of each bank run in every leaf. The counts of writes (8 per register class) are fixed.

*Objective*: CPU control leg. `SEP_VERSION_ID` reads 0xDEADBEEF in its field and ignores writes. Once `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK.lock` is 1, a write to `SEP_NMI_VEC` does not change the vector field. A write of 0 or of all-ones to the RW1S `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK` leaves it at 1. The two read-only sense status registers ignore writes. The two write-only timeout registers accept a write with a response; the effect of the write is not graded, because the register description marks the per-block timeout registers as reserved and not implemented. Scratch leg. Each scratch bank holds eight 32-bit RW fields in 64-bit slots. Bits 63:32 have no field in the register description, so a write to them changes no field, for any write order and shape. The register description states no read value for the reserved bits of these registers, so the leaf logs them and does not grade them. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SEP_VERSION_ID`, `SEP_NMI_VEC`, `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK`, `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`, `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS`, `TIMEOUT_CLEAR`, `TIMEOUT_MODE`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (Dual Scratch Register Banks, Scratch Register Reference); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_scratch.adoc` (`SCRATCH[8]`, bits 63:32 with Access "-"); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (scratch banks).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, which grades the reset value, the write mask, the read-only and the reserved bits of both scratch banks and of the CPU control block, and excludes `SEP_VERSION_ID`, the status registers and the timeout registers from its write sweep; `sep_nmi_sanity_test`, which grades the default vector, the vector write-back, the set of the lock and the ignored vector write after it; `sep_axi_smoke_test`, which writes and reads `SEP_NMI_VEC` once; `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`, which grades the distinct pattern in every register of both banks (`CHK-BANK-ALIAS`) and the clear and retain across a CPU reset and a cold reset; and `sep_clock_gate_control_test`, which grades `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`. This test grades only the new cells: the writes to `SEP_VERSION_ID`, the two status registers and the timeout registers, the vector field of `SEP_NMI_VEC` after locked writes of drawn values on the drawn lane, the lock writes of 0 and all-ones, and the write-shape cells of the scratch banks with non-zero upper write data. The open owners keep the rest: the reset value of `SEP_NMI_VEC` (`sep_nmi_sanity_test`, `CHK-VEC-DEFAULT`; `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, `CHK-RESET`) and the reserved bits of `SEP_NMI_VEC` after a write before the lock (`sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, `CHK-RESERVED`). The index independence, the clear and retain readbacks, the first read of `SEP_NMI_VEC` and the readbacks of the writes before the lock are controls here (`CTL-SCR-NEIGHBOUR`, `CTL-SCR-SEQ`, `CTL-NMI-DEFAULT`, `CTL-NMI-STORE`). Cells: `SEP_VERSION_ID` writes of random values and all-ones; `SEP_NMI_VEC` writes after the lock; lock writes of 0 and all-ones; `TIMEOUT_CLEAR` and `TIMEOUT_MODE` writes; writes of the complement of bit 0 to the two status registers; 16 scratch registers with random order, shape and non-zero upper bits across WDT pulses and cold resets. Reset-time clearing of the lock bits is not scored. Functional coverage: `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_cg` (`cp_version_id`, `cp_nmi_vec`, `cp_nmi_lock`, `cp_status_ro_write`), `sep_cpu_ctrl_timeout_inert_cg` (`cp_timeout_write_inert`), `sep_aperture_reserved_access_cg` (`cp_reserved_field_write` for the scratch cells), `sep_scratch_reg_access_cg` (`cp_bank_reg_access`), and the Phase 1 `sep_scratch_reset_cg`. The first reads, the readbacks before the lock, the neighbour compare and the clear and retain readbacks are controls and fill no Phase 3 bin.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>`. Draw all values of both legs and print a `DRAW` line for each draw group. Boot with `+skip_fuse_sense` and wait until `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` is high (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Every access in this procedure must get a response (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check that owns the access). The response code of a write to a read-only register or a reserved field is not stated, so only the arrival of the response is scored.
. `SEP_VERSION_ID`. Read it. It reads 0xDEADBEEF. Error injection at the AXI register write: write 8 random values, among them all-ones, on the drawn lane. The expected response is that the write is ignored (RDL access type R), and a response arrives. After each write read the 64-bit register: bits 31:0 read 0xDEADBEEF (CHK-CC-RO). Bits 63:32 are logged (`OBS-CC-RSVD`).
. `SEP_NMI_VEC` first read. Before any write, read the 64-bit register: 0xC000_0100 (`CTL-NMI-DEFAULT`; the reset value stays graded by the open owners).
. `SEP_NMI_VEC` writes before the lock. Error injection at the AXI register write: write 8 random values on the drawn lane, with bit 0 set in at least 4 and bits 63:32 non-zero in at least 4 (reserved bits). Expected response: the reserved bits are ignored and a response arrives. After each write read the 64-bit register. Bit 0 reads 0 and bits 31:1 equal the model of the stored vector (`CTL-NMI-STORE`). Bits 63:32 are logged (`OBS-CC-RSVD`). A write to the high half alone leaves the stored vector unchanged in the model.
. Lock. Read `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK`: `lock` (bit 0) reads 0. Write 1 and read it: `lock` reads 1. This pair is the control of the lock check, because the readback shows both values.
. `SEP_NMI_VEC` writes after the lock. Error injection at the AXI register write: write 8 random values after the lock, with bits 63:32 non-zero in at least 4. The expected response is that the write is ignored (RDL: the vector cannot change once locked), and a response arrives. After each write read the register: bits 31:1 equal the vector before the write (CHK-NMI-LOCK). Bits 63:32 are logged (`OBS-CC-RSVD`).
. Lock write cases. Error injection at the AXI register write: write 0 to `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK` 3 times and all-ones once (the all-ones write sets reserved bits), in a drawn order and on the drawn lane. Expected response: the lock stays 1, the reserved bits are ignored and a response arrives. After each write read it: `lock` (bit 0) reads 1 (CHK-NMI-LOCK). Bits 63:1 are logged (`OBS-CC-RSVD`).
. `TIMEOUT_CLEAR` and `TIMEOUT_MODE`. Write 8 random values to each, among them all-ones, on the drawn lane. Each write gets a response (CHK-CC-INERT). Then read `TIMEOUT_INTERRUPT` and print `OBS-TIMEOUT`.
. Status registers. Read `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` twice each, separated by an idle gap. Bit 0 of the two reads of a register is equal; a differing pair fails the leaf as a failed precondition, because a changing input cannot show an inert write. Error injection at the AXI register write: for each register write the complement of the bit 0 that it reads, with random upper bits (a write to a read-only register). Expected response: the write is ignored and a response arrives. Read each register again. Bit 0 is unchanged (CHK-CC-INERT). Bits 63:1 are logged (`OBS-CC-RSVD`).
. Scratch leg. Read all sixteen scratch registers (cold bank at 0x1080_2000, warm bank at 0x1080_2080) and keep the 16 low words as the neighbour snapshot.
. For each event of the drawn sequence, in order, do the following.
.. `WRITE_BANK(b)`: write the eight registers of bank `b` in the drawn permutation. Each register gets a unique non-zero 32-bit value and random non-zero bits 63:32, in the drawn shape: one 64-bit lane write, or two 32-bit half writes in the drawn order. The write of the bits 63:32 is error injection at the AXI register write (kind: write to a reserved field with no register field). The expected response is that the upper bits are ignored, the low word is stored and a response arrives. After each register write read that register as 64 bits (CHK-SCR-SHAPE), and read the other 15 registers and compare them with the snapshot (`CTL-SCR-NEIGHBOUR`). Update the snapshot with the written low word. For the cold bank compare the low word of the register with `scratch_cold_probe_o`.
.. `WDT_PULSE`: pulse `wdt_rst_ni` low and release it. Wait until `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` is high (a bounded wait). Read all sixteen registers, print `CTL-SCR-SEQ` for the warm and cold bank (the specification gives the warm bank cleared and the cold bank retained; the readbacks are controls) and keep the readback as the new snapshot.
.. `COLD`: pulse `rst_ni` low and release it. Wait until `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` is high (a bounded wait). Read all sixteen registers, print `CTL-SCR-SEQ` (both banks cleared) and keep the readback as the new snapshot.
. Evaluate every checker and print the summary lines.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CC-RO
|Bits 31:0 (`version_id`) of `SEP_VERSION_ID` read 0xDEADBEEF after every write attempt (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: R, reset 0xDEADBEEF). Bits 63:32 are reserved with no stated read value; the leaf logs them (`OBS-CC-RSVD`) and does not grade them. A DUT that stores a write shows a changed word. Anchor: the real 64-bit read-back through the CPU-LSU master (approved). Control: the first read before any write shows 0xDEADBEEF, so a changed word is attributable to a write. Evidence: `CHK-CC-RO PASS: SEP_VERSION_ID[31:0]=0xdeadbeef after <n> writes` and `OBS-CC-RSVD: reg=<name> reserved=0x<..>`.
|CHK-NMI-LOCK
|After every `SEP_NMI_VEC` write after the lock, bits 31:1 of the 64-bit read equal the vector before the write. A write of 0 to the lock, and a write of all-ones, leave `lock` (bit 0) at 1 (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: `nmi_vec` cannot change once locked, `lock` RW1S). Each locked write that reaches the low half carries bits 31:1 that differ from the stored vector, so a DUT that stores a write after the lock shows a changed vector, and one that clears the lock reads 0. The reserved bits (63:32 of the vector, 63:1 of the lock) have no stated read value; the leaf logs them (`OBS-CC-RSVD`) and does not grade them. The first read and the readbacks before the lock are not part of this check; they stay with `sep_nmi_sanity_test` and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`. Anchor: the real 64-bit read-back (approved). Control: `CTL-NMI-STORE` shows that before the lock the register stores bits 31:1, so an unchanged vector after the lock is not a dead register. `lock` reads 0 before the write of 1 and 1 after it, so the readback can show a clear. Evidence: `CHK-NMI-LOCK PASS: locked_writes=<n> vec=0x<..> unchanged lock write-0 x<k> and all-ones x<j> stay 1`. Control lines: `CTL-NMI-DEFAULT: first_read=0x<..>` and `CTL-NMI-STORE: writes=<n> bit0=0 bits31:1_exact`.
|CHK-CC-INERT
|After a write of the complement of bit 0, bit 0 (the only field) of `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` and of `SMC_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` reads unchanged (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`: R). Bits 63:1 have no stated read value and are logged (`OBS-CC-RSVD`). Every access, including the `TIMEOUT_CLEAR` and `TIMEOUT_MODE` writes, gets a response. A DUT that stores the write flips the bit and fails. The `TIMEOUT_INTERRUPT` read after those writes is a logged observation (`OBS-TIMEOUT`), not a check: `sep_cpu_ctrl_timeout_inert_rand_test` owns that contract with live controls. Anchor: the real read-back (approved). Control: the baseline reads show a stable value, and the complement write drives the opposite level, so a writable bit shows. Evidence: `CHK-CC-INERT PASS: sep_sense=<v> smc_sense=<v> unchanged responses=<n>/<n>` and `OBS-TIMEOUT: timeout_interrupt=0x<..> after TIMEOUT_CLEAR and TIMEOUT_MODE writes`.
|CHK-SCR-SHAPE
|For each bank, order and shape, bits 31:0 of the 64-bit readback of a written scratch register equal the written low word (`sep_scratch.adoc`: `data` RW; bits 63:32 reserved). Bits 63:32 have no stated read value; the leaf logs them (`OBS-CC-RSVD`) and does not grade them. A DUT that drops the low-half write of a two-half write, lets the high-half write or the upper write data reach the `data` field, or steers a lane wrongly shows a wrong low word. Anchor: the real 64-bit read-back (approved), and `scratch_cold_probe_o` for the cold-bank low word. The probe is not independent, because both sides come from the same flops, so it cross-checks the read mux only. Control: the low word reads back the written non-zero value, so the register stores; the neighbour compare (`CTL-SCR-NEIGHBOUR`) and the clear and retain readbacks (`CTL-SCR-SEQ`) are logged and owned by open tests. Evidence: `CHK-SCR-SHAPE PASS: bank=<b> order=<perm> shape=<64\|2x32> low_word=0x<v> upper32_written=0x<u>`. Control lines: `CTL-SCR-SEQ: step=<i> event=<WDT_PULSE\|COLD> warm=<cleared\|retained> cold=<cleared\|retained>` and `CTL-SCR-NEIGHBOUR: bank=<b> index=<i> neighbours_unchanged=<k>/<k>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The effect of the vector at a bark is graded in `sep_nmi_vector_bark_rand_test`. The TRNG select storage and lock are graded in the crypto and entropy area. The test does not claim the hardware effect of `TIMEOUT_CLEAR` and `TIMEOUT_MODE` (the register description marks them as reserved and not implemented), the route of `SEP_SW_DEBUG` to the CLA (no observation point), or the response code of a refused write. The reset value and the write mask of both scratch banks and the clear and retain across a CPU reset and a cold reset stay with `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` and `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`.

'''''

[[sep_timer_clock_contract_test]]
===== 5.22 `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+sep_edn_jtag_reset_waive` for the `sep_reset_n` override pulse of leg L (<<sep-vplan-phase3-edn-waiver>>), `reseed = 4`. Every leaf runs leg R at all five clock ratios and leg W at three prescaler values. The reference counter leg (R), the watchdog threshold leg (T), the wakeup timer leg (W), the bus shape leg (S) and the configuration lock leg (L) all drive the timer registers over the LSU splice and run in one leaf, in this order. Leg L runs last, because clearing `WDOG_REGWEN.regwen` locks the watchdog configuration until the next cold reset. Tool: Verilator and VCS. The `dbg_rstb_i` event of leg L runs on VCS only. Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu_stub`; testlist `system.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_timer_clock_contract_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master on `s_axi` (`SepAxiAgent` over `ocah_axi_vip`, approved) for 32-bit and 64-bit accesses; cocotb clock drive of `clk_ref_i` with a chosen ratio and start phase and of `clk_wdt_i` with a fixed period `P_w` and a chosen start phase, and an edge counter on `clk_i`, `clk_ref_i` and `clk_wdt_i` at the pins (approved); `wdt_timer_rst_req_o`, `rst_ni`, `wdt_rst_ni_i`, `dbg_rstb_i`, `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, the `SW_RESET_N` register, the `ip_rst_n[3]` tap, `jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` and `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` (approved); register model from the generated IP-XACT (`sep_reg_meta`) and the access types of `aon_timer.adoc`. The four adapter stage nets `SEP_CORE.u_sep_wdt_wrap.sep_wdt_tlul_axi_req`, `sep_wdt_tlul_axi_req_offset`, `axi_lite_req` and `tl_d_i` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Only the coverpoint `cp_adapt_stage` reads them. A cell of it hits only on the stage handshake of a 32-bit access whose CHK-WDT-SHAPE readback passes, so CHK-WDT-SHAPE fills it. No inject.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. RANDCFG for the clock configuration of legs R, T and W. Leg R walks the five ratios `k` of {2, 3, 5, 8, 13} (`clk_ref_i` period = `k` x `clk_i` period) in an order that the seed draws; the seed never selects which ratio runs. The start phase of `clk_ref_i` in each ratio pass is 0 to one `clk_ref_i` period. The period `P_w` of `clk_wdt_i` is not drawn: it is fixed at 8 `clk_i` periods, a test parameter that the leaf logs (the open timer tests use the same value), so that a register round trip through the clock crossing completes inside the AXI response timeout. The start phase of `clk_wdt_i` is 0 to one `clk_wdt_i` period. Leg R reloads twice, in a drawn order: `W_lo`, 0 to 2^64 - 2^32 with the low word below 0xFFFF_FFF0, and `W_hi`, 0 to 2^64 - 2^32 with the low word at 0xFFFF_FFF0 or above, so the count carries into the high word. The bark threshold `b` is 32 to 64 and the bite threshold `d` is `b` + 32 to `b` + 64 (`d` above `b`; never 0, never equal, never reversed). A second, larger bark threshold `b2` is `b` + 32 to `b` + 64, and the bite threshold moves to `b2` + (`d` - `b`). The near-2^32 bark threshold `bm` is 0xFFFF_FF00 to 0xFFFF_FF10, the near-2^32 bite threshold `dm` is `bm` + 32 to `bm` + 48, and the preload is `bm` - 32. At least 0xC0 counts stay between `dm` and 2^32, more than the count advances over the passes that follow the bite (below 2 x `D_pass` + `A`, step 1), so no count read of the run reaches the wrap. Leg W runs three prescaler passes in a drawn order: `p` = 0, `p` = 1 and `p` = `p_m`, a drawn value of 2 to 15. The `p` = 0 pass writes the wakeup halves high first, the `p` = 1 pass low first, and the `p_m` pass takes a drawn half order. Wrap and counts above 2^64 - 2^32 are not drawn (not stated). RAND-REP for the register matrices of legs S, W and L: the offset order, the patterns (0, all-ones, walking one, 0xA5A5_A5A5), the idle gaps between count reads (0 to 40 `clk_i` cycles), the order of the five issue gaps of the phase walk in the `k` = 5 pass, the control threshold `Ts` (40 to 200) and gap `g` (16 to 32), the gap `r` past the 32-bit boundary (32 to 48), the non-zero lock values `Xb`, `Xd`, `Xh` and `Xl` of leg L, and the event permutation of leg L with its repeat count (1 to 3). `reseed = 4` samples other orders, start phases, reload values, thresholds, `p_m`, patterns, gaps and permutations. Every leaf runs the five ratios, both reload classes, the three prescaler classes and both half orders. An absolute wakeup expiry time or a ratio between prescalers is not scored.

*Objective*: Leg R. `REFERENCE_COUNTER` counts `clk_ref_i` cycles after cold reset deasserts. After a 64-bit write the readback is at or just above the written value and keeps advancing at the `clk_ref_i` rate (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `REFERENCE_COUNTER`: "the readback is at or just above the written value"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Clock Distribution: `clk_ref_i` is "counted by `REFERENCE_COUNTER`"). Leg T. With `WDOG_CTRL.enable` = 1 the count advances. The short name `aon_timer.adoc` below means `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aon_timer/regs/gen/adoc/aon_timer.adoc`. `INTR_STATE.wdog_timer_bark` sets when the count reaches `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`. `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` rises when it reaches `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` after an unanswered bark, later than the bark (`hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`, Bark Interrupt and Bite Reset; `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`). `INTR_TEST` forces the bark status even when disabled. Leg W. The 64-bit wakeup threshold and count are two 32-bit halves each. `INTR_STATE.wkup_timer_expired` sets when the count reaches the threshold, including across the 32-bit boundary. A larger threshold expires later (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI`, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `WKUP_COUNT_HI`, `WKUP_COUNT_LO`, `INTR_STATE`). Leg S. Watchdog registers answer 32-bit accesses on either 64-bit lane at zero-based offsets 0x00 to 0x34 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`, Bus Interface; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`, row Always-On Timer). Each register returns the value that its access type gives: a read-write register returns the written pattern masked to its field bits, an RW1C register clears the written bits, an RW0C register cannot be set by a write, and a write-only register has an effect only. A `WDOG_COUNT` read while counting is coherent: it never falls. Leg L. Clearing `WDOG_REGWEN.regwen` locks the watchdog configuration. Software cannot set it again. Only the cold reset restores it to 1: the watchdog is in the cold reset domain, and `wdt_rst_ni`, `dbg_rstb_i`, a software engine reset and a `sep_reset_n`-only event do not reset it (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller; `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_REGWEN`). The count unit of `WDOG_COUNT` is not stated by the frozen specification (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The checks grade count relations only. The count rate is logged as `OBS-WDT-RATE`.

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`, which grades that the reference counter runs (`CHK-REFCNT-RUNS`), the wakeup count, expiry, cause, `INTR_TEST` and a prescaler bound (`CHK-WKUP-*`, `CHK-INTR-TEST` on the wakeup bit), the thresholds ignored under the lock (`CHK-REGWEN-LOCK`, `CHK-REGWEN-SCOPE`) and the pet (`CHK-WDOG-PET`); `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`, which grades that the bite request is low before enable and high after an unanswered bark (`CHK-BITE`), the bark NMI and a second bark; `sep_nmi_sanity_test`, which grades the bark to the NMI; `sep_address_map_test`, which reads the reference counter low half twice; and `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`, which excludes the counters and the watchdog control registers. This test grades only the new cells: the reference counter rate, reload and cold-reset restart; the order and the relation of the bark and bite thresholds; the forced bark with the counter far below the threshold; the registers on both lanes with all four access types; the sticky `regwen` and its retention over the other reset events; and the wakeup threshold across the 32-bit boundary. The open owners keep the rest. The small wakeup threshold, the disabled hold of the count and the single threshold run are controls here. Cells: five ratios in every leaf; reload with and without carry in every leaf; cold reset with the counter armed at 2^40 or above; bark and bite with drawn thresholds; a larger bark threshold; thresholds near 2^32; a forced bark with enable 0 and 1; a wakeup run across the 32-bit boundary with two gaps at prescaler 0, 1 and a drawn value of 2 to 15, with both half orders; 11 registers x 4 patterns x both lanes; coherent count reads; regwen set once, four reset events and a cold reset. The retention of `regwen` and of the watchdog and wakeup thresholds over the four events, and their cold default, are graded only here: `sep_cold_reset_phase_test`, `sep_cpu_reset_retention_test`, `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` and `sep_ip_reset_override_matrix_test` log these registers and do not grade them. Functional coverage: `sep_clk_domain_cg` (`cp_domain_crossing`, `cp_xdomain_phase`), `sep_cpu_ctrl_reg_cg` (`cp_refcnt`, `cp_refcnt_ratio`, `cp_refcnt_write_class`), `sep_wdt_config_bark_cg` (`cp_enable_outcome`, `cp_bark_status`, `cp_access_x_register` (the reads of the coherent-read step), `cp_thold_order`, `cp_lock_x_reg` (the sticky and cold-reset cells), and `cp_adapt_stage`), `sep_wdt_wakeup_timer_cg` (`cp_wkup_thold_x_presc`), `sep_reset_scope_retention_cg` (`cp_reset_class`, and the watchdog timer cell of `cp_no_sw_reset_units`), and the Phase 1 `sep_wdt_bark_cg` (`cp_bark`). The controls `CTL-WDT-ENABLE`, `CTL-WDT-THOLD`, `CTL-WDT-PAUSE`, `CTL-WKUP-SMALL`, `CTL-WKUP-CAUSE` and `CTL-REGWEN-LOCKED` fill no Phase 3 bin. The control runs `CTL-WDT-ENABLE` (step 9) and `CTL-WKUP-SMALL` (step 13), and the recovery reset of a watchdog-run baseline (`OBS-WDT-BITE-HOLD`, step 5), run with the graded window closed. The split-order and expiry bins of `cp_wkup_thold_x_presc` sample only the runs with the crossing class (runs 1 and 2).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>`. Draw every value and print one `DRAW` line for each group. Start `clk_ref_i` at the first ratio of the drawn order and its drawn start phase, and `clk_wdt_i` at the period `P_w` and its drawn start phase. Boot with `+skip_fuse_sense` and wait until `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` is high (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Every wait in this procedure is a bounded wait, and a timeout fails the check that owns it. Registers `REFERENCE_COUNTER`, `WDOG_*`, `WKUP_*` and `INTR_*` cross clock domains. Each readback that follows a write is polled until its field bits equal the expected value (until it is at or above the written value for `REFERENCE_COUNTER`), and the poll count is logged. Every compare and poll of an `aon_timer.adoc` register in this leaf masks the read to the field bits of the register description. The reserved bits have no stated read value, so the leaf logs them (`OBS-WDT-RSVD: reg=<name> raw=0x<..>`) and does not grade them. An allowance `a` applies to the edge comparisons of leg R. It is a small number of `clk_ref_i` edges, an environment choice that the leaf logs. The specification gives no tolerance. A second allowance `A` of 4 `clk_wdt_i` counts, an environment choice that the leaf logs, applies to every compare in legs T and W that sets a status or a bite request against a count read: the count read is a copy that crosses clock domains and can lag the true count, so a status that is set while the count read is up to `A` below its threshold is not a fault. Every threshold gap and preload distance of a graded run that the draws give is at least 8 times `A`. For each run the leaf logs `D_pass`, the count advance over the longest poll pass, and it fails as a failed precondition if 2 x `D_pass` is not below the smallest threshold gap of the leg minus `A`. The count read of the pass that first sees an event can trail the event by up to two passes (the status read can miss the event by one pass, and the count read comes after the status read), so with a larger `D_pass` the compares could not tell a wrong threshold from the right one.
. Leg R, rate. Run this step once for each ratio, in the drawn order. Before each pass after the first, stop `clk_ref_i` in its low phase with no `REFERENCE_COUNTER` access in flight, and start it at the next ratio and its drawn start phase. Read `REFERENCE_COUNTER` as one 64-bit access. Wait 400 `clk_ref_i` periods (a test parameter). Read it again. Count the `clk_ref_i` rising edges `E` and the `clk_i` cycles `N` at the pins between the two read-data samples. `delta` is the difference of the two reads (CHK-REFCNT-RATE, one line per ratio). In the `k` = 5 pass, then issue five more single reads of `REFERENCE_COUNTER`, each after an idle gap of 0, 1, 2, 3 or 4 `clk_i` cycles in the drawn order, so that the completing `clk_i` edge takes every position inside a `clk_ref_i` period (the phase walk). Count the `clk_ref_i` rising edges at the pin between the read-data samples of consecutive walk reads (CHK-REFCNT-PHASE). The reload and cold-reset steps of leg R and the later legs run at the last ratio of the order.
. Leg R, reload. Run this step twice, with `W` = `W_lo` and with `W` = `W_hi`, in the drawn order. Write `W` as one 64-bit access. Poll until the readback is at or above `W`. The first readback is `R_w`. Count the `clk_ref_i` edges `E_w` from the write response to the first readback. Take 16 further reads. Wait until at least 32 `clk_ref_i` edges have passed since the write, and read once more. When the low word of `W` is 0xFFFF_FFF0 or above, the high word of this read is the written high word plus 1 (CHK-REFCNT-WRITE). A 32-bit half write is not made (the register description states no reload rule for it).
. Leg R, cold reset. Write 2^40 and poll until the readback is at or above 2^40 (CHK-REFCNT-RESET). Log the armed readback. Pulse `rst_ni` low and release it. Count the `clk_ref_i` edges `E_r` from the release. Wait until `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` is high. Read the counter: the first read is `R_r`. Take 16 further reads. The last read is above `R_r`.
. Leg T, watchdog run. A run takes a bark threshold `bx`, a bite threshold `dx` and a preload `c0`. It has five parts. (a) Baseline: write `WDOG_CTRL` = 0, write 1 to `INTR_STATE.wdog_timer_bark`, write `WDOG_COUNT` = `c0`, write `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` = `bx` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` = `dx`, and poll until `WDOG_COUNT` reads `c0`, `INTR_STATE.wdog_timer_bark` reads 0 and `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` is 0. If `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` is still 1 at the end of the bounded wait, print `OBS-WDT-BITE-HOLD`, pulse `rst_ni` low and release it, wait for `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, and write the baseline again. (b) Enable: write `WDOG_CTRL` = 1 and record the time of the write response. (c) Poll loop. Each pass samples `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` at the address handshake of a read of `INTR_STATE`, then reads `INTR_STATE`, then reads `WDOG_COUNT`. (d) Stop when a pass shows the bark status set, `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` high and a count read at or above `dx`. (e) Record the time of the first pass that reads the bark status set, the count read of that pass, and the count read of the stop pass.
. Leg T, base run. Run with `bx` = `b`, `dx` = `d`, `c0` = 0 (CHK-WDT-ORDER, CHK-WDT-THOLD-MONO). From the first and the last count read of this run print `OBS-WDT-RATE` with the count difference and the `clk_wdt_i` rising edges counted at the pin between the two reads.
. Leg T, larger bark threshold. Run with `bx` = `b2`, `dx` = `b2` + (`d` - `b`), `c0` = 0 (CHK-WDT-ORDER, CHK-WDT-THOLD-MONO). Record the count read of the first pass that reads the bark status set, as in step 5. Print the time from the enable response to that pass as `OBS-WDT-BARK-TIME`, with the same time of the base run.
. Leg T, thresholds near 2^32. Run with `bx` = `bm`, `dx` = `dm`, `c0` = `bm` - 32, written while the timer is disabled (CHK-WDT-MAX).
. Leg T, enable control (`CTL-WDT-ENABLE`). Do the baseline with `bx` = 0xFFFF_0000, `dx` = 0xFFFF_0010, `c0` = 0. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 1. Poll until a count read is at least 2. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 0. Wait 64 `clk_wdt_i` periods (a test parameter) and read `WDOG_COUNT`. Wait 64 periods and read it again. The two reads are equal and the bark status reads 0. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 1. After 64 periods a read is larger.
. Leg T, forced bark. Do the baseline with the values of step 9. With enable 0, write `INTR_TEST.wdog_timer_bark` = 1. Poll `INTR_STATE` until the bark status reads 1. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.wdog_timer_bark` and poll until it reads 0. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 1 (the count is far below `bx`). Repeat the force and the clear. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 0. Sample `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` on every `clk_i` rising edge from the first force to the end of the clear (CHK-WDT-INTRTEST).
. Leg T, `pause_in_sleep` and `ALERT_TEST`. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 2 and poll until it reads 2. Write 0 and poll until it reads 0 (`CTL-WDT-PAUSE`). The specification states that the CPU must reset the counter periodically, and it names no register for it and no effect after a bark, so no pet is made. Write 1 to `ALERT_TEST` and wait for the response (CHK-WDT-ALERT-ACCESS).
. Leg W, wakeup run. Leg W runs three prescaler passes (`p` = 0, `p` = 1, `p` = `p_m`) in the drawn order. Each pass runs steps 13 to 15 with the `p` and the half order of the pass, then writes `WKUP_CTRL` = 0 and clears the status. A run takes a threshold `(thi, tlo)` and a count preload `(chi, clo)`. It has four parts. (a) Write `WKUP_CTRL` = `p` x 2 (enable 0). (b) Write the two threshold halves and the two count halves with `WKUP_CTRL.enable` = 0, in the half order of the pass, and poll each readback until it equals the written half. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.wkup_timer_expired` and poll until it reads 0. (c) Write `WKUP_CTRL` = `p` x 2 + 1 and record the time of the write response. (d) Poll loop with back-to-back reads. Each pass reads `INTR_STATE`, then reads `WKUP_COUNT_HI`, `WKUP_COUNT_LO` and `WKUP_COUNT_HI` again; a pass whose two high reads differ is read again. Stop at the first pass that reads the expired status set. The count read of that pass is the 64-bit value from the two high reads and the low read that follow the status read. The expiry time is the time of the status read. A threshold at or below the count is not drawn (the result is not stated).
. Leg W, control run (`CTL-WKUP-SMALL`, class SMALL). Run with threshold (0, `Ts`) and preload (0, `Ts` - `g`). Write `WKUP_CTRL` = 0 and clear the status.
. Leg W, run 1. Run with threshold (1, `r`) and preload (0, 0xFFFF_FFF0). Record the count read at expiry `n1` and the expiry time `t1` (CHK-WKUP-WIDE). Between runs write `WKUP_CTRL` = 0, reload the count below the threshold of the next run, clear `INTR_STATE.wkup_timer_expired` and read it as 0.
. Leg W, run 2. Run with threshold (1, 2 x `r`), the same preload, the same `p` and the same half order. Record the count read at expiry `n2` and the expiry time `t2` (CHK-WKUP-WIDE, CHK-WKUP-LATER). Print `OBS-WKUP-TIME` with `t1` and `t2`. Write `WKUP_CTRL` = 0.
. Leg S, baseline. Read `WKUP_CAUSE`. After the wakeup expiries of leg W it reads 1 (`CTL-WKUP-CAUSE`; `aon_timer.adoc`, `WKUP_CAUSE`: the timer requested a wakeup; the open owner `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test` grades the same set). A read of 0 fails the leaf, because the read of the bit is then not live. Write 0 to `WKUP_CAUSE` and poll until it reads 0. The walk starts from a known state.
. Leg S, walk. Do this with the watchdog timer and the wakeup timer disabled. For every pattern `P` of {0, all-ones, walking one, 0xA5A5_A5A5} and for each register of {`WKUP_CTRL`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI`, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `WKUP_COUNT_HI`, `WKUP_COUNT_LO`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`}, in the drawn offset order, write `P` as a 32-bit access at the zero-based offset of the register, with the strobes of the lane that address bit 2 selects (the offsets 0x4, 0xC, 0x14, 0x24 select the high half, the others the low half). For `WKUP_CTRL` clear bit 0 of `P`. Poll the readback until its field bits equal the expected value: bits 12:0 equal `P` & 0x1FFE for `WKUP_CTRL`, and the 32-bit field equals `P` for the others (CHK-WDT-SHAPE; the four threshold registers are `CTL-WDT-THOLD`). For `WKUP_CAUSE` write `P` and read `cause` (bit 0) as 0.
. Leg S, interrupt registers. For every `P`: write all-ones to `INTR_STATE`; write `P` to `INTR_TEST`; poll `INTR_STATE` until bits 1:0 read `P` & 0x3; write all-ones to `INTR_TEST`; poll until bits 1:0 read 0x3; write `P` to `INTR_STATE`; poll until bits 1:0 read 0x3 & ~`P`; write all-ones to `INTR_STATE`. `INTR_TEST` is never read.
. Leg S, `WDOG_CTRL` walk. Write `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` = 0xFFFF_FFF0 and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` = 0xFFFF_FFF8 (far above the count) and `WDOG_COUNT` = 0. For every pattern `P` write `P` to `WDOG_CTRL` and poll until bits 1:0 read `P` & 0x3. A pattern with bit 0 set enables the timer. At the end write `WDOG_CTRL` = 0.
. Leg S, coherent reads. Write `WDOG_COUNT` = 0 with the timer disabled, poll until it reads 0, and write `WDOG_CTRL` = 1. Take 32 reads of `WDOG_COUNT` with random idle gaps. After read 8 and after read 24, with the timer enabled and counting, read `WDOG_CTRL` between two count reads (bits 1:0 read 1, the written value; CHK-WDT-COHERENT). After read 16, with the timer enabled and counting, write `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` with new values at or above 0xFFFF_0000 (bite above bark, both far above the count), and read each back (`CTL-WDT-THOLD`). After read 32, write `WDOG_CTRL` = 0, write `WDOG_COUNT` = 0x1000, and poll until it reads 0x1000. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 1. Take 32 more reads. No write to the count is made while the timer counts. Write `WDOG_CTRL` = 0 (CHK-WDT-COHERENT).
. Leg L, lock. Read `WDOG_REGWEN.regwen`: it reads 1. Write two drawn non-zero values `Xb` and `Xd` (they differ from the reset value 0) to `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`, and two drawn non-zero values `Xh` and `Xl` to `WKUP_THOLD_HI` and `WKUP_THOLD_LO` (the wakeup timer is disabled). They land (poll until the readbacks equal them). Write 0 to `regwen` and poll until it reads 0. Write 1 to `regwen`, wait 64 `clk_wdt_i` periods and read it (CHK-REGWEN-STICKY). Error injection at the AXI register write: write two other values to the thresholds while locked. Expected response: ignored (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_REGWEN`). The response code is not stated. Read the thresholds after the wait: they equal `Xb` and `Xd` (`CTL-REGWEN-LOCKED`).
. Leg L, events. Apply the drawn permutation of the four events: `wdt_rst_ni` low then high; `dbg_rstb_i` low then high (VCS only); a `SW_RESET_N` pulse (write 0 to `hmac_sw_rst_n`, wait for the `ip_rst_n[3]` tap to fall, write 1, wait for it to rise); a `sep_reset_n` override pulse (`jtag_sep_reset_n_ovrd_i` = 1 with `jtag_sep_reset_n_val_i` = 0 for a test-chosen number of cycles, then the override 0, then wait for `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` high), and repeat it the drawn number of times. After each event read `regwen` and the four thresholds, wait 64 `clk_wdt_i` periods and read them again (CHK-REGWEN-HOLD).
. Leg L, cold reset. Pulse `rst_ni` low and release it. Wait for `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`. `regwen` reads 1 and the four thresholds read 0. A write of a drawn value to `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` lands (CHK-REGWEN-COLD).
. Evaluate every checker and print the summary lines.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-REFCNT-RATE
|The counter delta equals the `clk_ref_i` edge count at the pin within the allowance `a`, and differs from the `clk_i` cycle count by more than 25 percent (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `REFERENCE_COUNTER`; `clk_rst.adoc`, Clock Distribution). A counter on `clk_i` fails when `k` >= 2. A stuck counter fails. Anchor: the real 64-bit reads and the edge counters at the pins (approved). Control: the ratio `k` differs from 1 in every leaf, so a `clk_i` counter gives a different delta. Evidence: `CHK-REFCNT-RATE PASS: k=<k> delta=<d> E_ref=<e> abs_diff=<m> clk_i_cycles=<n>`.
|CHK-REFCNT-PHASE
|In the phase walk of the `k` = 5 pass, each of the five reads is at or above the read before it, and the difference of two consecutive reads equals the `clk_ref_i` edge count at the pin between their read-data samples within the allowance `a` (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `REFERENCE_COUNTER`; `clk_rst.adoc`, Clock Distribution: `clk_ref_i` is counted by `REFERENCE_COUNTER`). The completing `clk_i` edge of the five reads falls at five positions of the `clk_ref_i` period, so a read that crosses the clock domain without a coherent copy shows a fall or a jump at one position. A DUT that returns a torn or a stale count fails. Anchor: the real 64-bit reads and the edge counter at the pin (approved). Control: CHK-REFCNT-RATE shows the counter advancing at the `clk_ref_i` rate in the same pass. Evidence: `CHK-REFCNT-PHASE PASS: k=5 gaps=<order> deltas=<list> E_ref=<list> monotone=1 max_abs_diff=<m> allowance=<a>`.
|CHK-REFCNT-WRITE
|After the 64-bit write the first readback is at or above `W` and within the edges since the write plus the allowance, later reads are non-decreasing, and a carry from the low word appears in the high word when the low word of `W` is 0xFFFF_FFF0 or above (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `REFERENCE_COUNTER`). A DUT that ignores the write reads far below `W` and fails the bounded poll. A DUT that drops the carry shows the old high word. Anchor: the real reads (approved). Control: the count of `clk_ref_i` edges since the write bounds the first readback from above, so a free-running counter that ignores the write fails. Evidence: `CHK-REFCNT-WRITE PASS: W=0x<..> first_readback=0x<..> polls=<p> monotone=1 carry=<0\|1>`.
|CHK-REFCNT-RESET
|Before the `rst_ni` pulse a readback of the armed counter is at or above 2^40, or the check fails. After the release the first read is below 2^40 and within the edges since the release plus the allowance, and later reads increase (`sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `REFERENCE_COUNTER`: counting after deassert of cold reset). A DUT that keeps the count through cold reset reads at or above 2^40. Anchor: the real reads and the edge counter (approved). Control: the armed readback at or above 2^40 proves that the counter held a value that a reset must clear. Evidence: `CHK-REFCNT-RESET PASS: armed_readback=0x<..> first_read=0x<..> E_since_release=<e>`.
|CHK-WDT-ORDER
|In the base run and in the larger-threshold run, no pass shows the bark status set while the count read after it is below `bx` - `A` (the allowance for the count copy that lags; step 1). After a count read at or above `bx`, the bark status reads set within a bounded number of further passes (an environment allowance, logged). No pass shows `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` high while the bark status reads 0, or while the count read after it is below `dx` - `A`. The bite request rises after the bark, when the count read has reached `dx`, and no earlier (`watchdog.adoc`, Bark Interrupt and Bite Reset; `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`). The count is non-decreasing, so a read taken after the status read bounds the count at the status read from above. A DUT that sets the bark early, never sets it, or raises the bite before the bark fails: every threshold gap is at least 8 times `A`, so an early event reads far below its threshold. Anchor: the real reads and `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` (approved). Control: the bite request rises later in the same leaf, so "bite low before the bark" has a live control. Evidence: one line for each run, `CHK-WDT-ORDER PASS: run=<base\|larger> bark=<b> bite=<d> allowance=<A> count_at_bark=<c> bark_early=0 bite_with_bark_clear=0 bite_early=0 count_at_bite=<c2>`. Control and observation lines: `CTL-WDT-ENABLE: disabled_at_count=<c> reads=0x<..>,0x<..> equal bark=0 reenabled_advance=1` and `OBS-WDT-RATE: dcount=<d> E_wdt=<e>`, which is not graded.
|CHK-WDT-THOLD-MONO
|A larger bark threshold gives a later bark in count: the count read at the bark pass of the larger-threshold run is at or above `b2` - `A`, and so above the count read at the bark pass of the base run (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`). Both runs start from a count of 0 with the timer disabled and use the same poll loop. A DUT that ignores the new threshold sets the bark near `b`, reads far below `b2` - `A` (`b2` - `b` is at least 32), and fails. The bark times are logged as `OBS-WDT-BARK-TIME` and are not graded, because a time compare has no margin that the specification gives. Anchor: the count reads of the bark passes (approved). Control: CHK-WDT-ORDER shows the bark following the threshold in the base run. Evidence: `CHK-WDT-THOLD-MONO PASS: b=<b> count_at_bark=<c> b2=<b2> count_at_bark2=<c2> allowance=<A> later=1`.
|CHK-WDT-MAX
|With thresholds near 2^32 and a preloaded count 32 below the bark threshold, no pass shows the bark status set while the count read is below `bm` - `A`. The bark status and the bite request follow the count reads that reach `bm` and `dm`, with no wrap, within the allowances of CHK-WDT-ORDER (a bite request with the count read below `dm` - `A` fails) (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`: 32-bit fields). A DUT that truncates the threshold fires early or never. Anchor: the real reads and `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` (approved). Control: the count reads are non-decreasing and at or above the preload, so a wrap shows as a fall. Evidence: `CHK-WDT-MAX PASS: b=<b> d=<d> count_at_bark=<c> count_at_bite=<c2> early=0`.
|CHK-WDT-INTRTEST
|`INTR_TEST` sets the bark status with enable at 0 and at 1, the W1C write clears it, and `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` stays 0 (`aon_timer.adoc`, `INTR_TEST`, `INTR_STATE`). A DUT that needs enable for the force, or that raises the bite request on a forced bark, fails. Anchor: the real `INTR_STATE` reads and `wdt_timer_rst_req_o` sampled on every `clk_i` rising edge (approved). Control: CHK-WDT-ORDER shows the bite request rising in the same leaf, and the count is far below `bx`, so the status is not a threshold crossing. Evidence: `CHK-WDT-INTRTEST PASS: enable=<0\|1> status=1 cleared=1 bite=0`.
|CHK-WDT-ALERT-ACCESS
|The `ALERT_TEST` write completes with a response (`aon_timer.adoc`, `ALERT_TEST`). The effect of the write is not graded (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A DUT that hangs on the write fails the bounded wait. Anchor: the AXI response (approved). Control: the preceding register accesses of the leg complete in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-WDT-ALERT-ACCESS PASS: response=<r>`. Control line: `CTL-WDT-PAUSE: write1_read=<v> write0_read=<v>`, which is not graded.
|CHK-WKUP-WIDE
|The expired status reads 0 before enable and while the count is below the threshold, and 1 after expiry with the count at or above the threshold, including across the 32-bit boundary (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI`, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `WKUP_COUNT_HI`, `WKUP_COUNT_LO`, `INTR_STATE`). Concretely, in run 1 and run 2, no pass shows the status set while the count read after it is below the threshold - `A` (the allowance for the count copy that lags; step 1), and the status sets within a bounded wait. A DUT that compares only the low half expires at the wrong count, far below the threshold. Anchor: the real status and count reads (approved). Control: the status reads 0 before enable and 1 after expiry in the control run of the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-WKUP-WIDE PASS: p=<p> run=<1\|2> class=CROSS32 thr=0x<hi>_<lo> preload=0x<hi>_<lo> allowance=<A> status_before=0 count_at_expiry=0x<..>`. Control line: `CTL-WKUP-SMALL: p=<p> thr=0x0_<Ts> preload=0x0_<Ts-g> status_before=0 status_after=1`.
|CHK-WKUP-LATER
|The run with gap 2r expires later in count than the run with gap r: the count read at the expiry of run 2 is at or above (1, 2r) - `A`, and so above the count read at the expiry of run 1 (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`, `INTR_STATE`). The expiry of each run is taken from `INTR_STATE.wkup_timer_expired` polled with back-to-back reads from the enable, and the status is cleared and read as 0 between the runs. A DUT that ignores the new threshold expires near (1, r), reads far below (1, 2r) - `A` (`r` is at least 32), and fails. The expiry times are logged as `OBS-WKUP-TIME` and are not graded. Anchor: the count and status reads (approved). Control: the status reads 0 between the runs, so the second expiry is a new event. Evidence: `CHK-WKUP-LATER PASS: p=<p> r=<r> n1=0x<..> n2=0x<..> allowance=<A> status_between=0`.
|CHK-WDT-SHAPE
|On both lanes the field bits of every readback of `WKUP_CTRL`, `WKUP_COUNT_HI`, `WKUP_COUNT_LO` and `WDOG_CTRL` equal the masked pattern, bits 1:0 of `INTR_STATE` equal the cleared-bit result, `INTR_TEST` forces the status, and `WKUP_CAUSE.cause` reads 0 (`aon_timer.adoc`, field access types). The reserved bits have no stated read value; the leaf logs them (`OBS-WDT-RSVD`) and does not grade them. A DUT that drops a field bit, sets an RW0C bit by a write or loses a lane fails. Anchor: the real 32-bit reads on the lane that the address selects (approved). Control: a pattern written to a read-write register reads back different from its reset value, so a dead register fails. For `WKUP_CAUSE`, `CTL-WKUP-CAUSE` of step 16 reads the bit at 1 after the wakeup expiries of the same leaf, so a read of the bit that is stuck at 0 fails the leaf. Evidence: `CHK-WDT-SHAPE PASS: regs=<n> lanes=2 patterns=<p> exact types=<RW\|RW1C\|W\|RW0C>`, `CTL-WKUP-CAUSE: before_clear=1 after_clear=0` and `CTL-WDT-THOLD: reg=<name> lane=<l> pattern=0x<p> readback=0x<r> timer=<enabled\|disabled>`.
|CHK-WDT-COHERENT
|The count reads are non-decreasing within each segment, the first read after the re-enable is at least the written count 0x1000, and at least three reads show an increase, so the check can fail. Every `WDOG_CTRL` read taken between two count reads of the counting segment has `enable` = 1 and `pause_in_sleep` = 0, the written value (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_CTRL`; bits 31:2 are logged). Non-decreasing reads follow from a counter that counts up while it is enabled and is not written (`hw/sys/sep/doc/watchdog.adoc`, introduction: the timer counts up; `aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_COUNT`: the current watchdog counter value). A DUT that tears a count read or serves a value older than an earlier read shows a fall. Anchor: the real reads (approved). Control: the increases prove that the count moves during the read segments. Evidence: `CHK-WDT-COHERENT PASS: reads=<n> segments=2 monotone=1 increases=<k> ctrl_reads=<c> ctrl=1`.
|CHK-REGWEN-STICKY
|After `regwen` clears, a write of 1 leaves it at 0 (`aon_timer.adoc`, `WDOG_REGWEN`: RW0C, locked until the next reset). A DUT that sets it again reads 1. Anchor: the real read after the wait (approved). Control: `regwen` reads 1 before the clear, so the readback shows both values. Evidence: `CHK-REGWEN-STICKY PASS: before=1 write1_after_clear regwen=0`.
|CHK-REGWEN-HOLD
|After each event `regwen` reads 0 and the four thresholds (`WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI`, `WKUP_THOLD_LO`) equal the values written before the lock (`hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture: the watchdog uses cold reset; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). This check is the one owner of the watchdog retention across a `wdt_rst_ni` pulse, a `dbg_rstb_i` pulse, a software engine reset and a `sep_reset_n` override. A DUT that resets the watchdog at an event shows `regwen` at 1 or thresholds at 0. Anchor: the real reads (approved). Control: CHK-REGWEN-COLD shows the same registers returning to their defaults at the cold reset of the same leaf, so the readback can change. Evidence: `CHK-REGWEN-HOLD PASS: events=<list> regwen=0 bark=0x<..> bite=0x<..> wkup_hi=0x<..> wkup_lo=0x<..> unchanged`.
|CHK-REGWEN-COLD
|After the cold reset `regwen` reads 1, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI` and `WKUP_THOLD_LO` read 0, and a write of a drawn non-zero value to `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` lands (`aon_timer.adoc`, Reset column of `WDOG_REGWEN`, `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, `WDOG_BITE_THOLD`, `WKUP_THOLD_HI` and `WKUP_THOLD_LO`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, Reset Architecture: the watchdog uses cold reset). A DUT that keeps the lock or a threshold through the cold reset fails. This check is the one owner of the cold default of these registers. Anchor: the real reads (approved). Control: before the reset `regwen` reads 0 and the thresholds read their armed values (the last reads of CHK-REGWEN-HOLD), so the registers held a state that a reset must clear. Evidence: `CHK-REGWEN-COLD PASS: regwen=1 bark=0 bite=0 wkup_hi=0 wkup_lo=0 write_lands=1 armed_before=<m>/<m>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the count unit of `WDOG_COUNT`, a threshold written below the running count, the ordering and zero-value rules of the thresholds, the pet (the register write that resets the counter, and its effect after a bark), sleep behaviour, the alert receiver idle encoding and the effect of an `ALERT_TEST` write, which registers the lock gates, 32-bit half writes of `REFERENCE_COUNTER` (the register description states no reload rule for them), or counter wrap. Only the two thresholds and `regwen` are scored under the lock, and the response code of a write while locked is not stated. The `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS.wdt` latch is not graded: the specification states no access that makes the watchdog bridge return an error (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs of tested features>>). The reference counter run, the wakeup count and expiry, the thresholds ignored under the lock and the pet stay with `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`. The bite request before enable and after an unanswered bark, and the bark to the NMI, stay with `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` and `sep_nmi_sanity_test`.

'''''
[[sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test]]
===== 5.23 `sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu` (only the CPU LSU reaches the ROM port with data loads), Verilator and VCS, the staged fuse image holds `ROM_CTL` = 0, `reseed = 4`

*Source Module*: `sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU firmware on the CPU LSU path, with the values logged on the outbound mailbox (approved). ROM image model: a test-owned table of 8192 64-bit words that the bench writes into the ROM macro through `tb_backdoor_mem` before reset release, little-endian (approved). The firmware and its parameter block are staged in SRAM through the same backdoor (approved). `rst_ni`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` (approved). The ROM request and response taps `rom_req` and `rom_rsp`, the ROM macro request `rom_macro_req` and `rom_macro_addr` (the outputs of the ROM interface shim, which the adopter places outside SEP), and the LSU request taps `lsu_rom_req` and `lsu_xbar_req` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Added hook (error inject): the ROM macro bit-flip hook (word address, bit mask), which the test adds to the inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, in this draw order: image, probes, stores, flips, reset offset. Dimensions: the 8192 image words (words 0 to 15 hold a fixed boot stub with distinct words), the probe addresses, the sizes (1, 2 and 4 bytes, aligned), the base word of the sequential run (0 to 7680, so the 512 words stay inside the 8192-word aperture), the store addresses, sizes and data, the flip words (16 to 8190, so both neighbours of a flipped word are ROM words outside the boot stub) and bit positions, and the reset offset as a fraction `u_o` in [0,1), scaled in step 14 to o = floor(`u_o` times W) once W is measured. The leaf reads a seed-drawn subset of the aperture (the 512 seeded addresses of step 9 and the 512-word sequential run of step 10) and the fixed boundary words (the top word in step 7, word 0 and the 13 walking-one words in step 8). No leaf reads every word: the walking-one cells cover every word-address bit in every leaf, and the drawn words change with the seed. `reseed = 4` samples four images, four probe plans and four reset offsets. The leaf runs one reset in flight at its drawn offset. The ROM path of `cp_otf_reset_x_path` is one cell with no offset-third axis: the ROM interface answers in the `clk_i` cycle after the request, so W is one cycle and o is 0 in every leaf. The image is a test-owned input: the specification states no ROM content. A 64-bit load is not drawn.

*Objective*: With the staged fuse image holding `ROM_CTL` = 0 (little-endian, no row swap), every 8-byte word of the 64 KiB ROM aperture returns the loaded image word for read sizes 1, 2 and 4 bytes, at word address (addr - 0x1004_0000) >> 3. A byte or halfword store receives a response and does not reach the macro. A bit flip in a stored word returns the stored data with OKAY, because the ROM has no ECC and no integrity sideband. A cold reset that lands with a ROM access in flight leaves the interface usable. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc` (Boot ROM, ROM Architecture, Hardware Configuration and Integrity); `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc` (`ROM_CTL`); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_efuse_map.adoc` (`ROM_CTL`); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (boot ROM window); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`sep_boot_rom_req_o`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller); `hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_boot_rom.rdl`.

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`, which grades 32-bit loads of ten words (the base word, the top valid word and a few between), the exact image literal and one word store that leaves the ROM unchanged. `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` and `sep_rom_sanity_test` grade the instruction fetch. `sep_axi_map_refuse_test` grades that the address one past the ROM is refused. This test grades only the new cells: reads of 1 and 2 bytes (with the base and top words), the walking-one words, a seed-drawn set of words, byte and halfword stores, a flipped stored bit and a reset in flight on the ROM. The 32-bit loads of the base and top words and the word store are controls here. The open owners keep the rest. Cells: the top word 8191 at sizes 1, 2 and 4 at every aligned byte offset; a walking-one sweep over the 13 bits of the word index and every aligned byte offset at sizes 1, 2 and 4; 512 seeded addresses; a sequential run; back-to-back loads; an SRAM word load as the control for the LSU taps; 16 byte and halfword stores; 1-bit and 2-bit flips in stored words; `rst_ni` at one drawn offset of the ROM response window W. Functional coverage: `sep_mem_aperture_cg` (`cp_mem_x_class`, `cp_access_size`, `cp_macro_if` (the ROM cells), `cp_otf_reset_x_path` (the ROM cells)), `sep_documented_absence_cg` (`cp_rom_write_master`, `cp_rom_write_response` (the byte and halfword stores)) and the Phase 1 group `sep_boot_rom_cg` (`cp_lsu`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Draw the image, the probes, the stores and the flips from `SepSeededRng` in the stated order. Build the ROM image of 8192 64-bit words: words 0 to 15 hold the fixed boot stub that jumps to the firmware in SRAM, and every other word is a draw.
. Before reset release, write the image into the ROM macro through `tb_backdoor_mem`, and stage the firmware and its parameter block (the probe plan) in SRAM through the same backdoor. The firmware executes from SRAM, so only its data loads use the LSU ROM path.
. Release the reset. The CPU fetches the stub from the ROM base and jumps to the firmware. The bench records the response window W of the fetch of the stub word at word address 1: the `clk_i` cycles from its `rom_req` handshake to its `rom_rsp` beat, with no reset applied.
. The firmware prints `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for each draw group. It reads `ROM_CTL`, logs it and stops with a fail marker if it is not 0x0.
. From this step to the end of the leaf, the bench samples `lsu_rom_req`, `rom_req`, `rom_rsp` and `lsu_xbar_req` on every clock.
. The firmware loads one drawn SRAM word as the control for the LSU taps.
. The firmware reads the top word 8191 at sizes 1, 2 and 4 and at every aligned byte offset (14 loads) and logs the address, the size and the value of each load on the outbound mailbox.
. Walking-one sweep: for the word 0 and for each of the 13 words whose index has one bit set, the firmware reads the word at sizes 1, 2 and 4 and at every aligned byte offset (14 cells for each word) and logs each load.
. The firmware reads 512 seeded addresses across the aperture at drawn sizes and logs each load.
. The firmware reads a sequential run of 512 consecutive words from a drawn base word (0 to 7680), at a drawn size, and logs each load.
. The firmware issues eight blocks of 16 consecutive load instructions at drawn addresses and sizes, with no other instruction between the loads of a block, and logs each load.
