. Error injection. Point: CPU LSU. Kind: byte and halfword stores to the ROM aperture. Expected response: a response arrives, the macro word is unchanged and the store makes no ROM macro request (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture). The bench samples `rom_macro_req` and `rom_macro_addr` on every clock of this step. For each of 16 stores in the drawn order, the firmware reads the target word as two 32-bit loads, stores a byte or a halfword at a drawn aligned address with data that differs from the image bytes in the stored lanes, waits for the store to complete, logs whether the store retired or trapped (the trap handler logs `mcause` and resumes after the store), and reads the word a second time as two 32-bit loads. The host fails the check if the completion marker of a store does not appear within a bounded wait.
. Error injection. Point: ROM macro array. Kind: a 1-bit flip in each of 8 stored words and a 2-bit flip in each of 8 other stored words, drawn from words 16 to 8190, with no two flipped words adjacent. Expected response: a load returns the stored (flipped) data with OKAY and no error (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, Hardware Configuration and Integrity). The firmware logs `FLIP_READY` and waits for the bench to set a release flag in SRAM (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-ROM-NOECC). The bench applies the flips through the ROM macro bit-flip hook and sets the flag. The firmware executes one `ecall` (the trap handler counts it and resumes after it), then reads each flipped word as two 32-bit loads, and the words before and after it, and logs each load and the count of the traps that it takes.
. Error injection. Point: `rst_ni`. Kind: a cold reset that lands with a ROM access in flight. Expected response: after the release, the first `rom_req` handshake is at word address 0 with no replay of the abandoned fetch, and its `rom_rsp` beat carries image word 0; beats before the release are not scored (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). The anchor access is the fetch of the stub word at word address 1, so a stale beat carries a different word from the first fetch after the release. At the drawn offset o (log `u_o`, W and o): apply a setup reset (`rst_ni` low until `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` is seen low, then high, a bounded wait); take the `rom_req` handshake of the stub word at word address 1; lower `rst_ni` o cycles after it, before its `rom_rsp` beat; raise `rst_ni`; wait for `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` high (a bounded wait); wait for the first `rom_rsp` beat after the release (a bounded wait).
. The bench evaluates every checker from the logged values, the image model and the tap samples.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROM-SWEEP
|Every logged load equals the image bytes of its address and size: bytes `[8*off +: 8*size]` of the 64-bit image word at word index (addr - 0x1004_0000) >> 3, with `off` the byte offset in the word (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_efuse_map.adoc`, `ROM_CTL`: bit 0 = 0 selects little-endian, swap field 0 selects no swap). The `ROM_CTL` read of step 4 must be 0x0; any other value fails the check, because the expected bytes then do not hold. The boundary words (word 0, the 13 walking-one words and the top word 8191) are each read at sizes 1, 2 and 4 at every aligned byte offset, and the seeded addresses and the sequential run read a seed-drawn subset of the other words. A wrong DUT shows a mismatch at the first bad word-address bit, byte lane or size (an aliased word, a swapped byte, a stuck lane). Anchor: the real load values through the CPU LSU, logged on the outbound mailbox (approved), compared on the host with the image that the bench wrote into the macro. The image words are draws, so a DUT that returns a constant or an address-derived value fails. Evidence: `CHK-ROM-SWEEP PASS: rom_ctl=0x0 probes=<n> sizes=1,2,4 probes_by_size=<a>/<b>/<c> boundary_words=15/15 drawn_words=<w> mismatches=0`.
|CHK-ROM-PORT
|Each load of steps 7 to 11 appears once on `lsu_rom_req` and once on `rom_req` with the expected word address and read intent, and with no write-enable cycle. The counts cover the window from the first load of step 7 to the last load of step 11. `lsu_xbar_req` shows no request for a ROM address (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture: a dedicated path outside the local crossbar, a 64-bit word address). The firmware runs from SRAM, so no instruction fetch of the ROM falls in this window. A DUT that routes a ROM load through the crossbar, repeats a request or drives the wrong word address fails. Anchor: the taps `lsu_rom_req`, `rom_req` and `lsu_xbar_req`. Control: the SRAM word load of step 6 appears on `lsu_xbar_req` and not on `lsu_rom_req`, so a silent tap can fail. Evidence: `CHK-ROM-PORT PASS: loads=<n> lsu_rom_req=<n> rom_req=<n> word_addr_ok=<n> we_cycles=0 lsu_xbar_rom_addr=0 control_lsu_xbar=<m> control_lsu_rom_for_sram=0`.
|CHK-ROM-STORE
|Each of the 16 byte and halfword stores gets a response, and the word reads back as the image. The completion marker of a store appears within the bounded wait, and the word that the firmware reads after the store equals the word that it read before, which equals the image word (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, lines 22-24: stores receive a response and do not modify the ROM). The response code is not stated, so a retired store and a trapped store both count as a response. A DUT that takes the store into the macro shows a changed readback, because the store data differs from the image in the stored lanes. A DUT that drops the store with no response shows a timeout. Anchor: the real readback through the CPU LSU (approved). Control: the loads of the same leaf return image words (CHK-ROM-SWEEP), so the readback can show a change. Evidence: `CHK-ROM-STORE PASS: n=<n> sizes=1,2 responses=<n> traps=<k> readback=image`.
|CHK-ROM-STORE-WE
|In step 12, from the first store to the last readback, the ROM macro request (`rom_macro_req` with `rom_macro_addr`) at the word address of a store occurs only for the readback loads of that word: two before the store and two after it (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture: the SEP ROM interface shim converts the CPU memory request for the ROM macro, and stores are not forwarded to the macro; `doc/integrator/src/smu-sep.adoc`, Module Overview: the ROM shim is instantiated outside SEP). A store on `rom_req` with the write enable set is not a FAIL, because the store filter is in the shim after `sep_boot_rom_req_o`. A DUT that forwards a store to the macro shows an extra macro request at the store word. Anchor: the `rom_macro_req` and `rom_macro_addr` taps. Control: each readback load of step 12 appears on `rom_macro_req` at the word address of the store, so a silent tap fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-ROM-STORE-WE PASS: stores=<n> extra_macro_req=0 control_readback_macro_req=<m>`.
|CHK-ROM-NOECC
|Each flipped word returns the stored data, which is the image word with the flipped bits inverted, and the load retires with no trap, so the response is OKAY (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, Hardware Configuration and Integrity: no ECC or integrity-error sideband). This holds for the 1-bit and the 2-bit flips. The words before and after each flipped word read exact. A DUT with ECC that corrects the flip returns the image word and fails. A DUT that flags an error shows a trap. Anchor: the real load values and the trap count through the CPU LSU (approved), with the flips from the ROM macro bit-flip hook. Controls: the neighbouring words read exact, so the read path is live; the `ecall` before the flip reads raises the trap count by 1, so the count can show a trap. Evidence: `CHK-ROM-NOECC PASS: flips1=8 flips2=8 data=stored resp=OKAY traps=0 neighbours_exact=<n> control_ecall_traps=1`.
|CHK-ROM-OTF
|At the drawn offset o, the `rst_ni` fall lands before the `rom_rsp` beat of the anchor fetch (`midflight=1`). After the release (`sep_cpu_reset_n_o` high), the first `rom_req` handshake carries word address 0, no request at word address 1 appears before it, no `rom_rsp` beat appears between the release and that handshake, and the first `rom_rsp` beat after it carries image word 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `sep_reset_n` resets the SRAM and boot ROM interfaces, and the CPU restarts at the reset vector). Beats before the release are not scored: the ROM macro sits outside SEP and can answer the abandoned request, and the specification gives no assertion timing from the `rst_ni` fall to `sep_reset_n` (<<sep-vplan-phase3-unstated>>). A run where the response beat comes before the `rst_ni` fall fails the check, because the access is not in flight. A DUT that replays the abandoned fetch shows a request at word address 1 before the word-0 request, and a DUT that does not restart at the reset vector shows another first word address. W is a measurement of the calibration boot of step 3 and not an expected value. Anchor: the `rom_req` and `rom_rsp` taps, with `rst_ni` and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` (approved). Control: the calibration boot of step 3 completes the same fetch with no reset and its beat carries image word 1, so the taps show the anchor fetch. Evidence: `CHK-ROM-OTF PASS: offset=<o>/<W> midflight=1 first_req_word=0 replay_word1=0 beats_before_req=0 first_rsp=0x<image_word_0>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The reserved-address DECERR cell is graded in `sep_fabric_row_response_matrix_test`. This test does not claim the ROM read data and address mapping with a non-zero `ROM_CTL` (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), a 64-bit load, the ROM content or any technology-specific integrity behavior. The word store, the 32-bit base and top word loads, the instruction fetch and the refusal of the address one past the ROM stay with the open owners named in Test Coverage. A read of every ROM word in one leaf is not made. The ROM leg of a reset in flight is the only reset-in-flight cell of the ROM; the SRAM leg is in `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test`.

'''''

[[sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test]]
===== 5.24 `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), Verilator and VCS, `+skip_fuse_sense` with `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1, so `rst_ni` is the only open term of the reset-in-flight leg, `reseed = 4`. The shape leg and the reset-in-flight leg share the SRAM access stimulus and run in one leaf. Verilator is two-state, so the leaf reads only written bytes.

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_axi_shape_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master on `s_axi` and inbound master on `m_axi` (a `SepAxiAgent` over `ocah_axi_vip` for each, approved), each with its monitor (approved). SRAM byte model: a byte shadow of every written byte, which scores only bytes that the shadow holds. `rst_ni`, `sep_cpu_reset_n_o`, `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (approved). `sram_req` and `sram_rsp` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the address, the size (1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes), the burst type, the length, the ID (distinct and non-zero), the outstanding depth (1 to 4) and the master. Reset-in-flight leg: the path order, for each path the reset offset as a fraction `u_o` in [0,1) (scaled in step 9 to o = floor(`u_o` times W) once W is measured), and the address and the data of each access. Each path runs one reset in flight at its drawn offset in each leaf. `reseed = 4` samples four sets of draws. The SRAM offset-third bins of `cp_otf_reset_x_path` are seed-dependent and fill on a merge of regressions: each third of a path has a probability of 1/3 in a leaf.

*Objective*: The 256 KiB scratch SRAM at 0x1000_0000 serves INCR bursts of every length and single-beat bursts of every type, the single-beat WRAP burst (AxLEN = 0, not a legal AXI4 length) included, which the test drives as error injection. Written bytes read back. Every multi-beat burst of this test comes from the inbound master. A multi-beat FIXED or WRAP burst from the inbound master is refused: the write ends with SLVERR and leaves SRAM unchanged, and every read beat returns SLVERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, lines 62-65). A cold reset that lands with a scratch SRAM access outstanding leaves the interface usable: the SRAM interface is reset by `sep_reset_n`, which follows `rst_ni` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, lines 44-49). No stale response appears after the reset, and a write after the reset lands and reads back. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (scratch SRAM window); `hw/sys/sep/regs/include/sep_sram.rdl`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` (Top Level AXI4); `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` (Single-Beat Register Access); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`rst_ni`, `sep_sram_req_o`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_sram_burst_type_test`, which grades one 4-beat FIXED and one 4-beat WRAP burst from the CPU-LSU master (refused, memory unchanged, every read beat SLVERR). `sep_sram_inbound_burst_attr_test` grades INCR bursts of 1 to 256 beats from the inbound master and the unstrobed bytes. `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test` and `sep_sram_smoke_test` grade the reach, the patterns and the contiguous strobes. `sep_reset_mid_transfer_recovery_test` grades the cold reset with probe registers and has no SRAM path. This test grades only the new cells: the refused FIXED and WRAP bursts of other lengths from the inbound master, the single-beat FIXED and single-beat WRAP cells, the outstanding depth, and the reset in flight at the SRAM interface. The CPU-LSU burst refusal, the INCR lengths and the address reach are controls here. The open owners keep the rest. Cells: outstanding INCR bursts of lengths 1, 2, 7 and 16 within one 4 KiB page, with read and write interleaved; FIXED bursts of lengths 2, 3, 8 and 16 and WRAP bursts of lengths 2, 8 and 16 on the inbound master; single-beat FIXED on the inbound master and the CPU-LSU splice, and single-beat WRAP (AxLEN = 0) on both as error injection; the address reach over the full 256 KiB (control `CTL-SRAM-REACH`); an SRAM read and an SRAM write through the CPU-LSU splice, each with `rst_ni` at one drawn offset inside its response window W. Functional coverage: `sep_mem_aperture_cg` (`cp_macro_if` (the SRAM cells), `cp_requesting_master` (the single-beat FIXED and WRAP cells of each master), `cp_sram_burst` (the refused multi-beat FIXED and WRAP cells of the inbound master), `cp_otf_reset_x_path` (the SRAM cells)) and the Phase 1 group `sep_sram_access_cg`. `CTL-SRAM-REACH`, the INCR bursts and the control writes of the refusal step fill no cell of `cp_requesting_master` or `cp_sram_burst`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Reset the DUT with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `ext_boot_seq_done_i` at 1. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for each draw group. Clear the byte shadow.
. Through the CPU-LSU master, program one inbound filter entry (`FILTER_CONFIG`, `START_ADDR`, `END_ADDR`) that admits every inbound access of this test: `entry_enabled` = 1, `read_allowed` = 1, `write_allowed` = 1, `allow_burst` = 1 (an entry with `allow_burst` = 0 matches only single-beat accesses, so a multi-beat burst finds no match and ends with the block-by-default DECERR and not the SRAM SLVERR), `src_id` = 0 (wildcard), `allow_ns` equal to the AxPROT[1] that the inbound master drives on every access (a fixed value for the leaf), `START_ADDR` = 0x1000_0000 and `END_ADDR` = 0x1003_FFFF (with `allow_burst` = 1 the granule is 4 KB, so the range is exact). Read back every field and `START_ADDR` and `END_ADDR`, and compare them with the programmed values (`hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, Match, Then Permit and Address Range Granule). Write no `SYS_DIS` bit, so the inbound filter stays in its default-block state for all other addresses.
. Reach control (`CTL-SRAM-REACH`). On both masters, write a unique 64-bit word with single-beat accesses of a drawn size (1, 2, 4 or 8 bytes, natural strobe; each of the four sizes runs at least once on each master in every leaf) at the first word, at the last word 0x1003_FFF8, at each of the 15 word-address bits and at 128 drawn addresses over the 256 KiB. Update the shadow with each write. Read each word back and compare it with the shadow. Every write uses the natural strobe of its size and address. Sparse and zero strobes are not driven.
. Outstanding depth. On the inbound master, write INCR bursts of lengths 1, 2, 7 and 16 within one 4 KiB page with drawn sizes and distinct non-zero IDs, and update the shadow with each completed write. Then issue reads of those bursts and writes of new bursts, interleaved, with a drawn outstanding depth of 1 to 4. No read overlaps an outstanding write. Log the peak count of outstanding requests as `OBS-SRAM-DEPTH`.
. Error injection. Point: the inbound master, SRAM target. Kind: multi-beat FIXED bursts of lengths 2, 3, 8 and 16 and multi-beat WRAP bursts of lengths 2, 8 and 16, with drawn sizes. Expected response: SLVERR on the write response and on every read beat, with no change in SRAM (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4). The 4-beat cases are graded by an open test and are not drawn. For each burst, in the drawn order: write every word that the burst would touch with single-beat accesses of the natural strobe, so that the shadow holds each byte; write an INCR burst and a single-beat FIXED burst with the same ID, and read both back (the control that the master and the ID can write); read the words that the burst would write (every beat address) and keep them as the pre-read; issue the refused write burst with data that differs from the pre-read in every beat; read the same words a second time; issue the refused read burst of the same type and length.
. Single-beat FIXED (AxLEN = 0). On the inbound master and on the CPU-LSU splice, write one word with a single-beat FIXED burst, with distinct data, and read it back.
. Error injection. Point: the inbound master and the CPU-LSU splice, SRAM target. Kind: a WRAP burst with AxLEN = 0 (a WRAP length of 1 is not a legal AXI4 length; the legal WRAP lengths are 2, 4, 8 and 16). Expected response: the access is served with OKAY and the data lands, because the SRAM target serves single-beat bursts of every type (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, lines 61-62). On each master, write one word with this burst, with distinct data, and read it back with the same burst.
. Reset in flight. Measure the response window W of each path with no reset: for an SRAM read and for an SRAM write through the CPU-LSU splice, count the `clk_i` cycles from the request handshake (AR for a read, the last of AW and W for a write) to the R or B beat on the bus monitor.
. Error injection. Point: `rst_ni`. Kind: a cold reset that lands with an SRAM access outstanding. Expected response: the interface is usable after the release, no stale response appears, and a write after the reset lands and reads back (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). For each path in the drawn order, at its drawn offset o (log `u_o`, W and o): issue the access with a drawn address, a drawn data value and a drawn ID; lower `rst_ni` o cycles after its request handshake, before its response beat; hold it low until `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` is seen low (a bounded wait); raise it; wait for `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` high (a bounded wait); drop the abandoned access in the bench master; write a different drawn value to a different drawn address with a different ID through the CPU-LSU splice; read it back; watch the bus monitor for a bounded quiet wait for any R or B beat with the abandoned ID. SRAM content written before the reset is not scored.
. The bench evaluates every checker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SRAM-OUTSTANDING
|With 1 to 4 outstanding bursts, every read beat returns the shadow bytes of its own address and carries the ID of its request, and every B beat carries the ID of its write, with zero mismatches (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, Top Level AXI4: the SRAM serves INCR bursts of every length). A DUT that returns the data of another burst, mixes the IDs or loses a beat fails. Anchor: the R and B beats on the `m_axi` monitor (approved) against the byte shadow. The shadow is a Python model that holds only written bytes. Control: `CTL-SRAM-REACH` shows that the same masters write and read back every probed word, so the shadow and the read path are live. The `CTL-SRAM-REACH` line is `CTL-SRAM-REACH: words=<n> first=1 last=1 bits_walked=15 mismatches=0`, and a mismatch in it raises UVM_ERROR. The peak outstanding count is logged and not graded, because the specification states no depth limit. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-OUTSTANDING PASS: depth=<d> bursts=<n> id_match=1 data_match=1`.
|CHK-SRAM-REFUSE
|Each FIXED or WRAP burst of length 2 or more from the inbound master ends the write with SLVERR and leaves the pre-read words unchanged. The read burst of the same type and length returns SLVERR on every beat, and the count of the beats equals the length (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, lines 62-65). A DUT that takes the write shows a changed post-read, because the burst data differs from the pre-read in every beat. A DUT that answers OKAY or fewer beats fails. Anchor: the B and R beats on the `m_axi` monitor and the real read-back of the words (approved). Control: the INCR burst and the single-beat FIXED burst with the same master and ID write successfully in the same leaf, so the refusal is attributable to the burst type. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-REFUSE PASS: type=<FIXED or WRAP> len=<l> master=inbound bresp=SLVERR unchanged=1 rbeats=<l> all_SLVERR`.
|CHK-SRAM-SINGLE
|A single-beat FIXED access (legal stimulus, step 6) and a single-beat WRAP access with AxLEN = 0 (error injection, step 7) are each served with OKAY, and the data lands: the read-back equals the shadow (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, line 62: single-beat bursts of every type). A DUT that refuses every FIXED or WRAP burst, single-beat included, shows SLVERR and fails. A DUT that rejects the WRAP burst of length 1 as an illegal length shows a non-OKAY response and fails the WRAP cell only. Anchor: the B and R beats on the monitor of each master and the read-back (approved). Control: the INCR writes of the same leaf land on both masters. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-SINGLE PASS: type=<FIXED or WRAP> master=<inbound or cpu_lsu> resp=OKAY landed=1`.
|CHK-SRAM-OTF
|For each path at its drawn offset, the reset lands before the response beat of a real access (`midflight=1`: the monitor shows the request handshake, the reset fall o cycles later, and no response beat before the fall). After the release, the write of the drawn value reads back that value with OKAY, and no R or B beat with the abandoned ID appears on the bench-port monitor, which `rst_ni` does not reset (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). A DUT that keeps the abandoned access answers it after the release or hangs the next access and fails. Anchor: the real read-back and the monitor beats (approved), with `rst_ni` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`. Control: the same path completes with the drawn data in the no-reset measurement of step 8, so a response is visible on the monitor. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-OTF PASS: path=<RD or WR> offset=<o>/<W> midflight=1 readback=0x<v> resp=OKAY stale_beats=0`.
|CHK-SRAM-OTF-REPLAY
|The `sram_req` tap shows no replay of the abandoned request after the reset: no request with the abandoned address appears between the release and the post-reset write. A DUT that replays the access shows the request. Anchor: the `sram_req` tap. Control: the post-reset write appears on `sram_req`, and the read of the post-reset readback shows its `rvalid` beat on `sram_rsp`, so neither tap is silent. A missing control event fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-OTF-REPLAY PASS: path=<RD or WR> offset=<o>/<W> replay=0 control_write_seen=1 control_rsp_seen=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The ROM leg of a reset in flight is in `sep_boot_rom_aperture_sweep_test`. This test does not claim the SRAM write strobe contract (partial, sparse and zero strobes; see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), the SRAM content after a reset (the specification states no retention rule), or a depth of outstanding requests that the DUT accepts. The CPU-LSU burst refusal, the INCR lengths, the address reach and the 4-beat FIXED and WRAP cases stay with the open owners named in Test Coverage.

'''''

[[sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test]]
===== 5.25 `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, testlist target `lsu_stub_all_live`, with `tools = ["vcs"]`, `reseed = 4`. No firmware runs. Testlist `memory.toml`, groups `all` and `cpu_stub`. The graded invocation overrides the target to `default`, because the stub target has no TCM controller; on that full-CPU VCS build the no-CPU force-splice holds the core and drives the LSU register path.

*Source Module*: `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test`

*BFM / Model*: The secure DMA, programmed through its registers over the LSU register path with the CPU-LSU master (`SepAxiAgent` over `ocah_axi_vip`), is the master for every TCM access. ICCM and DCCM word model: the written 32-bit data of each word. `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` and `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` reads and writes, `dbg_iccm_active_o`, `dbg_dccm_active_o`, `ext_debug_bus_o` (all approved). Added hook (error inject): the TCM bit-flip hook (word address, bit mask), which the test adds to the inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. `sep_cpu_tcm_rsp` and the TCM write data and ECC fields of `sep_cpu_tcm_req` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. Every single-bit position (0 to 38) runs for each memory on every seed. Dimensions: the word address, the data, and the double-bit pairs, 16 for each memory with at least 4 of each class: data-data, data-ECC and ECC-ECC (the hook numbers bits 0 to 31 as data and bits 32 to 38 as ECC). `reseed = 4` samples four sets of addresses, data and pairs.

*Objective*: ICCM (256 KiB at 0xC000_0000) and DCCM (128 KiB at 0xC004_0000) hold 32-bit words with 7 ECC bits and do not alias (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Local Memory; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`, ICCM and DCCM rows). On an externally initiated (DMA-path) read, a single-bit error returns the corrected data, and a two-bit error returns an error response. The error is not a CPU trap (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Memory ECC Errors, Correctable (single-bit) errors, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors). A non-OKAY response to a DMA transfer sets `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.host_path_err` (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`). Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (CPU, Local Memory); `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` (Fabric Topology: the CPU TCM target); `hw/sys/sep/doc/dma.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` (ICCM and DCCM windows); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (Memory ECC Errors, Correctable (single-bit) errors, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors, Debug bus mirror); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`sep_cpu_tcm_req_o`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hello_world_test`, which grades that firmware runs from the ICCM with an image that the bench loads, `sep_dma_hash_test` (CHK-COPY), which compares a DMA copy from SRAM to DCCM word for word, and `sep_dma_basic_test` (CHK-ICCM), which grades the round trip from SRAM to ICCM and back to SRAM. Those open owners keep the single-region copy cell and the firmware run from the ICCM. This test grades the data of the DMA copies between SRAM and every aperture word of ICCM and DCCM, in both directions, through the real readback. `sep_fabric_dma_endpoint_matrix_test` has no TCM endpoint. This test grades only the new cells: the aperture reach of both memories at the first and last word, at every word-address bit and at drawn words, the alias check between ICCM and DCCM, and the DMA-path ECC response. The copy of the first word and the clean reads are controls here. The clean reads are the control of the error cells. The graded cells of the aperture leg are the address walk, the alias check and the copy data between SRAM and each TCM word. Cells: the first and last word of ICCM and DCCM, every word-address bit (16 for ICCM, 15 for DCCM) and 64 drawn words for each memory; clean reads; a single-bit flip at each of the 39 positions of a stored word in each memory; 16 double-bit flips for each memory over the classes data-data, data-ECC and ECC-ECC. Functional coverage: `sep_mem_aperture_cg` (`cp_mem_x_class` (the TCM cells), `cp_macro_if` (the TCM request cell), `cp_tcm_ecc` (the single and double error cells)), `sep_cpu_ecc_irq_cg` (`cp_dma_ecc_response`) and `sep_fabric_dedicated_port_cg` (`cp_tcm_dma_dir_range`; the cell and its group are defined in the fabric section).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the reset with the core held. Wait for the register path to answer (a bounded wait). Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for each draw group.
. Read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`. It reads 0x0. Program the secure DMA enabled memory range with `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE` = 0x0 and `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT` = 0xFFFF_FFFF, which covers the SRAM staging words, the ICCM and the DCCM, and write `RANGE_VALID` = 1 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/secure_dma.rdl`: a transfer with no configured range ends in `ERROR_CODE.RANGE_VALID_ERROR`). Read the three registers back; a value other than the written one fails the leaf.
. Aperture. Draw distinct non-zero 32-bit data words for the aperture set of each memory: the first word, the last word, one word for each word-address bit (word index 2^k for k from 0 to 15 in ICCM and from 0 to 14 in DCCM) and 64 drawn words. Write each data word to a staging word in the SEP SRAM through the CPU-LSU master. Copy each staging word to its TCM word with the secure DMA, one 4-byte transfer for each word, and wait for the DMA to report completion (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-TCM-APERT). Write every word of both memories before the first read.
. During every DMA copy of step 3, sample `dbg_iccm_active_o` and `dbg_dccm_active_o` on every clock and keep, for each memory, whether its tap rose and whether the other tap rose.
. Read every aperture word back: copy it with the secure DMA to a staging word in the SEP SRAM, read the staging word through the CPU-LSU master, and compare it with the written data. Read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` after each copy (the clean reads). This rule applies to every DMA read of this step and of steps 7 and 8: the readback staging word is not the source staging word of the write, and before each copy the CPU-LSU master writes the bitwise complement of the expected data to it and reads it back. A copy that does not land leaves the complement and fails the compare.
. During every DMA copy of step 5, sample `dbg_iccm_active_o`, `dbg_dccm_active_o` and `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]`, and log `OBS-TCM-DBG` for each read.
. Single-bit flips. For each memory and each bit position k from 0 to 38, in a drawn order: draw a word address and data; write 1 to `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR.clr` and read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` (0x0); write the word with the secure DMA (the hardware encodes the ECC); read it clean with the secure DMA, compare it and read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` (the control); error injection, point: the ICCM or DCCM macro array, kind: a one-bit flip of bit k of the stored 39-bit word through the hook, expected response: the DMA read returns the written (corrected) data (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors; the response code is not stated for the DMA path and is not scored); read the word with the secure DMA and wait for the DMA to report completion (a bounded wait); compare the staging word with the written data; read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` and log it as `OBS-TCM-SBE-STATUS` (not scored); sample the stored word on `sep_cpu_tcm_rsp` and the written word and ECC on `sep_cpu_tcm_req`.
. Double-bit flips. For each memory and each drawn pair of bit positions, in a drawn order: draw a word address and data; write 1 to `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR.clr` and read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` (0x0); write the word with the secure DMA; read it clean, compare it and read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` (the control, 0x0); error injection, point: the ICCM or DCCM macro array, kind: a flip of the two bits of the pair in the stored word through the hook, expected response: the DMA read returns an error response, not OKAY (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors: an error response on that transaction); read the word with the secure DMA and wait for the DMA to report completion or error (a bounded wait); read `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`; clear the DMA error status and write 1 to `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR.clr`.
. The bench evaluates every checker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TCM-APERT
|Every aperture word reads back the data written to it, and no word of the other memory or of another address changes the value: all words of both memories are written before the first read, so an alias of ICCM and DCCM (or of two word addresses) returns another word's data and fails (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Local Memory; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`, ICCM and DCCM rows). The graded cells are the alias and address-walk result and the DMA copy data between SRAM and each TCM word, in both directions. Anchor: the real read-back through the secure DMA and the CPU-LSU master (approved). Control: during every DMA copy to or from an ICCM word `dbg_iccm_active_o` rises, and for a DCCM word `dbg_dccm_active_o` rises (taps approved), so a stuck-low tap fails. Each readback staging word holds the complement of the expected data before its copy (step 5), so a read copy that does not land fails. Whether the other tap stays low is logged as `OBS-TCM-EXCL` and is not graded, because the specification states no exclusivity rule for the clock enables. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-APERT PASS: iccm_words=<n> dccm_words=<m> aliases=0 control_each_tap_rose=1`. `OBS-TCM-EXCL: mem=<ICCM or DCCM> other_tap_rose=<0 or 1>`.
|CHK-TCM-CLEAN
|`DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` reads 0x0 at the start (RDL reset value), and reads with no flip return the written data and leave `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.host_path_err` at 0, with no DMA error status (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`). A DUT that flags an error on a clean read fails. Anchor: the real `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` read and the data read-back (approved). Control: the clean reads of the aperture words show the DMA copy path is live, because their data match the written data. The clean reads at the addresses of the error steps are the control of CHK-TCM-SBE and CHK-TCM-DBE. Control for the flag: CHK-TCM-DBE sets `host_path_err` in the same leaf, so a dead flag fails there. No other step of the leaf sets the flag, and the data match is a control. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-CLEAN PASS: reads=<n> data_match=1 host_path_err=0 control_dbe_flag=1`.
|CHK-TCM-SBE
|For each memory and flipped bit, the DMA read returns the written data (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors). The response code and `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` after the read are not graded, because the specification does not state the response code of a corrected DMA read. A DUT with no correction returns the flipped data and fails. Anchor: the real data read-back (approved). Control: the stored word on `sep_cpu_tcm_rsp` differs from the written word in bit k, so the flip is real and the correction is real. The data bits 0 to 31 compare with the data of the response, and the bits 32 to 38 with its ECC. The readback staging word holds the complement of the written data before the read (step 5), so a read that ends with an error and moves no data fails. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-SBE PASS: mem=<ICCM or DCCM> addr=0x<..> k=<k> staging_before=0x<~v> data=0x<v> stored_bit_flipped=1`.
|CHK-TCM-DBE
|For each drawn pair, the DMA read ends with `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.host_path_err` set, which shows a non-OKAY response (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS`). The pairs cover the classes data-data, data-ECC and ECC-ECC in both memories. A DUT that corrects or ignores a two-bit error leaves the flag at 0 and fails. Anchor: the real `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` read (approved), with the flip from the TCM bit-flip hook. Control: the flag reads 0 before the pair (after the clear) and in the clean read of the same address with no flip (CHK-TCM-CLEAN), and the stored word on `sep_cpu_tcm_rsp` differs from the written word in the two flipped bits. The flag is cleared between pairs through `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-DBE PASS: mem=<ICCM or DCCM> addr=0x<..> k1=<a> k2=<b> class=<data-data or data-ECC or ECC-ECC> host_path_err=1 clean_flag=0`.
|===

`OBS-TCM-DBG: mem=<ICCM or DCCM> kind=<single or double or none> bus367_364=0b<....>` logs the `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` value during each DMA read. The line is not scored.

`OBS-TCM-SBE-STATUS: mem=<ICCM or DCCM> k=<k> dma_bus_err_status=0x<v>` logs `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` after the read of a singly flipped word. The line is not scored.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The CPU trap half of the contract, and the debug-bus pulses on a CPU access, are graded in `sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test`. This test does not claim whether a DMA-path error drives the `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` mirror, the response code of a corrected DMA read, or the data that the DMA writes to its destination after a double-bit error (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The firmware run from the ICCM stays with `sep_hello_world_test`.

'''''

[[sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test]]
===== 5.26 `sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu` (real core and firmware), Verilator and VCS (VCS is preferred for the pulse timing), `reseed = 4`

*Source Module*: `sep_tcm_ecc_cpu_trap_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU firmware (an image with a trap handler and an NMI handler; the bench loads it through the `tcm_load_i` image load of ICCM and DCCM, which stores valid ECC) with CSR reads logged on the outbound mailbox, the CPU trace port, `ext_debug_bus_o`, `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` and the PIC register reads (all approved). Added hook (error inject): the TCM bit-flip hook with a time-0 flip list (word address, bit mask for each flip, applied before reset release), which the test adds to the inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, passed to the firmware in its parameter block: the threshold exponent t (0 to 5); the arming order of the DCCM correctable leg (the arming comes before or after the count reaches 2^t: miss first or reach first); the single-bit flip positions (bits 0 to 38); for the DCCM, the word addresses of the single-bit flips, drawn only inside a reserved DCCM data window that the firmware image leaves unused (the firmware linker script keeps `.data`, `.bss`, the stack, the trap vector and the PIC vector table outside it); for the ICCM, the flipped words, drawn only inside the straight-line code block; the positions of the three double-bit flips and, inside the same window, the addresses of the DCCM load word and the DCCM store word (the vector-table word of PIC source 2 has the address that the table fixes). The correctable leg runs on the ICCM and then on the DCCM in every leaf, each memory with its own 2^t + 1 flips, so the seed never selects the memory. The uncorrectable and NMI legs run in every leaf. `reseed = 4` samples four sets of draws.

*Objective*: A single-bit TCM error on a CPU access is corrected and counted in `MICCMECT` or `MDCCMECT`. The counter holds the count in bits [26:0] and a threshold exponent in bits [31:27], and resets to 0. The counter raises `mip.MCEIP` once the count reaches 2^threshold. The request is delivered with `mcause` 0x8000_001E when `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE` are set. Errors count silently while `mie[30]` is clear. No PIC register or PIC source is involved. `MICECT` never increments. An uncorrectable DCCM error traps on the access and has no enable: DCCM load cause 5, DCCM store cause 7, with `mscause` = 1. An uncorrectable DCCM error on the fast-interrupt path is an NMI with `mcause` 0xF000_1000 taken through `SEP_NMI_VEC`. Single-bit pulses appear on `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` (ICCM bit 367, DCCM bit 365) and double-bit pulses on bit 366 (ICCM) and bit 364 (DCCM). This leaf drives double-bit errors in the DCCM only. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (Non-PIC Interrupt Paths, Memory ECC Errors, Correctable (single-bit) errors, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors, Debug bus mirror); `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (Local Memory); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`ext_debug_bus_o`); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc` (`SEP_NMI_VEC`, `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK`).

*Test Coverage*: `sep_hello_world_test` grades that firmware runs from the ICCM. `sep_nmi_sanity_test` grades that the watchdog bark enters the handler at the programmed vector after the set of the lock, and that entry is a control here. This test grades only the new cells: the correctable counter and its threshold, the delivery of `MCEIP`, the two uncorrectable traps, the NMI on the fast-interrupt path, the debug-bus pulses and the absence of a PIC source. The open owners keep the rest. Cells, all in every leaf: the counter at counts below 2^t (DCCM) and at or above 2^t (DCCM, and ICCM at the sample after the code block, where only the block totals are graded) with `mie[30]` clear, and the counter with `mie[30]` set (DCCM); the arming at a count at or above 2^t; the exponent clamp; the DCCM load and store traps, each after a clean neighbour access of the same type; the NMI through the DCCM vector-table word of PIC source 2; the pulses on bits 367, 365 and 364; the `meip` words and `MICECT` against their baseline after the correctable leg and after each uncorrectable trap, with a pending PIC source as the control. Functional coverage: `sep_cpu_ecc_irq_cg` (`cp_ce_counter`, `cp_ce_clamp`, `cp_mceip_delivery`, `cp_tcm_trap_cause`, `cp_nmi_fast_path`, `cp_ecc_dbg_pulse`, `cp_ecc_no_pic`), `sep_debug_trace_cg` (`cp_traced_event`) and the Phase 1 group `sep_cpu_boot_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host draws t, the order and the flips, and writes them to the parameter block. Before reset release, it loads the firmware image through `tcm_load_i`. In the same load, for every word that carries a flip and for the word before and the word after it, the host stores a known data word with valid ECC. For the DCCM, each such data word is a draw from `SepSeededRng`; for the ICCM, the words are the instructions of the code block. The host writes the expected value of every DCCM load and store word and of its neighbour to the parameter block, so the expected data comes from the draw and not from the DUT. It then applies the time-0 flip list through the TCM bit-flip hook: 2^t + 1 distinct single-bit flips in each of the ICCM and the DCCM (DCCM data words, and ICCM instruction words of one straight-line code block in which each flipped word is followed by instructions that sample the counters), and three double-bit flips in the DCCM: a word that the firmware loads, a word that a byte store hits, and the vector-table word of PIC source 2 (mailbox interrupt 1). The ICCM flips are in the code block only. The flipped DCCM words, other than the vector-table word, lie in the reserved window, and no flip lies in `.data`, `.bss`, the stack or the trap vector. The words next to the load word and the store word hold no flip.
. Release the reset. The firmware prints `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for each draw group, installs its trap handler, and logs a begin marker and an end marker on the mailbox around each leg and around each flipped-word access.
. The firmware reads `MICCMECT` and `MDCCMECT`, which read 0x0, and reads `MICECT` and logs it.
. The firmware writes the exponent 31 (bits [31:27]) with a count field of 0 to `MICCMECT` and to `MDCCMECT`, and reads each back (bits [31:27] read 26). It then writes t with a count field of 0 to each register and reads each back (bits [31:27] read t).
. The firmware makes PIC source 2 pending with a usable priority and its `meie` bit set, with the recipe of the open mailbox PIC tests, and keeps `mie.MEIE` and `mstatus.MIE` clear so that the core takes no external interrupt. It reads every `meip` word and keeps them as the baseline. The word that holds source 2 is non-zero.
. ICCM correctable leg, with `mie[30]` clear. The firmware takes a sample (it reads both counters and `mip[30]` and logs them), executes the straight-line code block over the 2^t + 1 flipped instruction words, and takes a sample after each flipped word, logging `OBS-TCM-INCR` for each. These samples are not graded, because the IFU fetches ahead and a correction can fall between the two reads of a sample. After the last word of the block, the firmware takes the quiescent sample: it reads both counters and `mip[30]` and logs them. No flipped word remains to be fetched, so no count changes between the reads. The readback of `MICCMECT` is at or above 2^t. The firmware then arms the delivery control: it sets `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE`, and the handler logs `mcause`, clears `mie[30]` and returns with `mret`. The firmware then writes `MICCMECT` with the exponent t and a count field of 0 and reads it back with the count 0, so that `MCEIP` is low before the DCCM leg.
. DCCM correctable leg, order miss first. With the count at 0, below 2^t for every t, the firmware sets `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE`, takes a sample (no trap may follow), and clears both. With `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE` clear, the firmware takes a sample, then reads flipped DCCM words one at a time and takes a sample after each (logging `OBS-TCM-INCR`), until the readback of `MDCCMECT` is 2^t - 1 (the miss cell). It sets `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE` and takes a sample. It reads the next flipped word. The count reaches 2^t or more and the core takes one trap: the handler logs `mcause`, clears `mie[30]` and returns with `mret`. The firmware then reads one more flipped word with `mie[30]` clear and takes a sample. If an access skips the readback 2^t - 1, the leaf logs `OBS-TCM-INCR` and runs the leg in the form of the next step.
. DCCM correctable leg, order reach first. With the count at 0, below 2^t for every t, the firmware sets `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE`, takes a sample (no trap may follow), and clears both. With `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE` clear, the firmware takes a sample, then reads flipped DCCM words one at a time and takes a sample after each (logging `OBS-TCM-INCR`), until the readback of `MDCCMECT` is at or above 2^t. It then sets `mie[30]` and `mstatus.MIE`. `MCEIP` is set, so the core takes one trap: the handler logs `mcause`, clears `mie[30]` and returns with `mret`.
. After the correctable leg, the firmware clears `mstatus.MIE`, reads every `meip` word and reads `MICECT`, and logs them (the first PIC check).
. Error injection. Point: the DCCM macro array, the time-0 flip list. Kind: a two-bit flip of the DCCM load word. Expected response: a load access fault with `mcause` 5 and `mscause` = 1, and a one-clock pulse on bit 364 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors). With `mie` and `mstatus.MIE` clear, the firmware loads the clean neighbour word (it returns its expected data with no trap), then loads the flipped word. The trap handler logs `mcause` and `mscause` (CSR 0x7FF) and resumes after the load. The firmware does not retry the load. It then reads every `meip` word and `MICECT` and logs them (the second PIC check).
. Error injection. Point: the DCCM macro array, the time-0 flip list. Kind: a two-bit flip of a DCCM word that a byte store hits. Expected response: a store access fault with `mcause` 7 and `mscause` = 1, and a one-clock pulse on bit 364. With `mie` and `mstatus.MIE` clear, the firmware stores a byte to the clean neighbour word and reads it back (the store takes no trap and the byte lands), then stores a byte to the flipped word. The trap handler logs `mcause` and `mscause` and resumes after the store. The firmware then reads every `meip` word and `MICECT` and logs them (the third PIC check), before the leg of the next step.
. Error injection. Point: the DCCM vector-table word of PIC source 2. Kind: a two-bit flip on the fast-interrupt path. Expected response: an NMI with `mcause` 0xF000_1000 taken through `SEP_NMI_VEC` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Uncorrectable (double-bit) errors). The firmware writes the address of its NMI handler to `SEP_NMI_VEC` and 1 to `SEP_NMI_VEC_LOCK`, and sets the PIC vector table base to the DCCM table that holds the flipped word at the entry of source 2. It sets `mie.MEIE` and `mstatus.MIE`, so the pending source 2 is taken. The NMI handler logs its entry address and `mcause`, and ends the firmware with an end marker.
. The host counts the pulses on `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` on every clock, samples `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` on every clock from step 5, and evaluates every checker from the mailbox log, the trace port, the pulse counts and the vector samples.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TCM-CE
|In the DCCM leg, after each load of a flipped word, the readback of `MDCCMECT` rises by at least 1 (`OBS-TCM-INCR` logs the amount, which is not graded), and in every sample `mip.MCEIP` is set if and only if the readback is at or above 2^t (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Correctable (single-bit) errors: the counter raises a request once the count reaches 2^threshold, and `MCEIP` follows the comparator). In the ICCM leg, two samples are graded, because the IFU fetches ahead and a correction can fall between the two reads of a per-word sample: at the sample before the block, the count field of `MICCMECT` reads 0 and `MCEIP` is clear, and at the quiescent sample after the block, the readback of `MICCMECT` is at or above 2^t and `MCEIP` is set. The per-word samples of the ICCM block are logged and not graded. The count field of the other counter reads 0 in every sample. `mcause` is 0x8000_001E once for each arming that finds `MCEIP` set, with the handler clearing `mie[30]` before `mret`, and never for an arming at a readback below 2^t. A DUT that sets `MCEIP` below the threshold, does not set it at or above it, takes no trap at the arming, takes the trap twice or takes another cause fails. Anchor: the firmware CSR reads on the outbound mailbox and the `mcause` that the handler logs (approved), and the trace interrupt event with `ecause` 0x1E (approved), with the flips from the TCM bit-flip hook. Control: in the DCCM leg the armed sample at the count 0 shows `MCEIP` low and no trap; in the DCCM miss case the samples show `MCEIP` low and no trap with the flipped words present, and the later crossing shows the trap, so a trap is attributable to the threshold. In the ICCM leg the trap comes only at the arming after the quiescent sample. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-CE PASS: mem=<ICCM or DCCM> order=<miss_first or reach_first> t=<t> counter=<n> mceip=<0 or 1> mcause=<0x8000001e or none>`, for the DCCM leg one line for the last sample below 2^t and one for the first sample at or above 2^t, for the ICCM leg one line for the sample before the block and one for the quiescent sample after it, and one line for each arming.
|CHK-TCM-SILENT
|With `mie[30]` clear, errors count with no trap: over the accesses of the silent part of the leg, the trap count of the handler stays 0 and the trace shows no interrupt event, also at a readback at or above 2^t (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`: until `mie[30]` is set, corrected errors are counted silently). A DUT that delivers `MCEIP` with `mie[30]` clear traps and fails. Anchor: the handler trap count and the trace port (approved), with the flips from the TCM bit-flip hook. Control: the arming of the same leg traps for the same counter readback, so the missing trap is attributable to `mie[30]`. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-SILENT PASS: mie30=0 mem=<ICCM or DCCM> accesses=<n> counter=<n> traps=0 mceip=<v> control_trap_at_arming=1`.
|CHK-TCM-CLAMP
|A write of the exponent 31 reads back 26 in bits [31:27] of `MICCMECT` and of `MDCCMECT` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`: a written threshold above 26 is clamped to 26). A DUT that keeps 31 or truncates to another value fails. This checker needs no flip. Anchor: the real CSR read-back through the firmware (approved). Control: the write of t (0 to 5) reads back t, so the field is live and a read of 26 is not a stuck value. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-CLAMP PASS: reg=<MICCMECT or MDCCMECT> wrote=31 readback=26 control_t=<t> control_readback=<t>`.
|CHK-TCM-UE
|Each uncorrectable access traps with the stated `mcause` (5 for the DCCM load, 7 for the DCCM store) and `mscause` = 1, with a pulse on the double-error bit (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, the table of uncorrectable-error traps, and `mscause` = 1 for uncorrectable ECC). The trace shows one exception event with `ecause` 5 or 7 for the access. A DUT that corrects the double-bit error, takes another cause or sets another `mscause` fails. Anchor: the `mcause` and `mscause` that the handler logs and the trace port (approved), with the flips from the TCM bit-flip hook. Control: the unflipped neighbour access of the same type returns the expected data (load) or lands the byte (store) with no trap. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-UE PASS: access=<DCCM_LOAD or DCCM_STORE> mcause=0x<c> mscause=0x1 pulse=0b<....> clean_neighbour=ok`.
|CHK-TCM-NMI
|The core takes the NMI at the `SEP_NMI_VEC` address with `mcause` 0xF000_1000 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, the table of uncorrectable-error traps: the DCCM access on the fast-interrupt path is an NMI taken through `SEP_NMI_VEC` like the watchdog bark). The first retired PC after the event equals the programmed vector, and the NMI handler logs `mcause` 0xF000_1000. A DUT that takes a load access fault, ignores the error or enters another address fails. Anchor: the `mcause` that the handler logs and the retired PC on the trace port (approved), with the flip from the TCM bit-flip hook. Control: the pending source 2 stays undelivered until the firmware sets `mie.MEIE` and `mstatus.MIE`, so the NMI is attributable to the delivery of that source. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-NMI PASS: mcause=0xf0001000 vec=0x<..> retired_pc=0x<..>`.
|CHK-TCM-DBG
|For each injected CPU-path DCCM access, a pulse appears on the right bit within the begin and end markers of the access (DCCM single 365, DCCM double 364), for the ICCM straight-line run at least one pulse appears on bit 367 within the leg markers, and for the vector-table read of step 12 at least one pulse appears on bit 364 within the leg markers. Each pulse is one `clk_i` cycle wide. In the same window no pulse appears on another of the bits 367, 365 and 364 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Debug bus mirror: single-cycle pulses, ICCM single 367, ICCM double 366, DCCM single 365, DCCM double 364). The pulses on bit 366 are logged as `OBS-TCM-DBG366` and are not graded, because no leg drives an uncorrectable ICCM error, so bit 366 has no live control in the leaf. The number of pulses for one word is not stated and is logged. A DUT that mirrors the wrong bit, a wide pulse or no pulse fails. Anchor: a counter on `ext_debug_bus_o[367:364]` (approved), with the flips from the TCM bit-flip hook. Control: the counter sees at least one injection of each driven kind, so a counter that never moves fails, and each of bits 367, 365 and 364 pulses in its own window, so a pulse on it in another window can show. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-DBG PASS: bit365=<a> bit367=<b> bit364=<d> width_clk=1 words_with_pulse=<w>/<w> pulses_per_word=<min>-<max>`.
|CHK-TCM-NO-PIC
|The `meip` words that the firmware reads after the correctable leg (step 9), after the DCCM load trap (step 10) and after the DCCM store trap (step 11) equal their baseline, and `MICECT` reads 0x0 in each of the three reads (`hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, Memory ECC Errors: neither memory reaches the PIC, and `MICECT` never increments). In the correctable leg, `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`, the vector that feeds the PIC, is unchanged in the 4 `clk_i` cycles around each pulse on bit 365 or 367 (the window is an environment choice). A DUT that routes a memory error to a PIC source changes a `meip` word or a vector bit and fails. Anchor: the firmware reads of the `meip` words and of `MICECT`, and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved), with the flips from the TCM bit-flip hook. Controls: the pending PIC source 2 makes a `meip` word non-zero, so an unchanged word is a real compare; the vector bit of source 2 (bit 1) rises when step 5 makes the source pending, so the vector sample is live; the `MDCCMECT` reads of the correctable leg return a non-zero count through the same firmware CSR read path, so a read path stuck at 0 cannot pass. The NMI leg is not covered, because that leg delivers the pending source 2 and the firmware ends in the NMI handler. Evidence: `CHK-TCM-NO-PIC PASS: after=<correctable or dccm_load or dccm_store> meip0=0x<..> meip1=0x<..> same=1 micect=0`, one line for each of the three reads, and `CHK-TCM-NO-PIC PASS: vector_unchanged_pulses=<k>/<k> window_clk=4 control_src2_bit_rise=1`.
|===

`OBS-TCM-INCR: access=<i> counter_before=<a> counter_after=<b>` logs the corrections that one access adds. The line is not scored.

`OBS-TCM-DBG366: pulses=<n>` logs the pulses on `ext_debug_bus_o[366]` in the leaf. The line is not scored.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. Firmware CSR reads are logged on the mailbox. The CPU trace trap event is a second observation point.

*Scope limit*: This test does not claim the uncorrectable ICCM error, the fetch trap and the NMI row alike (the register text and the interrupt chapter differ, see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so no ICCM double-bit flip is driven. It does not claim the response to a load from a reserved CPU-region address (not stated), the DMA-path half of the contract (graded in `sep_tcm_dma_aperture_ecc_test`), a count write behaviour or a count decrement. The firmware run from the ICCM stays with `sep_hello_world_test`, and the NMI entry through `SEP_NMI_VEC` after the bark stays with `sep_nmi_sanity_test`.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test]]
===== 5.27 `sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu` (real core and firmware), Verilator and VCS, `reseed = 4`

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_demux_unmapped_fetch_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU firmware with the console and a mailbox marker, the `tcm_load_i` image load of ICCM and DCCM, a test-owned ROM image of the boot stub and the ROM trap handler that the bench writes into the ROM macro through `tb_backdoor_mem` (`+sep_boot_rom_hex`) before reset release, the secure DMA registers, the CPU trace port and the LSU register path (all approved). The existing outbound responder `tb/sep_outbound_mbx.sv`, which answers OKAY for any address. The IFU request and response taps `ifu_req` and `ifu_rsp`, the port taps `rom_req`, `sram_req`, `ext_req` and `out_req`, the LSU request taps `lsu_rom_req` and `lsu_xbar_req`, and the TCM taps `xbar_tcm_req` and the ICCM and DCCM write enables of `tcm_req` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, passed to the firmware in its parameter block: three unmapped fetch targets U from {0x1005_0000, 0x1090_0000, 0x10A0_0000, 0x10C0_0000, 0x1100_0000, 0x2000_0100, 0x4000_0000, 0x0000_1000}, the ICCM word offset of the marker code and the marker value, the DCCM offsets, the pattern and the store value V (different from W). U excludes 0xC000_0000 to 0xCFFF_FFFF (core wiring), the boot ROM, the SRAM, the local alias 0xD000_0000 to 0xFFFF_FFFF (the alias rewrites the address, so the fetch never shows U) and the console address 0x8000_0000, because the begin marker, the end marker and the handler log are LSU stores to 0x8000_0000 on the outbound port inside the fetch window. No U equals an address that the firmware accesses inside the fetch window. `reseed = 4` samples four sets of draws.

*Objective*: The instruction-fetch demux sends boot ROM addresses to the ROM mux, SEP SRAM addresses to the local crossbar and every other address to a DECERR responder. The IFU never reaches the extension port or the outbound port. The load/store demux sends ROM addresses to the ROM mux and every other address to the local crossbar. The load/store unit cannot access ICCM. The ICCM, DCCM and PIC are tightly coupled to the core: the IFU and LSU reach them through the core wiring and not through the crossbar. The crossbar `cpu_tcm` target is the DMA path into ICCM and DCCM. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (CPU, lines 43-57: the demuxes and the DMA slave port); `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` (Top Level AXI4, Fabric Topology); `hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc` (ROM Architecture, lines 45-46: the CPU load/store unit cannot access ICCM); `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` (local alias, lines 42-45).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`, `sep_rom_sanity_test` and `sep_hello_world_test`, which fetch from the ROM and the ICCM. `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test` loads from the ROM. `sep_address_map_test` and `sep_axi_id_routing_test` reach registers from the CPU-LSU master. This test grades only the new cells: the IFU target set, the DECERR answer for an unmapped fetch, the LSU target set, and that the ICCM and DCCM are reached through the core wiring only (CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR). The ROM and SRAM fetches and loads, and the ICCM execution and DCCM readback (`CTL-CORE-PATH`), are controls here. The open owners keep the rest. Cells: ROM and SRAM fetches (controls) and fetches at three unmapped targets; ROM and SRAM loads, an LSU read at 0x2000_0100 (extension port) and an LSU store at 0x8000_0000 through one programmed outbound filter entry (outbound port), as the controls for the never-reach check; ICCM marker code and DCCM patterns through the core wiring, and a DMA copy to DCCM as the control for the crossbar tap; an LSU load and store at an ICCM word that holds a known word W, and the same pair at a DCCM word; a DMA copy of one SRAM word to an ICCM word as the control for the ICCM write enable. Functional coverage: `sep_mem_aperture_cg` (`cp_demux_target` (the IFU error-responder cell and the LSU cells), `cp_core_internal_path`), `sep_fabric_dedicated_port_cg` (`cp_tcm_refused_initiator`, `cp_ext_refused_ifu`) and the Phase 1 group `sep_cpu_boot_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Before reset release, the bench loads the firmware image, the ICCM marker code at the drawn word offset with the drawn marker value, and the word W at an ICCM data word outside the code, with valid ECC, through `tcm_load_i`. In the same phase, the bench writes the ROM image into the ROM macro through `tb_backdoor_mem`: a boot stub at the ROM base and the ROM trap handler. The reset vector keeps its default, the Boot ROM base 0x1004_0000 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Reset Vector), so the first fetch after the release is the boot stub. The boot stub sets `mtvec` to the ROM trap handler and jumps to the firmware entry in the ICCM. The firmware keeps the recovery address in `mscratch`. The ROM trap handler logs `mcause` and the trap value to the console and resumes at the address in `mscratch`.
. Release the reset. The firmware prints `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for each draw group.
. Through the CPU-LSU path, the firmware programs one outbound filter entry that admits the store of step 6 and reads the entry back. It programs the secure DMA enabled memory range with `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_BASE` = 0x0 and `ENABLED_MEMORY_RANGE_LIMIT` = 0xFFFF_FFFF, which covers the SRAM, ICCM and DCCM words of the DMA copies of steps 9 and 10, writes `RANGE_VALID` = 1 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/secure_dma/regs/secure_dma.rdl`: a transfer with no configured range ends in `ERROR_CODE.RANGE_VALID_ERROR`), reads the three registers back and stops with a fail marker if a value differs from the written one.
. Controls for the fetch. The CPU fetches code in ROM (the boot fetch and the ROM trap handler of step 7) and the firmware copies a short routine to SRAM with LSU stores, calls it and returns. The bench samples `ifu_req`, `ifu_rsp`, `rom_req` and `sram_req` for the ROM and SRAM fetches.
. Controls for the loads. The firmware loads a drawn ROM word and a drawn SRAM word, one at a time, and logs each marker before and after its load. The bench samples `lsu_rom_req` and `lsu_xbar_req`.
. Controls for the never-reach check. The firmware reads 0x2000_0100 through the LSU (extension port) and stores a word to 0x8000_0000 through the LSU (outbound port, the entry of step 3). The bench samples `ext_req` and `out_req`.
. Error injection. Point: the CPU IFU. Kind: a fetch at an unmapped address U, for each of the three drawn targets in order. Expected response: DECERR on the fetch response with no request on any other port (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, CPU). The firmware logs a begin marker, jumps to U, and the ROM trap handler logs `mcause` and the trap value, resumes at the recovery address and logs an end marker. The core reaction is not scored. The bench samples `ifu_req`, `ifu_rsp`, `rom_req`, `sram_req`, `ext_req` and `out_req` from the begin marker to the end marker. The marker stores and the handler log in this window go to 0x8000_0000 and are not requests for U.
. Core-internal path. The firmware calls the ICCM marker code, which logs the drawn marker value. The firmware then writes the seeded pattern at the drawn DCCM offsets and reads it back, and logs each value. The bench samples `xbar_tcm_req` during the marker run and the DCCM accesses.
. Control for the crossbar tap. The firmware programs a DMA copy of one word from SRAM to a DCCM word with the secure DMA registers, and waits for completion (a bounded wait). The bench samples `xbar_tcm_req`.
. Error injection. Point: the CPU LSU. Kind: a load and a store to the ICCM word that holds W. Expected response: no ICCM access (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, ROM Architecture); the response to the ICCM data access is not stated and is not scored. The firmware loads from the ICCM word and logs the value (or the trap, which the handler logs and resumes after), stores V to the ICCM word (the same handling). The bench samples `xbar_tcm_req` from the begin marker of the LSU load to the end marker of the LSU store, and closes this window before the GO write of any DMA copy of this step. The bench samples the ICCM write enable of `tcm_req` during the store. The firmware then programs a DMA copy of the ICCM word to an SRAM word with the secure DMA registers and waits for completion (a bounded wait). It reads the SRAM word and logs it. Control for the ICCM write enable: the firmware programs a DMA copy of one SRAM word to an ICCM data word that is not the word of W and not code, and waits for completion (a bounded wait). The bench samples the ICCM write enable of `tcm_req` during this copy.
. Control for the ICCM pair. The firmware loads a known DCCM word, stores a different value to the same DCCM word, and reads it back, and logs each value. The bench samples the DCCM write enable of `tcm_req` during the store.
. The firmware writes the end-of-firmware word. The bench evaluates every checker from the console and mailbox log, the trace port and the tap samples.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-IFU-DECERR
|The `ifu_rsp` response to each fetch at U is DECERR, and no request appears on `rom_req` or `sram_req` between the `ifu_req` for U and its `ifu_rsp` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, lines 51-54: every address other than ROM and SRAM goes to a DECERR responder). The firmware issues no ROM or SRAM data access in that window. A DUT that forwards U to the ROM mux or the crossbar, or that answers OKAY, fails. Anchor: the taps `ifu_req`, `ifu_rsp`, `rom_req` and `sram_req`. Control: the ROM and SRAM fetches of step 4 appear on those taps. Evidence: `CHK-IFU-DECERR PASS: fetch=0x<U> resp=DECERR rom_req=0 sram_req=0 control_rom=<n> control_sram=<m>`.
|CHK-IFU-NEVER
|No request for U appears on `ext_req` or `out_req` in the fetch window of step 7 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, lines 51-54: everything else goes to the DECERR responder; `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, Fabric Topology). The check compares the address of each `ext_req` and `out_req` request with U. The marker stores and the handler log in the fetch window go to the console address 0x8000_0000, which is never a U, so they are not U requests. The control accesses run outside the fetch window, so a target U that equals a control address is not confused with the control. A DUT that routes an IFU fetch to the extension port or the outbound port shows the request. Anchor: the taps `ext_req` and `out_req`. Control: the LSU read and the outbound store of step 6 appear on those taps in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-IFU-NEVER PASS: target=0x<U> ext_seen=0 out_seen=0 lsu_ext_seen=<n> lsu_out_seen=<m>`.
|CHK-LSU-ROUTE
|ROM loads appear on `lsu_rom_req` only, and SRAM loads on `lsu_xbar_req` only (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, lines 53-54: the LSU demux sends ROM addresses to the ROM mux and everything else to the local crossbar). A DUT that sends a ROM load through the crossbar or an SRAM load to the ROM mux fails. Anchor: the taps `lsu_rom_req` and `lsu_xbar_req`, in the marker windows of step 5. Control: each load appears on its own tap. Evidence: `CHK-LSU-ROUTE PASS: rom_addr_lsu_rom_req=<n> lsu_xbar_req_for_rom=0 sram_addr_lsu_xbar_req=<m> lsu_rom_req_for_sram=0`.
|CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR
|The core accesses of `CTL-CORE-PATH`, and the LSU load and store at the ICCM word in the window of step 10, raise no `xbar_tcm_req` activity (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, Fabric Topology: the CPU TCM target is the DMA backdoor and the CPU ICCM and DCCM are not reached through the crossbar). A DUT that routes a core access through the crossbar shows activity. Anchor: the tap `xbar_tcm_req`. Control: the DMA copy to DCCM of step 9 shows `xbar_tcm_req` activity, so a silent tap can fail. Evidence: `CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR PASS: core_windows=<n> lsu_iccm_windows=2 xbar_tcm_seen=0 control_dma_xbar_tcm_seen=<m>`.
|CHK-LSU-ICCM
|The value that the LSU load at the ICCM word returns is never W (a trap, which returns no value, also satisfies the check), and the ICCM word still reads W after the LSU store, read by a DMA copy to the SEP SRAM (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, lines 45-46: the CPU load/store unit cannot access ICCM). V differs from W, so a store that lands shows V. A DUT that lets the LSU read or write the ICCM fails. The response to the ICCM data access is not stated and is not scored. Anchor: the real values that the firmware logs and the real DMA readback of the ICCM word (approved). Control: the DCCM pair returns and writes data, so the load, the store and the readback paths are live. If the core reads or writes ICCM from the load/store unit, this check fails, and that result is a specification-versus-build finding and not an expected-fail entry. Evidence: `CHK-LSU-ICCM PASS: store=0x<V> macro=0x<W> load_ne_W=1 control_dccm_rw=1`.
|CHK-LSU-ICCM-WE
|No ICCM write enable appears on `tcm_req` for the LSU store of step 10 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`, lines 45-46). A DUT that writes the ICCM macro shows the enable. Anchor: the ICCM write-enable tap of `tcm_req`. Control: the DMA copy of an SRAM word to the ICCM in step 10 shows the ICCM write enable on the same tap (`hw/sys/sep/doc/rom.adoc`: the secure DMA copies an image to the ICCM). A control copy with no ICCM write enable fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-LSU-ICCM-WE PASS: iccm_we=0 control_dma_iccm_we=<n>`.
|===

`CTL-CORE-PATH` is a control and not a checker. The ICCM marker code executes and the logged marker equals the drawn marker value, and the DCCM patterns read back exact. `sep_hello_world_test` grades ICCM execution and DCCM data, and a DUT that routes core accesses through the crossbar passes this readback too, so it is the live control of CHK-CORE-PATH-XBAR: the core accesses of that check happen. The leaf fails as a failed precondition when the marker or a pattern differs. The line is `CTL-CORE-PATH: mem=<iccm_fetch\|dccm> addr=0x<..> value=0x<..> exp=0x<..>`.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The PIC hole and past-extent cells are graded in the mailbox, IRQ and PIC area. The ROM and instruction fetch smoke cells, the ROM load cells and the register-reach cells stay with the open owners named in Test Coverage. This test does not claim the core reaction to an unmapped fetch, the response to the ICCM data access from the load/store unit, or the response to a load from a reserved CPU-region address.

'''''
[[sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test]]
===== 5.28 `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: VCS only (`tools = ["vcs"]`, real core debug module). Two testlist entries of the one source module, four seeds each. Entry C: `cpu` (real core with a ROM spin loop), testlist `cpu.toml`, groups `all` and `cpu`; it runs steps 1 to 4 and 9 to 12. Entry S: `no_cpu` on the full-CPU VCS build (`--target default`: the core held by `mpc_reset_run_req` = 0, the LSU force splice, no firmware), group `all`; it runs steps 1 to 8, with step 1 without the ROM image and without the retirement wait. In `cpu` mode the core owns the LSU and the bench `s_axi` inputs reach nothing (`tb/tb_top.sv`, run modes), so the independent read of the register targets runs in entry S, where the bench VIP owns the LSU request. The debug module reaches the system bus without the hart (RISC-V Debug Specification, System Bus Access), so the system-bus accesses run with the core held.

*Source Module*: `sep_debug_master_target_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: A JTAG driver with a DTM shifter that drives the core TAP and reads the DMI data from `jtag_tdo_o`. In entry C the ROM spin loop keeps the core running. The test uses these probes and injects:

* Added hook (top-port drive), which the test adds to the HDL Top port table of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`: `dmi_core_enable_i` and `dmi_uncore_enable_i` as bench inputs (CHK-DMI-GATE, CHK-DMI-UNCORE).
* Added hook (top-port drive), which the test adds to the HDL Top port table of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`: bench-owned `jtag_tck_i`, `jtag_tms_i`, `jtag_tdi_i` and `jtag_trst_ni` as TB inputs and the DTM shifter (all checkers).
* Observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list: `jtag_tdo_o` (all checkers) and the `dbg_sb_req` and `dbg_sb_rsp` tap (CHK-DBG-SBA, the ID and beat part).
* Observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list: `dmi_uncore_en_o` and `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o` (CHK-DMI-UNCORE).
* Approved: `sep_cpu_reset_n_o`, `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, `wdt_rst_ni_i` (bench-driven), `dbg_rstb_i`, the CPU trace port (entry C), the LSU register path of entry S (`ocah_axi_vip` on the LSU force splice) and the SRAM word probes (CHK-DBG-RST as the control and anchor, CHK-DBG-SBA for the independent read).

The bench holds `dmi_uncore_rdata_i` at a constant 0 (no uncore model).

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the seven targets, for each of the two SRAM targets the order of its 32-bit and 64-bit accesses, and the write value of each access (full width of the access size, redrawn when it equals the value read before the write). `reseed = 4` samples different orders and values. The set of accesses (nine: the two SRAM targets at two sizes each, the five other targets at 32-bit size) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: Through the core JTAG TAP and the DMI core aperture (addresses 0x00 to 0x4F) the debug-module AXI master reaches the SEP fabric. With `dmi_core_enable_i` low the core accesses are ignored. With `dmi_uncore_enable_i` low the `dmi_uncore_*` outputs stay inactive, and with it high a DMI access at 0x50 and above raises them. `dbg_rstb_i` resets the EL2 debug module and does not reset the CPU or the SEP. The debug master is AXI4 with 64-bit data and a 1-bit ID that is zero-extended to 3 bits, and it passes the local alias remap into the SEP fabric. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (chapter introduction: the debug master data width, ID and alias remap; Processor Core and Debug: the JTAG debug interface and the AXI4 debug master); `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` (local alias); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`dmi_core_enable_i`, `dmi_uncore_enable_i`, `dmi_uncore_en_o`, `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o`, `dbg_rstb_i`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (SEP Reset Controller).

*Test Coverage*: `tb_top.sv` ties `dmi_core_enable` and `dmi_uncore_enable` to 0 and keeps the TAP pins in internal registers, so this test grades every cell of the DMI path, the TAP pins and the debug master. `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test` pulses `dbg_rstb_i` without a debug-module readback, and that pulse is a control of CHK-DBG-RST.

* The core-enable gate: a write of `dmactive` = 1 with `dmi_core_enable_i` low, then the enable raised and `dmactive` read; then a write of 1 and a read with the enable high as the control.
* Seven system-bus targets: an SRAM word and its local alias 0xD000_xxxx (32-bit and 64-bit size), `WDOG_BARK_THOLD`, HMAC `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER` (hash idle), `SEP_SW_DEBUG`, the cold scratch bank and secure DMA `INTR_SRC_ADDR_0[0]` (DMA idle), each 32-bit size only, so no access spans a neighbouring register or a reserved field. Each value is read back through the debug master and by the SRAM word probe or the bench LSU master of entry S.
* The master shape on the `dbg_sb_req` tap.
* DMI accesses at 0x50 and above with `dmi_uncore_enable_i` low (with the core enable high and low), and a control window with it high.
* A `dbg_rstb_i` pulse with `dmactive` set, and a `wdt_rst_ni` pulse as the control for the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap.

Error case: DMI accesses with `dmi_core_enable_i` low (ignored).

Functional coverage: `sep_debug_trace_cg` (`cp_dmi_aperture`), `sep_documented_absence_cg` (`cp_dmi_uncore`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Cold reset with the ROM spin loop image, `dmi_core_enable_i` = 0, `dmi_uncore_enable_i` = 0. Hold `dbg_rstb_i` low through the cold reset and release it together with `rst_ni`, so the EL2 debug module takes a real reset. Pulse `jtag_trst_ni` low and then high, leave the TAP in Run-Test/Idle and select the DMI register through the DTM shifter. A bounded wait for the first retirement on the CPU trace port; a timeout fails the leaf. `dmi_uncore_enable_i` stays 0 until step 10.
. Error injection, point `dmi_core_enable_i`, kind DMI accesses with the enable low: write `dmcontrol` (DMI address 0x10 of the RISC-V Debug Specification) with `dmactive` = 1 through the DTM. Expected response: the access is ignored. Then read `dmcontrol` with the enable still low and log the read data as an observation only (the specification does not state it).
. Raise `dmi_core_enable_i`. Read `dmcontrol`: `dmactive` reads 0.
. Control for the gate: with the enable high write `dmcontrol` with `dmactive` = 1 and read it back: `dmactive` reads 1. The debug module stays active for the next steps.
