. Arm a passive monitor on the `dbg_sb_req` tap. During step 7 it records, for each system-bus access, the address, the ID bits [2:1] and the number of data beats.
. Draw the order of the nine accesses and the drawn write values. The SRAM word is at a fixed 8-byte aligned SRAM address (no firmware runs in entry S), and its alias address is the same address at the 0xD000_0000 alias base.
. Run the access sequence for each of the nine accesses in the drawn order. The next access starts only after the sequence of the previous access ends, so an earlier write to the SRAM word never changes the value before the write of a later access:
.. Read the location by the independent path (SRAM word probe for the two SRAM targets, the bench LSU master on the LSU force splice for the other five) at the width of the access. This is the value before the write. Redraw the write value while it equals the value before the write.
.. Program `sbcs` for the access size with read-on-address disabled, and program `sbaddress0` for the target (the RISC-V Debug Specification system-bus access). A 32-bit access at the SRAM word covers its low 32 bits.
.. For a 64-bit access, write `sbdata1` with the upper 32 bits of the drawn value. Write `sbdata0` with the lower 32 bits of the drawn value; this write starts the access. A bounded wait for `sbbusy` low; a timeout fails the check.
.. Program `sbcs` with read-on-address enabled and write `sbaddress0` again with the target address. A bounded wait for `sbbusy` low; a timeout fails the check. Read `sbdata0`, and for a 64-bit access also `sbdata1`. The value of the two halves is the debug-master readback.
.. Read the same location again by the independent path at the width of the access. For the alias target this is the SRAM word at the direct address. This value is the CPU-path value.
. Stop the monitor. Compare the recorded values: each access shows one write record and one read record, the ID bits [2:1] are 0 in every record, and each 64-bit access has one data beat in each record.
. Uncore never window: with `dmi_uncore_enable_i` = 0 and `dmi_core_enable_i` = 1, issue one DMI read and one DMI write at address 0x50 and at address 0x7F (the lowest and the highest 7-bit address at or above 0x50). Wait for each access to complete (bounded). Then lower `dmi_core_enable_i` to 0, repeat the same four accesses, and raise `dmi_core_enable_i` to 1 again. The window covers every DMI access issued since step 1 with `dmi_uncore_enable_i` = 0, so the accesses below 0x50 of steps 2 to 4 are in it.
. Uncore control window: with `dmi_core_enable_i` = 1, raise `dmi_uncore_enable_i` to 1, issue one DMI read and one DMI write at address 0x50, wait for both to complete (bounded) and lower `dmi_uncore_enable_i` to 0.
. Debug reset: read `dmcontrol` (`dmactive` reads 1 as the control) and note the trace retirement count. Pulse `dbg_rstb_i` low for 16 `clk_i` cycles (a test parameter) and release it. A bounded wait for a retirement on the trace port after the release; a timeout fails the check. Read `dmcontrol`: `dmactive` reads 0. The `sep_cpu_reset_n` and `sep_reset_n` taps are sampled from the start of the pulse to this readback.
. Control for the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap: pulse `wdt_rst_ni_i` low for 16 `clk_i` cycles (a test parameter) and release it. A bounded wait for the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap to fall and then rise; a timeout fails the check.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DMI-GATE
|A write of `dmactive` = 1 with the enable low does not land: after the enable rises, `dmactive` reads 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `dmi_core_enable_i`: it enables DMI accesses to the core debug module). The debug module holds `dmactive` = 0 from the cold reset, in which `dbg_rstb_i` is low (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `dbg_rstb_i` resets the EL2 debug module). A design that ignores the enable reads 1 and fails. Control: a write of 1 with the enable high reads back 1, so the readback can show a landed write and a debug module that never accepts a write fails the control. The anchor is the real DMI readback; its data comes from `jtag_tdo_o`. The read data of a read with the enable low is not stated; the check logs it as an observation only. Evidence: `CHK-DMI-GATE PASS: gated_wr=1 enabled_rd_after=0 control_wr=1 control_rd=1 gated_rd_obs=0x<..>`.
|CHK-DBG-SBA
|For each target and size the value read through the debug master equals the value written and equals the value read by the backdoor or the CPU path, each compared at the full width of the access (both 32-bit halves for a 64-bit access). A wrong alias rewrite, a wrong size or a lost write shows a mismatch between the three values. The independent value is a real readback of the SRAM word probe or of the bench LSU master of entry S, and the value before the write, read immediately before that access, differs from the drawn value, so a location that the write never reached fails. On the `dbg_sb_req` tap the ID bits [2:1] are 0 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, chapter introduction: the 1-bit debug master ID is zero-extended to 3 bits), and each 64-bit access is one 64-bit beat (the same paragraph: the debug master is AXI4 with 64-bit data). The monitor of step 5 records both and step 8 compares them. Evidence: `CHK-DBG-SBA PASS: target=<name> addr=0x<..> size=<n> wr=0x<..> rd=0x<..> cpu_path=0x<..> id_hi=0b00 beats=<n>`, one line for each of the nine accesses.
|CHK-DMI-UNCORE
|With `dmi_uncore_enable_i` = 0, `dmi_uncore_en_o` and `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o` are 0 on every cycle of the window, including the accesses at 0x50 and above (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `dmi_uncore_enable_i`). A design that raises the outputs without the enable fails. Control: in the control window `dmi_uncore_en_o` rises for a read and a write and `dmi_uncore_wr_en_o` for the write, so a design with the outputs stuck at 0 fails. The anchor is the DUT outputs on every cycle of the window; the control proves that the same probe can rise. Evidence: `CHK-DMI-UNCORE PASS: en_o_cycles=0 wr_en_o_cycles=0 accesses=<k> at_or_above_0x50=<u> control_en_o_rise=<a> control_wr_en_o_rise=<b>`.
|CHK-DBG-RST
|After `dbg_rstb_i` `dmactive` reads 0, a retirement follows on the CPU trace port, and the `sep_cpu_reset_n` and `sep_reset_n` taps stay high from the start of the pulse to the readback (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `dbg_rstb_i` resets the EL2 debug module; `sep_reset_no` and `sep_cpu_reset_no` have no `dbg_rstb_i` term). A `dbg_rstb_i` that does not reach the debug module leaves `dmactive` at 1, and one that resets the CPU or the SEP drops a tap or stops the retirements. Controls: `dmactive` reads 1 before the pulse, the `wdt_rst_ni_i` pulse in the same leaf drops the `sep_cpu_reset_n` tap, so the tap can show a CPU reset, and the cold reset at the start of the leaf holds the `sep_reset_n` tap low until its release, so that tap can show a SEP reset. The anchors are the DMI readback, the trace port and the two taps. Evidence: `CHK-DBG-RST PASS: dmactive 1->0 after dbg_rstb_i; pc_advance=<n> sep_cpu_reset_n_low=0 sep_reset_n_low=0 control_wdt_sep_cpu_reset_n_low=1`, where `pc_advance` is the number of retirements after the release.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every checker of the steps that an entry runs PASSes: CHK-DMI-GATE, CHK-DMI-UNCORE and CHK-DBG-RST in entry C, CHK-DMI-GATE and CHK-DBG-SBA in entry S. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the behaviour of `dmi_active_o` (the specification states only "DMI active indicator"; see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), a ROM target through the debug master (no specification statement), or the DTP STAP leg of the debug path (no observation point in a SEP-only bench). The DMI register addresses and the system-bus access protocol come from the RISC-V Debug Specification that the core implements.

'''''
[[sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test]]
===== 5.29 `sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu` (real core and firmware; constants from a DCCM table), Verilator and VCS, four seeds. Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu`; testlist `cpu.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_isa_pmp_csr_test`

*BFM / Model*: The CPU firmware (an image booted from the ICCM with a trap handler at `mtvec`, built for RV32I outside the table rows of step 3 and the `bset` word of step 4, so that no other code retires an M, C, Zba, Zbb or Zbc encoding), an instruction model in the bench that computes the table values, the DCCM table loaded by backdoor before reset release (operands, expected results, 16 `pmpaddr` values and `mrac` values), the firmware CSR and result reads on the outbound mailbox (approved), and the CPU trace port with its monitor (approved). No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the operands of the instruction table, the 16 distinct `pmpaddr` values (a draw is redrawn when it equals an earlier one), and the `mrac` values (four per leaf, each a full 32-bit pattern). The operand set also holds fixed corner pairs (0, 1, 0xFFFF_FFFF, 0x8000_0000). `reseed = 4` samples different operands, address values and `mrac` values. The instruction list, the block of step 9 and the PMP regions are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: The core runs RV32IMC. The bit-manipulation build options enable Zba, Zbb, Zbc and Zbe; Zbf, Zbp, Zbr and Zbs are disabled, so a Zbs instruction is illegal. The PMP has 16 entries (`pmpcfg`, `pmpaddr`) with Smepmp enabled. `mrac` sets attributes and grants no permission. The specification gives the trace port only as "CPU trace output for debug" (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `sep_cpu_trace_o`). The check compares the monitor retirement count with the architectural `minstret` (RISC-V Privileged Architecture). Reading the trace port as one record for each retired instruction is an accepted probe assumption (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>, Trace port content). Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` (Processor Core); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` (`sep_cpu_trace_o`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_rom_sanity_test`, `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` and `sep_cpu_trace_diag_test`. `sep_rom_sanity_test` retires seven functions across the immediate, upper-immediate, register and jump formats. `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test` retires four staged-image PCs. `sep_cpu_trace_diag_test` grades one breakpoint trap and the trace record against the firmware-read `mcause` and `mepc`. This entry adds only the cells that execute an M, C, Zba, Zbb or Zbc instruction, execute a Zbs instruction, write the PMP address registers and set `mrac`. The retirement of base-format instructions and the trap record are controls here.

* M, C, Zba, Zbb and Zbc instructions with seeded operands against table constants, and one Zbs instruction (`bset`).
* Distinct values in `pmpaddr0` to `pmpaddr15`, and a read of `mseccfg`.
* `mrac` values against a PMP-denied and a PMP-allowed user-mode load.
* A straight-line block between two `minstret` reads, with a dependent chain and a load-use pair.

Error cases: `bset` (illegal-instruction trap) and a user-mode load from a denied PMP region (load access fault).

Functional coverage: `cp_traced_event` (the trap cell), `cp_instr_class` (M, C, Zba, Zbb, Zbc and the Zbs trap), and the Phase 1 `sep_cpu_boot_cg`. The base-format retirement and the control of step 5 fill no Phase 3 bin.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The bench draws the operands, the `pmpaddr` values and the `mrac` values. The instruction model computes the expected result of each table row from the architectural definition of the instruction in the ratified RISC-V specification that the extension names, and never from a core read. The bench loads the table (operands and expected results) and the other drawn values into the DCCM by backdoor before reset release.
. Release the reset with the reset vector at the ICCM base. The firmware installs the trap handler at `mtvec`. The handler logs `mcause` and `mepc` on the mailbox, counts the trap and returns with `mret`.
. Legal instructions: for each table row the firmware executes one instruction on the operands of the row and logs the result on the mailbox with the row index. The rows hold M (`mul`, `mulh`, `mulhsu`, `mulhu`, `div`, `divu`, `rem`, `remu`, including division by zero and the signed overflow case, whose results the ratified specification defines), C (`c.add`, `c.addi`, `c.sub`, `c.slli`), Zba (`sh1add`, `sh2add`, `sh3add`), Zbb (`andn`, `orn`, `xnor`, `clz`, `ctz`, `cpop`, `min`, `minu`, `max`, `maxu`, `rol`, `ror`, `rev8`, `orc.b`, `sext.b`, `sext.h`) and Zbc (`clmul`, `clmulh`, `clmulr`). The instruction word of each row is explicit in the image, so the assembler cannot replace it.
. Error injection, point CPU, kind `bset` (Zbs instruction): the firmware executes `bset` as a data word that holds the ratified encoding. Expected response: an illegal-instruction trap with `mcause` 2. The handler advances `mepc` by 4 and returns. The firmware logs the trap count.
. Control for step 4: the firmware executes one legal instruction of each of M, C, Zba, Zbb and Zbc again after the trap and logs the results.
. PMP: the firmware writes 0 to `pmpcfg0` to `pmpcfg3`, writes the 16 drawn values to `pmpaddr0` to `pmpaddr15`, and reads all 16 back. Then, for each entry `i` in order, it writes the bitwise complement of the drawn value to `pmpaddr<i>` and reads all 16 back. It logs every readback list. The firmware reads `mseccfg` and logs the value.
. `mrac`: for each drawn `mrac` value, the firmware writes `mrac`, stores a seeded word into an allowed SRAM region and a different seeded word into a denied SRAM region, writes `pmpcfg0` to `pmpcfg3` and `pmpaddr0` to `pmpaddr2` so that entry 0 gives read and execute to the ICCM page of the user-mode stub, entry 1 gives read to the allowed region and entry 2 matches the denied region with no permission (two 4 KiB naturally aligned regions of the SRAM that the firmware does not otherwise use), and enters user mode with `mret` at the stub.
. Error injection, point CPU, kind user-mode load from a denied PMP region: the stub loads the allowed word into one register, loads from the denied region into a second register, and then executes `ecall` as its defined exit. Expected response: a load access fault with `mcause` 5 at the denied load, for every `mrac` value. The handler logs `mcause` and both registers, sets the return to machine mode and returns. A core that grants the denied load reaches the `ecall`, and the handler logs `mcause` 8 (environment call from user mode) in place of 5. The firmware logs the `mrac` value of the iteration.
. Trace: with no interrupt enabled, the firmware reads `minstret` (R1), runs a straight-line block of 64 instructions with no branch, and reads `minstret` again (R2). The block holds a dependent chain (an instruction that reads the destination of the previous one) and a load-use pair (a load followed by an instruction that reads its destination). The firmware logs both values. The trace monitor counts the instructions that retire from R1 up to, and not including, R2. A `minstret` read returns the count of instructions retired before the read instruction (RISC-V Privileged Architecture), so the window is the same.
. The firmware logs the completion marker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ISA
|Each legal instruction of M, C, Zba, Zbb and Zbc returns the table value, and `bset` traps with `mcause` 2 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Processor Core: Zbs is disabled, so the instruction is illegal; the cause 2 is the RISC-V illegal-instruction code). A wrong result of any instruction, a core that executes `bset` without a trap, or a trap with another cause fails. The table value of each instruction is computed from the architectural definition of the instruction in the ratified RISC-V specification that the extension names, and never from a core read. The bench compares the results that the firmware logs with the table; the firmware does not compare. Control: a legal instruction of each extension returns the table value after the trap of step 4. The anchors are the firmware results and the `mcause` that the handler read from the CSR. Evidence: `CHK-ISA PASS: ext=M ops=<n> ok; ext=C ops=<n> ok; ext=Zba/Zbb/Zbc ops=<n> ok; Zbs mcause=0x2`.
|CHK-PMP
|The 16 `pmpaddr` registers do not alias each other: a write to one entry changes that entry and leaves the other 15 unchanged. The 16 readbacks after the first write are logged and are not compared pairwise, because the implemented width and granularity are not claimed and two distinct draws can read back equal on a correct core. The complement write makes the entry differ in every implemented bit, so a write that did not land fails. `mseccfg` reads without a trap (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Processor Core: 16 entries with Smepmp enabled). Entries that share storage show a change in another entry at the complement write and fail. Exact full-width readback is not scored. The anchor is the firmware readback of each CSR. Evidence: `CHK-PMP PASS: entries=16 complement_changed=16 others_unchanged=16 aliases=0 readback=<list>; mseccfg read ok`.
|CHK-MRAC
|For each `mrac` value the denied load raises `mcause` 5 (the RISC-V load access fault code), because `mrac` does not grant permission (`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc`, Processor Core, Memory attributes row). A core where an `mrac` value grants the load runs on to the `ecall` of the stub, logs `mcause` 8 and shows the denied word in the second register, and fails. Control: the allowed PMP region load succeeds with the same `mrac` value and returns the stored word, so the fault comes from the PMP and not from the user mode set-up. The anchors are the `mcause` that the handler read and the logged values of the register of the allowed load and the register of the denied load; the bench compares the logged `mcause` with 5. Evidence: `CHK-MRAC PASS: mrac=0x<..> denied mcause=0x5 allowed=ok`, one line for each `mrac` value.
|CHK-TRACE-INSTRET
|The `minstret` delta over the block equals the count of retirements that the monitor counts on the trace port. The `minstret` definition is the RISC-V Privileged Architecture; the specification gives the trace port only as "CPU trace output for debug" (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`, `sep_cpu_trace_o`), and the reading of the port as one record for each retired instruction is an accepted probe assumption (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>, Trace port content). A counter that skips or double counts a compressed instruction, a load-use pair or a dependent instruction, or a trace port that drops a retirement, shows a different count. The anchors are the CSR delta and the monitor count of the DUT trace port. The delta is the difference of two firmware reads, which is at least the block length, and the check fails when the trace count is 0. Evidence: `CHK-TRACE-INSTRET PASS: delta_instret=<n> trace_retires=<n>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. Firmware logs on the mailbox and the CPU trace monitor are the observation points.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim Zbe execution and the Zbf, Zbp and Zbr traps (the specification gives no encodings), the implemented `pmpaddr` width and granularity, the access to `pmpaddr16` and above (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), the four-stage pipeline depth (no observation point), or the PMP permission cells beyond the one denied and one allowed region. Only the new cells are graded: the open owners (`sep_rom_sanity_test`, `sep_boot_rom_smoke_test`, `sep_cpu_trace_diag_test`) keep the retirement of base-format instructions, the staged-image PCs and the breakpoint trap record.

'''''
